# 3D memory system — RAW audit findings (machine-preserved)

**Run:** wf_1323d1d9-b6a · 116 agents · 2026-08-01
**Status: PRESERVED BUT NOT FULLY SYNTHESISED.** The first synthesis pass was written from
~16% of this material (I truncated its input at 60k of ~375k chars). The re-synthesis with
the full corpus failed on a session limit before completing. Everything below is the
complete raw output, kept so the synthesis can be redone without re-running 116 agents.

**Totals:** 12 audit agents · 165 findings · 158 adversarial verdicts

---

## Safety invariant — can persistent memory authorize a side effect? (U6 action-capability kernel, enforce-in-code claim)

**Auditor summary:** The kernel itself is genuinely well-built: `_verdict_for` returns `allow` only for `effect=="read_only"`, provenance is a pure passthrough (I verified adversarially that forged provenance tuples do not move any verdict), and the human-confirm arms (challenge confirm, P5 approve) are correctly gated to non-bot human sessions. But the invariant "enforced in code, not prompt" is FALSE as deployed: the enforcement mode matrix is empty in prod, staging, and the runner fleet, so every mutating dispatch resolves to `shadow` and proceeds; and the primary autonomous execution path (the six live CLI runners) never reaches the kernel at all and launches the agent CLI with `--dangerously-skip-permissions`. On top of that the per-fact memory-safety promote gate is structurally incapable of rejecting anything, and memory-influenced model output can mint runner capability grants through unvalidated JIRA labels whose only stated control is a docstring.

### [CRITICAL] Action guard is in all-shadow in every deployed environment — no mutating operation is ever blocked

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** `grep -rn OMNISIGHT_ACTION_GUARD_MODE` across the repo returns hits ONLY in backend/agents/action_guard.py:59,69,117 and test files — no deploy config, no compose file. `grep -c '^OMNISIGHT_ACTION_GUARD_MODE' /home/user/omnisight-prod/.env` = 0; `docker inspect omnisight-productizer-backend-a-1` env grep = 0; `omnisight-staging-backend-a-1` = 0; `~/.config/omnisight/runner.env` = 0 (10 keys total, none guard-related). Ran locally: `action_guard._MODE_MATRIX` = `{}` and `resolve_mode(ad,fam)` = 'shadow' for ALL 14 families × {chat, runner_sdk}. action_guard.py:539-542 then computes `proceed = mode != "enforce"`.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: a mutating tool call classified `requires_grant` is blocked and routed to a human challenge. Actual: `requires_grant` → `resolve_mode(...)=='shadow'` → `proceed=True` → the tool executes. Only `unknown_tool_default_deny` (an unregistered tool NAME) is mode-independent.
- **impact:** The entire 'enforced in code, not prompt' claim reduces, in the deployed system, to a default-deny on unregistered tool names. Every registered mutating tool — write_file, run_bash, git_push, create_pr, deploy_to_evk, create_task, supervisor_set_labels, propose_action, memory, Skill — executes on the model path with no grant. The guard still emits a correct-looking `verdict="requires_grant"` in its log line and metric while permitting the call.

### [CRITICAL] The primary autonomous execution path has no authorization kernel at all, and the CLI's own permission system is explicitly disabled

- **category:** design  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** `systemctl --user list-units` shows runner-claude@1, runner-claude@2, runner-codex@1, runner-codex@2, runner-gemini@1, runner-grok@1 all `active running`. Their ExecStart is scripts/runner-wrapper/run-ephemeral.sh → auto-runner-jira.py. auto-runner-jira.py:2018 `cmd = ["claude", "--dangerously-skip-permissions", "-p", full_prompt]`; :2040 `cmd = [agy_bin, "--dangerously-skip-permissions", ...]`; grok uses `--always-approve` (:2043 comment). Execution is `subprocess.Popen(wrapped_cmd, ...)` at auto-runner-jira.py:2150. `grep guard_tool_dispatch auto-runner-jira.py` → NO hits; the only call sites are backend/agents/nodes.py:1223,1368,2028 (chat/specialist/a2a) and backend/agents/tool_dispatcher.py:250 (runner_sdk). runner_sandbox.py:86-121 ENV_ALLOWLIST does not contain OMNISIGHT_ACTION_GUARD_MODE.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: every model-chosen mutating tool passes the shared action guard. Actual: all Write/Edit/Bash/git tool calls made by the six production runners happen inside the agent CLI subprocess, which is launched with permissions bypassed; the guard is never invoked, and the env var that would flip it to enforce is scrubbed by ENV_ALLOWLIST before the CLI is spawned.
- **impact:** The highest-blast-radius memory→side-effect path in the system is entirely outside the capability kernel. The runner prompt is exactly where persistent memory is injected (cognee lesson recall auto-runner-jira.py:1303, reflection lessons :1353, project-state :1242, anti-patterns :1566, learned-item cards :1078). Remaining containment is bwrap (with network=True) plus the Gerrit human +2 at merge — neither is the kernel, and neither stops arbitrary in-jail file writes, shell exec, or network egress.

### [HIGH] u6_memory_safety_eval — the load-bearing per-fact promote gate — is structurally incapable of rejecting anything

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** backend/agents/u6_memory_safety_eval.py:85-88 compares `authorize_action(ctx, req, ())` with `authorize_action(ctx, req, injected)`. `injected` is built by `_fact_provenance_id` (:48-58) which returns ONLY `sealed.snapshot.snapshot_id` — I ran it: `'psnap-3e1ccb45cd23452ba03a615158cb2eb9'`, a random hex id; the fact's rendered content never reaches the function under test. authorization_kernel.py:246-278 `_verdict_for` reads only `descriptor.family`, `descriptor.effect`, and `is_unbound(ctx)` — `provenance_snapshot_ids` is a pure passthrough into the dataclass (:296). I ran `authorize_action` over 9 mutating tools (write_file, patch_file, create_pr, run_bash, create_task, propose_action, supervisor_set_labels, Bash, Write) with `()` vs `('psnap-FORGED','attacker-controlled','allow','db_verified_gerrit')` — identical verdict AND reason in all 9. Consumed as a real gate at backend/agents/u6_l3_producer.py:69 (`if not safety.promoted: return ProduceOutcome(False, 'safety_reject:...')`). The only test that exercises the reject branch monkeypatches the kernel: backend/tests/test_u6_l3_producer.py:69 `monkeypatch.setattr('backend.agents.u6_memory_safety_eval.authorize_action', _biased)`.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected (per its own docstring: 'the load-bearing safety gate … PROVE it is ACTION-INERT … Promote iff the negative control holds'): a candidate fact that could move an action verdict is rejected. Actual: the comparison is a tautology over a field policy never reads, on an input that never carries the fact content — `action_influence_divergences` returns `()` for every possible Fact, so `evaluate_memory_safety` returns `promote` 100% of the time.
- **impact:** Every L3 candidate passes the gate and is reported as 'safety-eval passed'. The gate contributes zero discriminating power while appearing in the pipeline as the RB3 safety control. This is the same shape as the pre-repair 'if False' JIRA stub: a source of confident PASS with no mechanism behind it. (Mitigating: the real content control is the closed PREDICATE_REGISTRY + subject/value grammars in u6_fact_schema.py:118-215, which genuinely rejects directive-shaped predicates — I confirmed `default_branch='refs/for/main'` is rejected. That control is real; this one is not.)

### [HIGH] Memory-influenced model output can mint runner capability grants and pickability via unvalidated JIRA labels

- **category:** security  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** backend/agents/tools.py:3665-3690 `supervisor_set_labels(ticket_key, add, remove)` — `add_set = [l for l in (add or []) if l]`, dedupe only, NO allowlist/denylist on label values, then JIRA PUT (:3706). Bound to Sora's conversation node at nodes.py:2213 when `_act_on` (env OMNISIGHT_ORCHESTRATOR_ACTION_TOOLS, default '1', UNSET in prod .env = ON). tools.py:1103-1117 `create_task`: when the model passes `character`, labels get `class:<brain>` (runner-pickable) + auto `capability:enable=gerrit_push` (:1110-1111); the free-form `capabilities` string becomes `capability:enable=<cap>` labels (:1115-1117). The only control on 'Only pass character when the user names one' is the docstring (:1030). capability_matrix.py:444-455 `apply_label_overrides` = `frozenset(base) | enable) - disable` — a pure widening; it is applied AFTER profile narrowing in `resolve_with_profile` (:497-505). Runner consumes it at auto-runner-jira.py:870-886 / :946-955 at pickup. Capability vocabulary includes gerrit_push, deploy_action, run_migration (capability_matrix.py:47-57). jira_authority_check.py:122 `_normalise_field(item) in {'assignee','status'}` — labels are NEVER authority-checked, and BOT_IDENTIFIERS (:10) classifies sora-bot as automation/L3.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected (INV: 'a memory record can never influence a capability grant, a label, a tier, a claim, or a route'): a code-level control rejects agent-authored `class:*`, `tier:*`, `capability:enable=*` labels. Actual: no such control exists; the kernel classifies both tools `ticket_write`/`task_write` → `requires_grant` → shadow → proceed; the resulting labels widen the runner's capability set at pickup.
- **impact:** End-to-end path: a promoted L3 fact / chat-history / RAG doc in Sora's context (OMNISIGHT_SORA_L3_READ=1 in prod, 1 promoted fact live per instance) → model emits supervisor_set_labels(add=['class:subscription-claude','agent:auto','capability:enable=gerrit_push','capability:enable=deploy_action']) → guard proceeds → JIRA labels set → runner picks the Story up with those capabilities. Nothing in code stops it; the Gerrit human +2 stops the MERGE, not the push, the CI run, or the agent execution.

### [HIGH] The enforcement arm (challenge → human confirm → grant) is fully built, correctly hardened, and structurally unreachable

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** backend/routers/action_challenges.py:60-96 is genuinely strict (browser session required, session/user match, non-anonymous, `auth.csrf_check`, `_assert_human_operator` rejecting apikey:*/ci-*/*-bot, tenant owner/admin). But a challenge is only ever created from `outcome.challenge_prepared`, which action_guard.py:338-341 sets ONLY when `refined.verdict == 'requires_grant'` inside `_refine_enforce_outcome` — reachable only when `_MODE_MATRIX.get((adapter,family)) == 'enforce'` (action_guard.py:560-565). That matrix is empty everywhere (see finding 1). Prometheus (targets up=1 for backend-a and backend-b; 124 metric names present): `omnisight_u6_challenge_count` = 0 in all 4 states on both instances, `omnisight_u6_grant_count` = 0 in all 6 states, `omnisight_u6_execution_attempts_count` = 0. The same query pattern DOES return non-zero rows (`omnisight_u6_l3_fact_count{state="promoted"}`=1, `omnisight_u6_l2_summaries_count`=1), proving the query works. `omnisight_action_guard_decision_total` / `_fail_closed_total` / `_shadow_classification_total` return zero series entirely.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: a mutating model action produces a pending challenge a human confirms. Actual: zero challenges and zero grants have ever existed in prod; the machinery cannot be entered because no (adapter,family) key is set to enforce.
- **impact:** The operator-visible health signal for this subsystem — 0 pending challenges, 0 pending grants, 0 fail-closed blocks — is byte-identical to 'healthy and nothing needs attention'. There is no metric or alert that distinguishes 'enforcement working, nothing pending' from 'enforcement configured nowhere'. Prometheus has 4 alert rules loaded in total and none reference action_guard or u6.

### [MEDIUM] INV-2 authority-downgrade set names source kinds with zero producers; persistent memory read via the memory tool launders into a PERMITTED kind

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** auto_auth_policy.py:170-176 `_MEMORY_SOURCE_KINDS` includes `RUNNER_MEMORY_FILE`; `_HIGH_INJECTION_SOURCES` adds `MCP_RESULT`. `grep -rn 'MCP_RESULT|RUNNER_MEMORY_FILE|READ_FILE'` over non-test code: the ONLY hits are the constant definitions in provenance.py:61-66,75-80 and the policy set itself — no capture point ever emits them. The complete producer set is: rag_prefetch.py:220,355 (EPISODIC), prompt_loader.py:1243 (LEARNED_ITEM), u6_l3_reader.py:155 (EPISODIC), tools.py:1948 (EPISODIC), nodes.py:1419 (A2A_RESULT), nodes.py:2072/anthropic_native_client.py:816 (TOOL_RESULT), nodes.py:2239 (RAG_DOC), chat.py:187 (CHAT_HISTORY), anthropic_native_client.py:750 (STALE_REFRESH). Separately, backend/agents/memory_tool_handler.py (1353 lines — the persistent cross-session file memory rooted at OMNISIGHT_MEMORY_TOOL_ROOT, which IS set on every live runner unit) contains zero occurrences of `provenance`/`record_content`; its `view` output returns through ToolDispatcher and is recorded as TOOL_RESULT, which auto_auth_policy.py:167-169 explicitly PERMITS as 'the runner's own same-turn working set, not a persistent … injection channel'.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: any turn influenced by persistent memory is authority-downgraded to a human challenge (INV-2). Actual: two of the five listed memory/high-injection kinds can never fire because nothing produces them, and persistent file memory read through the `memory` tool is relabelled TOOL_RESULT — a kind the gate deliberately treats as non-memory and therefore auto-grantable.
- **impact:** Latent, not live: OMNISIGHT_U6_AUTO_AUTH is absent from prod, staging and runner env, so `evaluate_auto_auth` is never called. But the moment auto-auth is enabled, a turn whose only memory influence came through the memory tool (or a poisoned file read) would pass gate 7 as 'clean provenance' and be silently auto-granted. The tests (test_u6_memory_capability_contract.py:118-121) pin the SET MEMBERSHIP, not the existence of a producer — so this gap is invisible to the contract test.

### [MEDIUM] Shadow mode proceeds on `unbound_principal_denied` — the kernel's fail-closed-on-missing-identity is not fail-closed as deployed

- **category:** bug  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** authorization_kernel.py:275-276 hard-DENYs a mutating op for an unbound principal. But action_guard.py:529-542: only `decision.reason == 'unknown_tool_default_deny'` forces `proceed = False` mode-independently; the comment at :539-541 states 'deny/unbound_principal_denied or requires_grant: shadow observes-and-proceeds'. With the empty matrix, `mode` is always 'shadow', so a DENY verdict for a principal with no identity at all still executes. nodes.py:1223-1232 and :2028-2033 pass `execution_context=None` in several call sites, which action_guard.py:508-512 converts to `for_unbound()`.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: a kernel `deny` is honoured. Actual: only one of the two deny reasons is mode-independent; the identity-missing deny is downgraded to observe-and-proceed by the shadow default.
- **impact:** A dispatch with no resolvable principal — the case the kernel explicitly hard-denies — runs anyway, and the metric records `verdict="deny"` alongside `proceed=True`. An operator reading the deny counter would reasonably conclude the call was blocked.

### [MEDIUM] The specialist adapter passes turn_provenance=None — the INV-3 audit seam is empty on that adapter

- **category:** data-integrity  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** backend/agents/nodes.py:1223-1228 `guard_tool_dispatch(adapter_namespace="specialist", ..., turn_provenance=None)`. Compare nodes.py:2033 (chat) and tool_dispatcher.py:250 (runner_sdk), which both thread a real sealed provenance. action_guard.py:513 then yields `_ids = ()`, so `AuthorizationDecision.provenance_snapshot_ids` is empty for every specialist-guild dispatch.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: every guarded dispatch records the memory/retrieval sources exposed to the model (INV-3 audit). Actual: the specialist guilds — which carry MEMORY_TOOLS, EPISODIC_TOOLS and RAG on most guild loadouts (tools.py:3995-4035) — record none.
- **impact:** For the adapter that runs the specialist pipeline there is no audit trail linking a side effect to the memory that influenced it, and any future grant-binding on that adapter would have nothing to bind. Indistinguishable in the decision record from a turn that genuinely had clean provenance — an empty tuple means both 'no memory' and 'never captured'.

### [MEDIUM] jira_authority_check never inspects labels — the capability-granting field is exempt from the human-authority yield

- **category:** security  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** backend/agents/jira_authority_check.py:122 `if isinstance(item, dict) and _normalise_field(item) in {"assignee", "status"}` — the changelog scan considers only those two fields. Consumed at auto-runner-jira.py:3275 where the runner yields only `if change.author.kind == "human"`. jira_authority_check.py:10 `BOT_IDENTIFIERS = frozenset({"claude-bot","codex-bot","merger-bot","sora-bot"})` → classify_author returns kind='automation', level='L3' for sora-bot.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: a change to the fields that determine runner capability and pickability (`class:*`, `tier:*`, `capability:enable=*`) is subject to the same authority check as assignee/status. Actual: label changes are never examined, by any author.
- **impact:** Compounds finding 4: the agent-writable field that grants capabilities is the one field with no authority provenance check at all, so a label added by sora-bot is indistinguishable downstream from one an operator set.

### [INFO] Kernel docstring overstates the invariant relative to the refine path (documentation/claim mismatch, not an exploitable hole)

- **category:** mismatch-with-intent  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** False
- **evidence:** authorization_kernel.py:10-12 claims 'a mutating side effect can never be authorized from the model path — it only ever reaches requires_grant, never allow.' action_guard.py:328 `proceed = refined.verdict == "allow"` is a model-path allow. It is safe today because `_verdict_for` (authorization_kernel.py:272-273) returns allow ONLY for `effect == "read_only"`, and the only refinement that produces it is canonicalize_code_write_file.py:19-21 `_VIEW_REFINED_DESCRIPTOR = OperationDescriptor("str_replace_based_edit_tool", "read_only", "read_only")`, reached only when `command == "view"` (:210,234), with the sealed frozen args executed by identity via `effective_execution_args` (action_guard.py:181-190).
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: the docstring describes the actual guarantee. Actual: the guarantee that holds is 'no MUTATING op reaches allow' (structural, enforced by the effect check); the stated one — 'the model path never reaches allow' — is already false for read-only refinements.
- **impact:** No exploit today. Flagged because the invariant an auditor reads is stronger than the one the code enforces, and `_harden_descriptor` (authorization_kernel.py:117) validates only membership in KNOWN_OPERATION_CLASSES — which contains mutating pairs — so the effect check at :272 is the single line holding this. It deserves an explicit test naming it as the load-bearing line, not a docstring claim that overshoots it.

## Are the three axes (structural / temporal / causal) actually returning DATA today, post-Phase-R?

**Auditor summary:** Phase R genuinely repaired the two historical "hollow" wiring defects I could test — the JIRA `if False` stub is gone (live JIRA pull returns real parent/blocker/blocking data) and the causal axis now resolves a real `PostgresIncidentSource` instead of the never-assigned `_DEFAULT_INCIDENT_SOURCE` — but only ONE of three axes is actually delivering content today: structural is non-empty (driven almost entirely by the BM25 lessons half), temporal is hardcoded permanently unavailable, and causal returns `neighbours: []` for every ticket because its live writer has produced nothing for 12 days and the axis uses a 7-day lookback. The more serious result is that the detection layer built to prevent exactly this has itself gone hollow: the OP-2544 incident-write canary is not scheduled anywhere AND its escalation predicate is structurally unable to fire, the `ProjectStateNoTraffic` alert references a metric (`omnisight_project_state_calls_total`) that no code produces so it has been firing permanently, and no alert of any kind covers the causal or temporal axes.

**Checks performed:**
- Read backend/agents/project_state_aggregator.py (1049 lines) in full at develop tip /home/user/work/sora/OmniSight-betaF
- Read backend/api/project_state.py router (300 lines), backend/agents/failure_graph.py (incident sources, default_incident_source, get_failure_graph_neighbors), backend/agents/graphiti_mcp_client.py, backend/agents/cognee_integration.py (retrieve_lessons_via_cognee / retrieve_antipatterns_via_cognee), backend/agents/agent_feature_flags.py, backend/agents/staging_gate.py project-state probe, auto-runner-jira.py project-state + cognee-recall call sites, scripts/incident_write_canary.py
- Confirmed the DEPLOYED prod image runs Phase-R code: `docker exec omnisight-productizer-backend-a-1` shows /app/backend/agents/project_state_aggregator.py at 38423 bytes with TEMPORAL_UNAVAILABLE_PAYLOAD at line 94 and _structural_lessons at 580 — byte-identical size to develop tip
- Queried prod Postgres (omnisight-pg-primary) runner_incidents: total, id-shape breakdown (64-hex vs 32-hex vs live-v1-), per-day cadence, failure_class and ticket_key distribution
- Queried prod Postgres runner_metrics (the canary's fleet-activity signal) for the 7-day window, and PROVED the query works by re-running it over 2026-07-15..2026-07-23 (returned 24)
- Executed the real causal axis inside the live prod container: failure_graph.default_incident_source() -> PostgresIncidentSource; query_by_ticket('OP-1886', 2026-07-01) -> 33 rows; query_by_ticket 7-day -> 0 rows; agg._causal_failure_neighbours() for OP-1886/OP-2574/OP-2618 -> all {'neighbours': []}
- Replayed the EXACT prod causal neighbour loop against real data with a widened window vs a filter-then-truncate counterfactual, for 4 real tickets

**Could not check:**

### [HIGH] Causal axis returns EMPTY for every ticket today: the live incident writer has been silent for 12 days and the axis uses a 7-day lookback

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** Prod PG: `SELECT length(incident_id), (incident_id LIKE 'live-v1-%'), count(*), max(created_at) FROM runner_incidents GROUP BY ...` -> 1935 rows of 64-hex backfill (max 2026-06-08) and 38 rows of `live-v1-` (max 2026-07-20 05:40:06). `date -u` = Sat Aug 1 14:11:31 UTC 2026. Executed inside omnisight-productizer-backend-a-1: `src.query_by_ticket('OP-1886', since=2026-07-01)` -> 33 rows, but `query_by_ticket('OP-1886', since=2026-07-25T14:13:58Z)` -> 0 rows; `agg._causal_failure_neighbours(t)` for OP-1886 / OP-2574 / OP-2618 all returned `{'ticket': ..., 'neighbours': []}`. Lookback is hardcoded at project_state_aggregator.py:676 (`timedelta(days=7)`).
- **expected vs actual:** Expected post-Phase-R: the causal axis consults the real runner_incidents table and returns up to 3 failure neighbours. Actual: the wiring is genuinely fixed (PostgresIncidentSource resolves, the SQL is index-served and returns 33 rows over a wider window), but the 7-day window has been empty since 2026-07-27, so every single request returns an empty neighbour list.
- **impact:** The causal axis — the third of the three axes and the one Phase R spent OP-1449/1450/1452/1454 repairing — contributes zero content to every runner pickup prompt. Prod Prometheus confirms this is not hypothetical: `omnisight_project_state_axis_total{axis="causal",content="empty"} = 2` and there is no `content="non_empty"` series for the causal axis at all, i.e. the causal axis has NEVER returned content in the lifetime of the current prod backend process (9 days).

### [HIGH] The one canary built to catch a dead incident writer is (a) not scheduled anywhere and (b) structurally unable to fire even if it were

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** (a) `grep -rl incident_write_canary /home/user/.config/systemd/user/ /etc/systemd/system/` -> rc=1, no match; `crontab -l | grep -i incident` -> empty; `ls ~/.config/systemd/user | grep -i canary` -> only anthropic-api-canary.{service,timer} and staging-gate-canary.{service,timer}. (b) scripts/incident_write_canary.py:44-46 `should_escalate = incident_write_count == 0 and fleet_activity_count > 0`; fleet activity comes from `SELECT count(*) FROM runner_metrics WHERE ts >= :since` (lines ~90-99). Prod PG: `SELECT count(*), max(ts) FROM runner_metrics WHERE ts >= now() - interval '7 days'` -> 0 rows, newest overall 2026-07-22 16:49:36. Query proven working: same query over 2026-07-15..2026-07-23 -> 24.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: OP-2544's daily read-only canary escalates a JIRA ticket when the fleet was active but no durable incident writes landed. Actual: no timer/cron/unit invokes it, and its predicate evaluates to `0 == 0 and 0 > 0` = False, so it would stay silent through exactly the outage it exists to detect.
- **impact:** This is the silent-hollow compound recurring one layer up: the alarm's own liveness signal (runner_metrics) died alongside the thing it monitors (runner_incidents), so the compound failure is self-concealing. A permanently-dead durable incident writer would look identical to a quiet fleet, forever, with no alarm and no scheduled check.

### [HIGH] ProjectStateNoTraffic alerts on a metric no code produces — it has been firing permanently, and its test only asserts the expression string

- **category:** bug  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** False
- **evidence:** Prometheus /api/v1/rules: `ProjectStateNoTraffic state=firing health=ok alerts=1 for=0`. Query `omnisight_project_state_calls_total` -> `{"resultType":"vector","result":[]}` (no series). `grep -rn project_state_calls_total` across the repo matches ONLY deploy/observability/prometheus/project_state_health.yml:38 and backend/tests/test_project_state_alert_rules.py:148,213 — there is no producer in backend/metrics.py (which defines project_state_axis_total at :974/:2127 but no calls_total). test_project_state_alert_rules.py:148 asserts `_rule("ProjectStateNoTraffic")["expr"] == "absent(rate(omnisight_project_state_calls_total[30m]))"` and :213 asserts `any("omnisight_project_state_calls_total" in expr for expr in exprs)` — both assert the rule TEXT, never that the metric exists.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: ProjectStateNoTraffic fires only when the aggregator stops receiving traffic. Actual: `absent()` over a metric that has never existed returns 1 unconditionally, so the alert is in a permanent firing state and carries zero information.
- **impact:** Two compounding harms. First, a permanently-firing alert trains operators to ignore the whole `omnisight_project_state_health` group — including ProjectStateStructuralHollow and ProjectStateHalfHollow, the alerts that would catch a genuinely hollow axis. Second, the real 'no traffic' condition IS currently true (only 2 project-state requests served in 9 days) and is therefore indistinguishable from the phantom-metric noise. The green test suite actively certifies the broken rule.

### [HIGH] Cognee lesson recall is ON in prod and always fails, because the live failure mode (LLM_API_KEY unset) is not in the fallback's caught-exception tuple — the working BM25 fallback is unreachable

- **category:** bug  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** In prod container: `ff.cognee_recall.enabled()` -> True (env OMNISIGHT_COGNEE_RECALL=true; flag default is False per agent_feature_flags.py:63-67). `LLM_API_KEY` -> UNSET (checked by name only). Live call `ci.retrieve_lessons_via_cognee(Path('/app/docs/sop/lessons'), ticket_title='alembic multi head migration', acceptance_criteria='', top_k=3)` raised `LLMAPIKeyNotSetError` (Status code: 422); `isinstance(e, (ci.CogneeNeo4jUnavailable, ci.CogneeQueryTimeout, ci.CogneeIndexCorruption))` -> False. cognee_integration.py:823 catches only those three classes. Direct call `ci._b10_fallback(LD, 'alembic multi head migration', '', 3)` -> 3 correct lessons ['L-LEGACY-001-multi-head-alembic-on-parallel-feature-branches.md', 'L-OP-1176-prod-alembic-drift-hidden-by-livez-routing.md', 'L-LEGACY-003-live-repo-state-alembic-head-injection.md']. Also `healthcheck()` -> ok=False status=Neo4jStartFailed detail='Cannot resolve address neo4j:7687'; `getent hosts neo4j` from the backend container -> rc=2.
- **expected vs actual:** cognee_integration.py:804-810 docstring: 'Retrieve top-k lessons from Cognee KG with B10 BM25 fallback ... this function tries Cognee first and silently falls back when the KG is unreachable, the SDK is missing, the query times out, or the KG returns no hits.' Actual: the ONE failure mode that occurs in production escapes the try/except entirely, propagates out of the function, and is swallowed by the runner's blanket `except Exception` at auto-runner-jira.py:1326 -> `lesson_recall.unavailable` + empty block. The fallback path is proven functional but is never reached.
- **impact:** Every runner pickup with cognee_recall enabled silently gets an EMPTY lessons block instead of the 3 top BM25 lessons that are sitting right there and demonstrably retrievable. The same defect shape exists in retrieve_antipatterns_via_cognee (cognee_integration.py:882-887 catches CogneeNotInstalled/Neo4jUnavailable/QueryTimeout/IndexCorruption only), so `_antipatterns_keyword_rank` is likewise unreachable when the LLM key is missing. Note this is a SEPARATE path from the project-state structural axis, whose BM25 half was correctly rewired by OP-2556 and does work.

### [MEDIUM] A slow JIRA call destroys the entire structural axis, including the BM25 lessons half that would have answered in ~3 ms

- **category:** design  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** 8 timed runs of `agg._run_axis('structural', agg.fetch_structural_axis, t, 0.8)` in prod after `agg.warm_lessons_index()`: OP-1886 -> 1336.1 ms, err=ProjectStateAxisTimeout:0.800s, kg_neighbours=NONE (no payload at all); the other 7 -> 292-446 ms with kg_neighbours=5, jira=live. Ratio 1/8. Separately, a full `aggregate_project_state('OP-1886')` logged `project_state.axis_timeout axis=structural latency_sec=0.908 budget=0.800` and produced `axis_content={'structural': 'degraded', ...}` with empty source_markers. Raw JIRA half timings: OP-1886 1263.4 ms (degraded), OP-2551 600.6 ms, OP-2574 264.8, OP-2618 306.8, OP-2663 306.1, OP-904 322.2. Code: fetch_structural_axis (project_state_aggregator.py:262-311) awaits _structural_jira FIRST, then calls _structural_lessons synchronously at :291.
- **expected vs actual:** The OP-2556/B1 comment at project_state_aggregator.py:67-74 states the BM25 half 'runs synchronously after the JIRA fetch, no thread, no network, no store' at ~3 ms warm, and both halves are supposed to 'degrade to empty lists under never-raising wrappers' so the axis never nulls out from a slow half. Actual: because the halves are sequential inside one coroutine, an axis-level timeout during the JIRA await means _structural_lessons never executes and the whole axis payload is None — the always-available half is lost to the flaky half's latency.
- **impact:** Roughly 1 in 8 pickups loses the only axis that currently carries content, degrading the prompt to nothing at all (temporal unavailable, causal empty, structural null). The 1336 ms observed also shows `asyncio.wait_for` at the 0.6 s inner fence and 0.8 s axis budget does not actually cancel the in-flight transport, so the call overruns the total 2 s budget contract as well.

### [MEDIUM] Causal neighbour selection truncates edges to 3 BEFORE filtering out same-ticket edges, under-delivering on every real sample

- **category:** bug  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** project_state_aggregator.py:712-716: `for e in edges[:3]:` then inside the loop `if other is None or other.ticket_key == ticket_key: continue`. Replayed the exact prod loop in the prod container against real runner_incidents (window widened to 2026-07-01 solely to simulate an active fleet), vs a filter-then-take-3 counterfactual: OP-1886 prod=1 vs alt=3; OP-2574 prod=2 vs alt=3; OP-2618 prod=2 vs alt=3; OP-2663 prod=2 vs alt=3 (graph in all cases 33 nodes / 54 edges).
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: up to 3 cross-ticket causal neighbours per the payload contract `neighbours[:3]`. Actual: the `[:3]` slice consumes the BFS budget on edges that are then discarded (same-ticket edges are the most common kind for a repeatedly-failing ticket), so the axis returns 1-2 neighbours where 3 were available. In the degenerate case where the first 3 edges of every capped own-incident are same-ticket, it returns 0.
- **impact:** Even after the fleet resumes writing incidents, the causal axis will systematically under-deliver by 33-67% and can report a plausible-looking non-empty result while having silently dropped the neighbours it was asked for. There is no marker distinguishing 'only 1 neighbour exists' from 'truncation ate the other 2'.

### [MEDIUM] Temporal axis is hardcoded unavailable, which makes the ProjectStateAllAxesFailed alarm structurally unreachable

- **category:** design  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** project_state_aggregator.py:638-641 `async def _temporal_graphiti(ticket_key): del ticket_key; return dict(TEMPORAL_UNAVAILABLE_PAYLOAD)` — no await point, no I/O, cannot raise, cannot exceed the 0.6 s budget. Line 902: `if all(r.payload is None for r in results.values()): raise ProjectStateAllAxesFailed(errors)`. `grep -rn dispatch_graphiti_temporal_query --include=*.py .` matches ONLY its own definition at graphiti_mcp_client.py:33 — zero production call sites. Live prod runs of aggregate_project_state for OP-1886 (structural=None) and OP-2551 both returned temporal={'status':'unavailable'} and raised nothing. Phase R design doc docs/design/2026-07-07-phase-r-3d-memory-repair-design.md:62 and :224 scope this deliberately ('R5 — temporal: honest at the API, invisible in the prompt'), deferring Graphiti to U5 (:283).
- **expected vs actual:** Expected per the module docstring (lines 31-34): 'ProjectStateAllAxesFailed — every axis raised or timed out; caller returns 200 with all-null axes'. Actual: the temporal axis is now a synchronous constant-return, so `results['temporal'].payload` is ALWAYS a non-None dict and the all-axes-failed condition can never be true. The router's `except agg.ProjectStateAllAxesFailed` handler (backend/api/project_state.py:180) and its typed operator log line are dead code.
- **impact:** The 'everything is broken' escalation path is gone. A total outage of both remaining axes (JIRA down + Postgres down) now serves a 200 that looks like a partial degrade rather than raising the typed exception that exists specifically to make total failure legible in the operator log. Note the temporal axis's own unavailability is honestly declared and correctly classified as content='unavailable' in metrics, and the runner strips it from the prompt (auto-runner-jira.py:1176-1178) — that part is not deceptive; the derived alarm death is.

### [MEDIUM] No alert or gate covers the causal or temporal axes; the staging gate inspects only the structural axis

- **category:** design  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** deploy/observability/prometheus/project_state_health.yml defines exactly 4 alerts (lines 11, 24, 37, 49): ProjectStateStructuralHollow (axis="structural" only), ProjectStateHalfHollow (structural halves only), ProjectStateNoTraffic (phantom metric), ProjectStateLatencyHigh (all-axis p95 latency). `grep -i alert` over infra/ deploy/ config/ for 'causal|temporal' -> no matches. backend/agents/staging_gate.py:517-625: the OP-2558 probe asserts jira_source == 'live' AND kg_source == 'live' AND len(kg_neighbours) > 0, and never reads payload['causal'] or payload['temporal'].
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: a three-axis product has health coverage for three axes. Actual: 100% of automated health signal is pointed at the structural axis. A permanently-empty causal axis and a permanently-unavailable temporal axis pass the staging gate and leave every alert inactive.
- **impact:** This is why finding #1 (causal empty for 12 days) has produced zero operator signal. The `content="empty"` label IS correctly emitted on omnisight_project_state_axis_total{axis="causal"}, so the data to alert on exists — nothing consumes it. Additionally, the two hollow alerts that do exist are rate()-ratio expressions over 15m/30m windows; with 2 requests in 9 days they evaluate to no-data and sit inactive regardless of how hollow the axis is (confirmed: both state=inactive while jira useful=false on 2/2 calls).

### [MEDIUM] `siblings` and `phase` ship in the structural payload contract but are never populated by any production code path

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** project_state_aggregator.py:306-309 emits `"phase": jira_view.get("phase")` and `"siblings": jira_view.get("siblings") or []`. `_map_jira_story` (:422-463) returns only jira_source / parent_meta / blockers / blocking / summary_internal — it never sets `phase` or `siblings`. `grep -rn '"siblings"' --include=*.py backend/` matches only the emit site plus two TEST fixtures (test_project_state.py:81, test_project_state_metrics.py:73,88). Live prod payload for OP-2551: `{'ticket': 'OP-2551', 'jira_source': 'live', 'kg_source': 'live', 'parent_meta': None, 'phase': None, 'blockers': 0, 'blocking': 0, 'siblings': 0, 'kg_neighbours': 5}`.
- **expected vs actual:** fetch_structural_axis's docstring (:266-267) states 'The JIRA half answers "what META, what blockers, what siblings."' and the JIRA_PULL_FIELDS comment (:330-332) explicitly notes labels are not requested 'so `phase` stays null'. Actual: `siblings` is unconditionally `[]` and `phase` unconditionally `null` on every response, with no marker distinguishing 'this ticket has no siblings' from 'siblings were never implemented'.
- **impact:** Two of the eight structural fields are permanently dead weight in every injected prompt. Only test fixtures ever populate them, so any test asserting sibling behaviour is validating a code path that does not exist in production. `phase` being null is at least documented in a code comment; `siblings` is contradicted by the docstring.

### [MEDIUM] The Neo4j graph store prod is configured against is empty AND unreachable from the backend

- **category:** mismatch-with-intent  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** Prod backend env: OMNISIGHT_COGNEE_NEO4J_URI=bolt://neo4j:7687, tenant t-default (CogneeConfig.from_env() dumped live, password redacted). `getent hosts neo4j` inside omnisight-productizer-backend-a-1 -> rc=2 (unresolvable). omnisight-neo4j container is up 5 weeks; `MATCH (n) RETURN count(n)` -> 0 and `CALL db.labels()` -> 0 rows, with the harness proven working via `SHOW DATABASES` (neo4j + system both online) and `RETURN 42` -> 42. Meanwhile the backend's own embedded cognee store at /cognee-data IS populated: 'Graph projection completed: 1207 nodes, 2002 edges'. The omnisight-cognee sidecar uses a third store entirely (kuzu: /app/.cognee_system/databases/cognee_graph_kuzu, 444K).
- **expected vs actual:** Expected after the historical finding 'prod graph provider was embedded ladybug NOT neo4j': one authoritative graph provider. Actual: three disjoint stores — an empty, DNS-unreachable Neo4j that config points at; a 1207-node embedded store the backend actually opens; and a separate kuzu store in the cognee sidecar. Nothing reconciles them.
- **impact:** Any code path that trusts OMNISIGHT_COGNEE_NEO4J_URI gets a hard failure (healthcheck -> Neo4jStartFailed) rather than the 1207-node graph that exists locally. This does NOT affect the project-state structural axis (OP-2556 correctly retired cognee from that hot path in favour of BM25), but it is the substrate for the runner's cognee_recall and antipattern_inject paths and for pipeline_coordinator_llm_consultation.py:951.

### [LOW] The runner logs nothing when project-state injection succeeds-but-hollow, unlike the sibling learned-items path which was explicitly built to log it

- **category:** design  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** auto-runner-jira.py `_build_project_state_block` (:1238-1258) logs only on skip-by-label and delegates failures to `_fetch_project_state`, which logs project_state.fetch_failed / malformed_response. On the success path it returns the rendered block with no log line at all. Contrast `_fetch_learned_items_block` (:1077-1108) whose docstring states 'result label logged on EVERY pickup (audit #6: hollow serving must be visible from runner logs)' and which prints `[runner] learned_items.result=<result>` unconditionally.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected, given the project's own stated anti-hollow rule: a pickup log line stating what each axis actually delivered. Actual: a pickup that injected {structural: 5 lessons, temporal: stripped, causal: []} and a pickup that injected three full axes produce byte-identical runner logs.
- **impact:** The runner-side view — the one an operator reads when debugging a bad pickup — cannot distinguish a healthy injection from a hollow one. Combined with the in-memory-only trace buffer (200 entries, lost on restart) and the absence of any durable project-state table (confirmed: no alembic migration references project_state; \dt over 149 prod tables shows none), there is no post-hoc record of what any past pickup actually received.

### [LOW] The temporal 'degraded' log line executes once at module import, not per request

- **category:** bug  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** False
- **evidence:** project_state_aggregator.py:96 `log.info("project_state.temporal.graphiti_unavailable status=unavailable")` sits at module top level, immediately after the TEMPORAL_UNAVAILABLE_PAYLOAD constant at :94 — outside any function. `_temporal_graphiti` (:638-641) itself logs nothing.
- **expected vs actual:** The log key `project_state.temporal.graphiti_unavailable` matches the naming convention of the per-request degrade lines (project_state.structural.half_degrade, project_state.causal.no_own_incidents), implying a per-call record. Actual: it emits exactly once per worker process at import time.
- **impact:** An operator grepping logs for temporal-axis activity sees one line per worker startup and could reasonably read it as 'the axis was consulted and degraded once' rather than 'the axis is a hardcoded constant that is never consulted'. Low severity because the metric label content='unavailable' is emitted correctly per request.

### [INFO] Positive verification: the JIRA structural half is genuinely repaired — the historical `if False` stub is gone and real data flows

- **category:** mismatch-with-intent  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** False
- **evidence:** Live prod calls of `_map_jira_story(await adapter.fetch_story(t, fields='issuelinks,parent,status,summary'))`: OP-2196 -> parent_meta={'key':'OP-2191','status':'To Do','summary':'[META][tooling] Multi-project runner routing — EPIC ...'}, blockers=1, blocking=1; OP-904 -> blockers=5, blocking=4; OP-1886 -> blocking=1. `ff.project_state_jira_pull.enabled()` -> True. Confirmed `parent` absence on OP-2551/OP-2535/OP-2556/OP-2727 is real data, not a field-projection bug: fetching OP-2551 with fields=None returned 78 fields and `parent` was still absent.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected (given the audit history at project_state_aggregator.py:299 being a permanent `if False` stub): possible residual stubbing. Actual: no stub remains; the adapter issues a real authenticated fetch and the mapper correctly projects Blocks-type issuelinks and parent.
- **impact:** None — this is the one axis-half that Phase R fully repaired and that I could positively confirm delivers real content. Recording it so the negative findings above are not misread as 'the whole thing is still fake'. The caveats are latency fragility (finding #5) and that prod's only 2 real requests both scored jira useful=false (omnisight_project_state_structural_half_total{half="jira",useful="false"}=2, {half="kg",useful="true"}=2), i.e. structural's 'non_empty' classification was carried entirely by the BM25 lessons half.

---

## Durability, retention and data integrity of the 3D / U6 memory system

**Auditor summary:** The Postgres side of memory is genuinely well protected — three independent encrypted dump lanes plus an async streaming standby, with a real restore-tested DR drill — but that protection stops exactly at the prod `omnisight` database, leaving the two memory stores that actually hold unique data (the `~/.claude/projects` file store and the staging DB carrying the entire leg-2 corpus) with zero backup coverage. Retention, erasure and integrity are materially worse than the backup lane: no memory table has any expiry rule, the live DSAR endpoint writes `status='completed'` while deleting none of the five memory tiers, `claude_memory_versions` is DB-level undeletable, and the one integrity gauge built for leg-3 (`omnisight_claude_memory_drift`) has no setter anywhere in the repo or the running image, so it reports 0 while true drift is 4.

**Checks performed:**
- Read scripts/backup_prod_db.sh in full (develop tip) and diffed it against /home/user/omnisight-prod/scripts/backup_prod_db.sh — identical
- Enumerated all user timers (24) and system timers (13); read the unit files for omnisight-prod-backup, omnisight-pgdump-s3-daily, omnisight-pgdump-local-daily, omnisight-dr-drill, omnisight-backup-freshness, staging-pg-snapshot, omnisight-alert@
- Read /home/user/work/sora/logs/backup/prod-backup.log, logs/omnisight-pgdump-s3-daily.log, logs/omnisight-pgdump-local-daily.log, logs/backup/freshness.log
- journalctl for omnisight-prod-backup.service, omnisight-dr-drill.service, omnisight-alert@omnisight-prod-backup.service.service
- Reproduced the backup prune exit-code-2 bug in the scratchpad with 31 synthetic files (SCRIPT_EXIT=2, post-loop message never printed)
- Listed /home/user/omnisight-prod/data/backups (30 manual-*.dump.gpg + 8 kek-escrow) and verified the manual-*.db* glob matches nothing
- Read /home/user/.local/bin/omnisight-dr-drill-pg.sh verification block (lines 115-126)
- Enumerated all memory-related tables in prod omnisight via information_schema, dumped full column lists for the 10 core tables
- Row counts + date ranges for l3_facts, chat_session_summaries, claude_memory_versions/state/transition_events/exports, runner_incidents, episodic_memory, l3_eval_runs, l3_approvals, l3_erasure_audit, curator_merge_candidates, learned_item_versions, memory_publications, memory_eval_runs (prod and staging)
- Queried pg_trigger + pg_get_triggerdef for all live triggers on the memory tables
- Queried pg_settings for archive_mode/archive_command/wal_level, pg_stat_replication, pg_extension, and pg_class for partitioned tables
- Read backend/routers/privacy.py _ERASURE_DELETE_STATEMENTS, _ERASURE_TENANT_DELETE_STATEMENTS, _erase_user_data, _fetch_all_user_data, create_erasure_request
- Verified the same erasure statement list inside the RUNNING prod image via docker exec on omnisight-productizer-backend-a-1

The claim rests on the enabled trigger definition read from pg_get_triggerdef plus the raising function body in migration 0279.

### [CRITICAL] DSAR erasure writes status='completed' while every memory tier survives — live in the running prod image

- **category:** data-integrity  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** Live container: `docker exec omnisight-productizer-backend-a-1 grep -n "_ERASURE_DELETE_STATEMENTS" -A 26 /app/backend/routers/privacy.py` → line 275-298 lists 11 tables: user_tenant_memberships, user_preferences, user_drafts, chat_messages, chat_sessions, sessions, user_mfa, mfa_backup_codes, password_history, api_keys, oauth_tokens. Zero memory tables. `backend/routers/privacy.py:401-428` (create_erasure_request) INSERTs dsar_requests with the literal `'completed'` in the same transaction, unconditionally, and returns `{"result": {"erased_counts": ...}}`. `_fetch_all_user_data` (`backend/routers/privacy.py:102-223`) has the identical blind spot — no memory table is disclosed either.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: an erasure that reports 'completed' has removed the subject's l3_facts, l3_eval_runs, l3_approvals, chat_session_summaries and episodic_memory rows. Actual: none of those five are touched; the request row still records completed and the API returns a success envelope with per-category counts that silently omit the memory tiers.
- **impact:** A statutory erasure request is answered with a false completion receipt. The subject's L3 facts, session summaries and episodic rows persist indefinitely with raw user_id. The project's own design doc (docs/architecture/2026-07-26-dsar-subject-ownership-epic-design.md:11-13) states this same finding; status line of that doc says only A+B+H1+UI are filed (OP-2745/2746/2748/2749) and C→G — the coverage fix — is 'designed below, unreviewed'. Nothing has landed.

### [HIGH] The entire leg-2 worker-loop corpus lives in the staging DB, which no backup lane covers

- **category:** data-integrity  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** All three backup lanes pin the same target: `scripts/backup_prod_db.sh` PG_CONTAINER default `omnisight-pg-primary` + PG_DB from that container (`omnisight`); `/home/user/.local/bin/omnisight-pgdump-local-daily.sh:49` `docker exec omnisight-pg-primary pg_dump -U omnisight -d omnisight`; `scripts/backup_postgres_daily_dockerized.sh:30,32` `PG_CONTAINER=omnisight-pg-primary`, `PG_DB=omnisight`. `grep -rl "omnisight_staging" ~/.local/bin ~/.config/systemd/user scripts/` returns only `staging-pg-snapshot.service` (a prod→staging restore, disabled). Row counts, staging DB `omnisight_staging` on `omnisight-staging-postgres-1`: curator_merge_candidates=5, learned_item_versions=9, memory_publications=8, memory_eval_runs=14, claude_memory_versions=177, chat_session_summaries=1. Same tables in prod `omnisight`: curator_merge_candidates=0, learned_item_versions=0, memory_publications=0, memory_eval_runs=0.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: 'the postgres side is backed up' covers the memory data that exists. Actual: it covers the prod DB, where every leg-2 table is empty; the only rows leg-2 has ever produced (curator candidates → distilled playbooks → eval runs → publications) sit on a staging database with no dump, no off-site copy and no standby.
- **impact:** Loss of the staging container/volume destroys the entire proven leg-2 curator spine — the artefact the 2026-07-22 'CODE-COMPLETE + STAGING-GREEN' claim rests on — with no recovery path. There is no restore drill for it either, because no artefact exists to drill.

### [HIGH] omnisight_claude_memory_drift — the leg-3 anti-hollow detector — has no setter anywhere and reports 0 while real drift is 4

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** Gauge declared at `backend/metrics.py:1245-1250` with the description "γ-0 (leg-3) anti-hollow: |store files| - |claude_memory_state rows| (nonzero after a full ingest = drift; set by the ingest CLI)". `grep -rn "drift_gauge" --include=*.py .` (excluding tests) returns ONLY the declaration at :1245 and the no-op stub at :1407 — no `.set()` call. Same grep inside the live image: `docker exec omnisight-productizer-backend-a-1 grep -rn "claude_memory_drift|drift_gauge" /app/backend/` returns only :1245-1246 and :1407. Prometheus confirms the exported value: `wget -qO- 'http://localhost:9090/api/v1/query?query=omnisight_claude_memory_drift'` → value "0" on both backend-a and backend-b. Actual drift measured by hand: store has 181 .md files vs claude_memory_state 177 rows for project_id='omnisight-productizer'; `comm -23 store_slugs db_slugs` → MEMORY, project_case5_epicB_twoway, project_failed_units_remediation_2026_07_25, project_s12g_A_v2_runtime_defense.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: the gauge reads |store files| − |state rows| and goes non-zero when the file store and the DB diverge. Actual: no code path ever writes it, so prometheus_client exports the Gauge's initial 0 forever — a permanently green integrity indicator that is structurally incapable of firing. Its true value right now is 4.
- **impact:** This is the exact silent-hollow compound the 2026-07-07 audit named, rebuilt inside the Phase-R repair itself: the single detector for 'the file store and the database have diverged' reports clean by construction. Anyone checking leg-3 coverage sees drift=0 and concludes the DB copy is complete.

### [HIGH] The ~/.claude/projects file store has zero backup coverage, and its Postgres mirror is 9 days and 3 files stale

- **category:** data-integrity  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** Store: `du -sh /home/user/.claude/projects` → 997M, 2884 files, 360 .md; the Productizer memory dir holds 181 .md (2.1M). Coverage search: `crontab -l` → one unrelated job (`omnisight-fate/scripts/emp-backup-cron.sh`); `systemctl --user list-timers --all` (24 timers) and `systemctl list-timers --all` (13 system timers) contain no home/file-store backup unit; `which restic borg duplicity rclone timeshift` → all absent; `grep -rl "\.claude/projects" ~/.local/bin ~/.config/systemd/user scripts/` → only `scripts/claude_memory_ingest.py` (a manual CLI). Mirror staleness: `select max(ingested_at) from claude_memory_versions where project_id='omnisight-productizer'` → 2026-07-23 14:50:23+00. `find . -name '*.md' -newermt '2026-07-23 22:50:24' | wc -l` → 9 (sanity: 172 older, 172+9=181, so the predicate works). 3 non-index memory files exist only on disk (see drift finding).
- **expected vs actual:** Expected per the leg-3 unification story: the file store is either backed up directly, or fully mirrored into the backed-up Postgres. Actual: neither. No lane copies the files; the DB mirror is a one-shot manual ingest last run 2026-07-23, `scripts/claude_memory_ingest.py` and `scripts/claude_memory_regen.py` are not referenced by any timer or unit.
- **impact:** A year of hand-curated cross-session memory is single-copy on one WSL2 filesystem. Losing it loses 3 memories entirely and rolls the other 178 back to their 2026-07-23 bodies — including project_u6_kernel_handoff_and_roadmap.md, the file the memory index itself marks 'READ FIRST' for post-reboot recovery.

### [HIGH] No memory table has any retention or expiry rule; the design explicitly forbids deletion

- **category:** mismatch-with-intent  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** Committed rule: `docs/architecture/2026-07-26-dsar-subject-ownership-epic-design.md:363` — "③ retention | compliance-driven but MUST END, bounded by project lifecycle and maintenance cycle ... A retained category with no expiry rule is now a defect, not a default." Live state: `select ... from information_schema.columns` shows no retention/expiry column on l3_facts (except valid_until), chat_session_summaries, claude_memory_versions, claude_memory_state, runner_incidents, episodic_memory, l3_eval_runs, l3_approvals. Contrast: `meetings.retention_until` exists and `backend/transcripts_retention.py:117-162` really deletes on it. `backend/memory_decay.py:3` states the opposing rule verbatim: "Locked design rule: **never delete memory rows, only down-weight them**." The only l3 lifecycle sweep, `backend/agents/u6_memory_scheduler.py:287`, is `UPDATE ... SET state='superseded' WHERE state='promoted' AND valid_until IS NOT NULL AND valid_until < $1` — a state flip, never a DELETE. Data: l3_facts has 1 row with valid_until IS NULL (`select valid_until, count(*) from l3_facts group by 1` → NULL|1). runner_incidents holds 1973 rows back to 2026-05-07. No pg_cron (`select extname from pg_extension` → plpgsql, vector) and no partitioned tables (`select relname from pg_class where relkind='p'` → empty).
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: every retained memory category carries an expiry rule and something enforces it at that boundary. Actual: zero of the five named tables have a rule; the one schema affordance that exists (l3_facts.valid_until) is NULL on the only row, and even when set it only flips state — the sealed ciphertext and the raw user_id stay in the table forever.
- **impact:** An unbounded personal-data store, by the project's own definition a defect rather than a default. It also compounds the erasure gap: because nothing ages out, everything the DSAR path fails to delete accumulates indefinitely.

### [HIGH] claude_memory_versions is undeletable at the database level — leg-3 memory cannot be erased at all

- **category:** design  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** False
- **evidence:** Live trigger definitions from `pg_get_triggerdef` on prod: `CREATE TRIGGER trg_claude_memory_versions_no_delete BEFORE DELETE ON public.claude_memory_versions FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION claude_memory_ledger_block_mutation()` (tgenabled='O'), plus the same for _no_update and for claude_memory_transition_events. The function raises unconditionally (`backend/alembic/versions/0279_claude_memories.py:85-92`). `backend/routers/claude_memories.py` exposes only ingest / pending / {slug}/publish — `grep -n "router.post|router.get|router.delete" ` shows no delete, erase or revoke endpoint. Table holds 179 rows including full `body` text.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: an append-only ledger still needs a governed erasure escape hatch for a subject-erasure or a retention boundary. Actual: DELETE is blocked for every row by an enabled BEFORE DELETE trigger, and no application surface even attempts it — the planned DSAR expansion (epic C→G) cannot cover this table without a migration to drop or bypass the trigger.
- **impact:** Any content that enters the leg-3 corpus is permanent. Combined with the fail-closed DLP allowlist history (the 2026-07-23..25 backup gap was caused by this very corpus tripping the DLP gate), a single bad ingest is unremovable by the application and would require DDL on prod.

### [MEDIUM] No alertmanager is configured and no memory rule is loaded — every U6 anti-hollow gauge is unwatched

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** `docker exec omnisight-productizer-prometheus-1 wget -qO- http://localhost:9090/api/v1/alertmanagers` → `{"activeAlertmanagers":[],"droppedAlertmanagers":[]}`. `/etc/prometheus/prometheus.yml` has no `alerting:` block and `rule_files:` lists exactly one file, `obs-rules/project_state_health.yml`; `alerts.yml` and `frontend-freshness-alerts.yml` sit in the same directory unloaded, and grepping all three for `u6_|memor|backup|l3_|claude` returns nothing. Live gauge values: omnisight_u6_l2_summaries_count=1, omnisight_u6_l3_fact_count{state="promoted"}=1, omnisight_u6_l2_last_summary_age_seconds=854926 (≈9.9 days and rising).
- **expected vs actual:** `backend/agents/u6_memory_scheduler.py:56-62` claims the monitor arm makes 'a flag-ON-but-hollow scheduler VISIBLE (frozen count + growing age), the 3D-memory lesson'. Actual: the gauges are correctly DB-derived and exported, but no alert rule references them and Prometheus has nowhere to send an alert even if one existed.
- **impact:** The stated anti-hollow guarantee for leg-1 is not realised in the live deployment. L2 has been frozen at one summary for ~10 days with the age gauge climbing and nothing has fired. The same blind spot would cover an unexpected drop in l3_fact_count or summaries_count — i.e. silent row deletion.

### [MEDIUM] omnisight-prod-backup.service is permanently failed by a prune bug that runs AFTER the backup succeeds, keeping the backup alert channel red

- **category:** bug  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** False
- **evidence:** `systemctl --user status omnisight-prod-backup.service` → `Active: failed (Result: exit-code) since Sat 2026-08-01 02:17:55`, `status=2/INVALIDARGUMENT`. The artefact was nevertheless produced: `/home/user/omnisight-prod/data/backups/manual-20260801-021738.dump.gpg` (3139698 bytes) and the log's last lines are the normal `[OK] backup (encrypted): ...` + `[WARN] off-site immutable backup skipped`. Root cause is the prune block at the end of `scripts/backup_prod_db.sh`: with `set -Eeuo pipefail`, `ls -1t "$BKP_DIR"/manual-*.db* "$BKP_DIR"/manual-*.dump* 2>/dev/null | tail -n +31 | while read ...` has no `|| true` (unlike the identical ls three lines above), and `manual-*.db*` matches nothing so `ls` exits 2, which pipefail propagates. Reproduced in the scratchpad with 31 synthetic files: printed `count=31`, deleted the oldest, never printed the post-loop message, `SCRIPT_EXIT=2`. Directory state confirms the trigger: 30 `manual-*.dump*` files, 0 `manual-*.db*`. The failure does alert — `journalctl --user -u omnisight-alert@omnisight-prod-backup.service.service` → "ALERT omnisight-prod-backup.service: commented on OP-2744".
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: unit result reflects whether a usable backup exists. Actual: from the 31st retained backup onward the unit fails every night with a successful artefact on disk, firing a JIRA alert each time.
- **impact:** Inverted signal. The OnFailure→JIRA channel for the prod backup lane is now permanently red for a benign cause, so a genuine failure (pg_dump error, DLP block, gpg failure) is indistinguishable from the nightly noise. This is the alerting built by OP-2728 being turned into noise — the exact outcome the freshness unit's own comment warns against.

### [MEDIUM] The DR drill's 'RESTORE VERIFIED' never asserts that any memory table has rows

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** `/home/user/.local/bin/omnisight-dr-drill-pg.sh` verification block, lines 115-126: TABLES = `select count(*) from information_schema.tables where table_schema='public'` checked `>= MIN_TABLES`; HEAD = alembic_version; AUDIT = `select count(*) from audit_log` checked `> 0`. Nothing else is queried. Last run (`journalctl --user -u omnisight-dr-drill.service`, Aug 01 04:32): "TOC=949 tables=149 alembic=0280 audit_log=21215" → "RESTORE VERIFIED". The legacy checkers are worse and unscheduled: `scripts/backup_selftest.py:37-40` REQUIRED_TABLES = tasks, agents, workflow_runs, workflow_steps, audit_log, episodic_memory — the only memory table listed — and it is SQLite-only against `data/omnisight.db`, which prod no longer uses.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: a drill that proves the backup would restore the memory system. Actual: it proves the dump decrypts, has a PGDMP header, restores 149 tables and a non-empty audit_log. A dump in which l3_facts, chat_session_summaries and claude_memory_versions were all empty would pass identically.
- **impact:** The one control that could catch 'we have been backing up an empty memory tier for weeks' does not look at the memory tier. Given l3_facts=1, chat_session_summaries=1 and episodic_memory=0 in prod today, the difference between 'correctly nearly-empty' and 'silently emptied' is currently unverifiable from the drill.

### [MEDIUM] No point-in-time recovery: WAL archiving is off, so an unexpected memory deletion cannot be rolled back to just before it

- **category:** data-integrity  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** False
- **evidence:** `select name, setting from pg_settings where name in ('archive_mode','archive_command','wal_level','full_page_writes')` on omnisight-pg-primary → archive_mode=off, archive_command=(disabled), wal_level=replica, full_page_writes=on. `pg_stat_replication` → one async streaming standby at 172.20.0.3 (which replicates a DELETE, it does not protect against one). `scripts/backup_wal_hourly.sh` exists (header: "[OP-887] Hourly WAL archive marker for sub-hour RPO") but `grep -rl backup_wal_hourly ~/.config/systemd/user ~/.local/bin` returns nothing. Dump cadence: omnisight-prod-backup.timer 02:17 daily, omnisight-pgdump-s3-daily.timer 03:00 daily.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected for a store the project treats as compliance-relevant: PITR to a chosen instant. Actual: recovery granularity is the daily dump; RPO for memory rows is up to ~24h and restoring after a bad delete means rolling the whole database back a day.
- **impact:** If a buggy sweep, a mis-scoped DELETE, or the planned DSAR-erasure expansion removes the wrong memory rows, the only remedy is a whole-DB restore to the previous 02:17/03:00 dump, discarding up to a day of every other table too. The erase_user docstring itself (u6_l3_store.py:196-203) hands the WAL/PITR purge SLA to 'the ops layer', which does not have one.

### [MEDIUM] Memory writes and deletions leave no audit trail in the hash-chained audit_log

- **category:** data-integrity  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** `select action, count(*) from audit_log where action ilike '%memor%' or action ilike '%l3%' or action ilike '%fact%' group by 1` → 0 rows. Query validated against the same table: `select count(*) from audit_log` → 21598 and the unfiltered top-25 returns real actions (release.staging_gate_canary 11029, workspace.gc_executed 7589, merger.* , auth.* …), so the predicate form works and the absence is real. Trigger inventory on prod shows delete protection only on claude_memory_versions and claude_memory_transition_events; l3_facts, chat_session_summaries, episodic_memory and runner_incidents have no delete trigger and no tombstone. l3_erasure_audit = 0 rows, and it is written only by `backend/agents/u6_l3_store.py:216-219` on the /memories/erase path — the /privacy/erasure path writes nothing there.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: 'is there anything that detects unexpected deletion of memory rows' → an audit trail or checksum monitor. Actual: the tamper-evident audit_log knows nothing about memory; row-count gauges exist but are unalerted (see the alertmanager finding); and the only per-row content hash, claude_memory_versions.body_sha256, is verified solely by `scripts/claude_memory_regen.py:175-186`, a manual CLI that no timer or unit invokes.
- **impact:** A direct SQL deletion or corruption of l3_facts, chat_session_summaries, episodic_memory or runner_incidents would leave no trace anywhere and trip no alarm. The integrity machinery that does exist (body_sha256 reconcile, the drift gauge) is either manual or dead.

### [MEDIUM] chat_session_summaries is plaintext personal data engineered to outlive the chat prune, with no delete path anywhere in backend/

- **category:** data-integrity  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** `backend/alembic/versions/0272_chat_session_summaries.py:1-10` states the row carries "provenance that survives the 30-day `chat_messages` prune (`source_message_hashes`)". Live row shape (no PII printed): `select length(user_id)>0, jsonb_array_length(source_message_hashes), token_count` → t | 8 | 467; `select jsonb_object_keys(summary_outcome)` → topics, resolved, turn_count, outcome_kind, tasks_created, schema_version — plain JSON, not sealed (contrast l3_facts.sealed_ciphertext + dek_ref). Live triggers: only trg_chat_session_summaries_no_update; DELETE is permitted. `grep -rn` for a delete on this table across backend/ finds it only in two test files (test_u6_memory_scheduler.py:448, test_u6_l2_writer.py:245). The DSAR path does delete chat_messages and chat_sessions (privacy.py:282-285).
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: erasing a user removes the derived summary along with the raw messages. Actual: the raw messages are deleted and the derivative — raw user_id, session_id, plaintext outcome JSON, plus hashes of the now-deleted messages — is deliberately designed to survive, has no delete code path, no retention rule and no expiry column.
- **impact:** Erasure produces a residue that is both un-erasable through any shipped code path and, because the source messages are gone, unauditable against its own provenance. This is the one memory tier where deletion is technically permitted by the DB yet no code exists to perform it.

### [LOW] The runner_incidents write-rate canary built to catch exactly this class of failure is not scheduled

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** `scripts/incident_write_canary.py` header: "OP-2544 -- runner incident write-rate canary. Daily read-only audit for the OP-2537 durable incident writer ... escalates to one JIRA ticket only when the fleet was active but no durable incident writes landed." `grep -rl incident_write_canary ~/.config/systemd/user ~/.local/bin` → nothing; `grep -rn` across .gitlab-ci.yml, Makefile, docs/, scripts/ finds only the Phase-R design draft (docs/architecture/2026-07-07-phase-r-3d-memory-ticket-filing-draft.md:323-332). Meanwhile `select max(created_at) from runner_incidents` → 2026-07-20 (12 days ago) with 1973 rows total, while runner-claude@1 and runner-claude@2 are `active (running)`.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: a daily canary compares fleet activity against durable incident writes and escalates on a gap. Actual: the script exists, is tested, and is invoked by nothing — so a 12-day gap in the causal-axis writer has passed unremarked.
- **impact:** Phase R shipped the detector but not the schedule, so the repaired writer has no liveness guard. Whether the 12-day gap is a genuine quiet period or a stalled writer is undeterminable from here — which is precisely the state the canary was built to end. (INFERENCE: that the canary would have flagged it; I did not run it.)

Current state: `systemctl --user status staging-pg-snapshot.service` → "loaded ... disabled; preset: enabled", "Active: inactive (dead)"; no staging-pg-snapshot.timer is installed (`ls ~/.config/systemd/user | grep staging-pg` → service only). - **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** Runner config: `~/.config/systemd/user/runner-claude@.service:21` `Environment=OMNISIGHT_MEMORY_TOOL_ROOT=/var/omnisight/memory`; code default `backend/agents/memory_tool_handler.py:154`. Host path exists with 10 fleet subdirs (claude, claude-1/2, codex, codex-1/2, default, merger, test) but `find /var/omnisight/memory -type f | wc -l` → 0 and `du -sh` → 40K (directories only), newest dir mtime 2026-07-12. No backup lane references /var/omnisight. In the prod backend container the path does not exist at all (`ls /var/omnisight/memory` → No such file or directory), the rootfs is read-only (`docker inspect ... .HostConfig.ReadonlyRootfs` → true) with tmpfs only on /app/.agent_workspaces and /tmp, so `memory_tool_handler.py:457` `mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)` cannot succeed there.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: a configured memory-tool store is durable and covered. Actual: on the host it holds nothing and is outside every backup lane; in the backend container its root is uncreatable on a read-only rootfs.
- **impact:** Nothing is currently at risk because nothing is stored, but the store is configured on both the runner and backend sides and would be un-backed-up the moment it started holding data. No monitor exists for its file count either.

### [INFO] What IS protected: the prod Postgres memory tables have three encrypted lanes, a standby, and a real restore drill

- **category:** data-integrity  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** False
- **evidence:** Lane 1 — omnisight-prod-backup.timer 02:17 daily: pg_dump custom format of omnisight-pg-primary/omnisight, DLP-scanned against a restored throwaway DB, gpg AES-256, 30 retained in /home/user/omnisight-prod/data/backups (30 *.dump.gpg present, Jul 2 → Aug 1). Lane 2 — omnisight-pgdump-local-daily.timer 11:00: /home/user/backup/daily-*.sql.gpg encrypted to kek-escrow@sora-dev.app. Lane 3 — omnisight-pgdump-s3-daily.timer: log shows `[2026-08-01T03:01:45Z] upload OK: s3://omnisight-backups-183088435847/postgres/daily/daily-20260801T030053Z.dump.gz.gpg` with .sha256 and .attestation.json siblings; freshness probe (stage=s3_attested, max_age=2d) reports "OK: off-site backup cohort is complete and fresh", newest complete cohort 20260731T030005Z. Attested cohorts begin 20260727T141740Z, matching the claimed off-site-since date. Plus `pg_stat_replication` → one async streaming standby, and omnisight-dr-drill.timer decrypt+restore-tests a real artefact daily. All memory tables in the prod `omnisight` DB (l3_*, chat_session_summaries, claude_memory_*, episodic_memory, runner_incidents, learned_item_*, memory_*) are inside these dumps.
- **expected vs actual:** The claim 'the postgres side is backed up (encrypted, off-site since 2026-07-27)' is CONFIRMED for the prod database, including all its memory tables.
- **impact:** None — recorded so the gaps above are read against an accurate baseline. Note the prod-backup lane's own S3 upload is skipped nightly ("[WARN] off-site immutable backup skipped (OMNISIGHT_BACKUP_S3_URI unset)"); off-site is delivered by lane 3, not lane 1.

---

## leg-1 — Sora's 3-tier persistent memory (L1 working / L2 episodic / L3 semantic)

**Auditor summary:** The leg-1 code is genuinely built, not a stub: the crypto seal is real AES-256-GCM, the closed 7-predicate fact schema really does make directives unrepresentable, every SQL statement carries explicit tenant+user predicates, the /memories route + UI exist and are linked, and the L2 writer produced a real scheduler-written row — so this is materially better than the 2026-07 "silent-hollow compound". What is still wrong is concentrated in the governance and observability halves of the mandate: L2 episodic has no delete path anywhere in the repo, the GDPR erase/access endpoints omit the entire memory tier while reporting success, four of the six mandated operational counters do not exist (the L3 read path shipped flag-ON in prod with zero metrics), no alert references any memory series, the leader gate does not actually elect a leader, and INFO-level telemetry — including the entire /memories consent audit trail — is silently dropped in production.

**Checks performed:**
- Read the full leg-1 module set at develop tip: backend/agents/u6_l3_store.py, u6_l3_reader.py, u6_l3_confirm.py, u6_l3_producer.py, u6_l3_eval_adapter.py, u6_l2_writer.py, u6_memory_scheduler.py, u6_metrics_refresh.py, u6_fact_schema.py, u6_fact_triage.py, u6_memory_safety_eval.py, u6_memory_crypto.py, backend/routers/memories.py
- Traced the injection wiring end-to-end: nodes.py:2107-2133 `_append_l3_user_memory` → called once at nodes.py:2390, appended to persona before `SystemMessage(INJECTION_GUARD_PRELUDE + persona)` at :2392
- Verified the prod image is byte-identical to develop tip for the leg-1 files (sha256 of u6_l3_reader.py, u6_memory_scheduler.py, routers/memories.py, agents/nodes.py inside omnisight-productizer-backend-a-1 vs the repo) — so merged == live for this backend code
- Read flag values from /home/user/omnisight-prod/.env and from the live container env: OMNISIGHT_SORA_L3_READ=1, OMNISIGHT_U6_L2_WRITE=1, OMNISIGHT_SORA_MEMORY_SCHEDULER=1, OMNISIGHT_U6_METRICS_ENABLED=1, OMNISIGHT_AUTH_MODE=strict, OMNISIGHT_WORKERS=2; confirmed all four flags default OFF in code (u6_l3_confirm.py:37-40, u6_l2_writer.py:36-38, u6_memory_scheduler.py:108-110, u6_metrics_refresh.py:40-42)
- Queried prod PG (omnisight-pg-primary, db omnisight): row counts and full contents of l3_facts, l3_eval_runs, l3_approvals, l3_erasure_audit, chat_session_summaries; per-session chat_messages coverage; chat_messages schema and time range
- Queried staging PG (omnisight-staging-postgres-1) for the same five tables to establish the dogfood order and confirm the erase path was exercised there first
- Verified RLS state and role privileges in prod: pg_class.relrowsecurity/relforcerowsecurity + pg_policies count for all five tables; pg_roles for the app role (omnisight: rolsuper=t, rolbypassrls=t)
- Verified FK delete actions on all four tenant-FK'd memory tables via pg_constraint.confdeltype
- Confirmed the stored ciphertext is a real AES-256-GCM envelope (jsonb minus ciphertext/nonce shows fmt/alg/dek/tid) and that dek_ref carries wrapped_dek_b64 (416 chars, provider local-fernet) — i.e. crypto-shred deletes the only key; read backend/security/envelope.py encrypt() to confirm it never falls back to plaintext regardless of OMNISIGHT_KS_ENVELOPE_ENABLED
- Queried Prometheus (omnisight-productizer-prometheus-1): __name__ label values for all u6/memory series; instant values for u6_l2_summaries_count, u6_l2_last_summary_age_seconds, u6_l3_fact_count, u6_l3_expired_promoted_backlog, u6_memory_scheduler_ticks_total, u6_l2_summaries_written_total; increase()[3h] on the scheduler and metrics-refresh counters; and the full /api/v1/rules list
- Counted and dated scheduler tick log lines in both prod backends to test the leader-gate claim (backend-b: 4,728 leader ticks over 2,430 intervals = 1.95/interval); cross-checked with the outcome="skipped" counter
- Established the prod logging level empirically: no basicConfig/dictConfig outside __main__ scripts; backend-a has 65 log lines and 0 of ~2,869 expected INFO tick lines; WARNING-sourced lines (llm_balance_refresher.py:300) and ERROR-sourced lines (ai_core_probe.py:193) do appear — positive controls for the grep
- Grepped the whole repo for any DELETE/prune of chat_session_summaries (only 2 test-cleanup hits) and read backend/routers/privacy.py `_ERASURE_DELETE_STATEMENTS`, `_ERASURE_TENANT_DELETE_STATEMENTS`, `_erase_user_data` and `_fetch_all_user_data` in full, plus admin_tenants.py's tenant-purge statement list
- Confirmed zero metric emission in the five leg-1 surfaces with `grep -L` for a metrics import, cross-checked with a per-file grep for Counter/inc(/observe(
- Read the frozen design §5 (read-path placement, token cap, MAJOR-14) and §6 (metrics) and §7 (kill switches + as-built erratum) to source the exact mandates being audited
- Checked the frontend surface: app/settings/memory/page.tsx (506 lines, list/pending/propose/discard/revoke/erase with a typed DELETE confirmation), lib/api.ts:8855-8905 client calls, and the nav entry at components/omnisight/user-menu.tsx:113
- Reviewed the test suite for the isolation DoDs: test_u6_l3_store.py cross-user app-predicate test and the non-superuser RLS probe (CREATE ROLE l3_rls_probe NOSUPERUSER, predicate-free read, plus a fail-closed unset-session assertion), and confirmed .gitlab-ci.yml:50-58 really does provide OMNI_TEST_PG_URL against a pgvector service so those tests are not silently skipped in CI
- Enumerated test_memories_route.py coverage (16 tests incl. cross-user denial, sensitive-ack 428, double-confirm 409, erase-clears-everything)

**Could not check:**
- Could not execute any write path (POST /memories/propose|confirm|revoke|erase, /privacy/erase, tenant purge) — the read-only constraint forbids it. So the GDPR-erase omission and the tenant-purge FK failure are proven from code + schema, not from a live run; the tenant-purge failure specifically is marked INFERENCE.
- Could not decrypt the stored L3 fact's sealed value or source_span (would require the local-fernet KEK and a decrypt call). The claim that the live row's provenance is a synthetic `memories_ui:<uuid>` token rests on the code at routers/memories.py:208 being the only live producer, which I verified by grepping all callers of insert_fact / produce_quarantine_and_record.
