Temporal being permanently `unavailable` is a deliberate R5 decision (project_state_aggregator.py:638-641 returns a pinned `{"status": "unavailable"}`), so that gap is defensible — but causal, the axis whose only writer never existed in the original audit, is currently classified `empty` on every served sample and no rule watches it.
- **impact:** The specific axis with the worst historical track record has zero alert coverage. It can silently return to the pre-Phase-R state and the only surviving signal would be a Grafana panel in a Grafana that has no dashboards.

### [MEDIUM] A causal-axis failure is encoded identically to a genuinely empty causal result, so no alert could ever separate them

- **category:** design  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** backend/agents/project_state_aggregator.py:658 (`except ImportError: return {"ticket": ticket_key, "neighbours": []}`) and :669 (`except Exception … return {"ticket": ticket_key, "neighbours": []}` after only a `log.info`). The classifier at project_state_aggregator.py:994-1000 skips the keys `{"ticket", "jira_source", "kg_source"}` and treats `[]` as no content, so both error returns land on `content="empty"` — the same label a successful query with genuinely no neighbours produces. `_classify_payload` (project_state_aggregator.py:982-992) reserves `"degraded"` for a `None` payload only, which these paths never return.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: the four-value content enum (non_empty|empty|unavailable|degraded) lets an operator distinguish 'no data' from 'the source broke'. Actual: for the causal axis, source breakage is laundered into `empty`. A failure becomes an empty result rather than an error — the exact pattern named in the original audit.
- **impact:** Even if a causal-axis alert were added tomorrow, it could not tell a dead incident source from a quiet week, so it would have to be tuned loose enough to be useless — or it would page on every idle period.

### [MEDIUM] Grafana holds zero dashboards and zero datasources; the project-state health dashboard has never been loaded

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** `docker cp omnisight-productizer-grafana-1:/var/lib/grafana/grafana.db` then `select id,uid,title,created from dashboard` -> no rows; `select name,type,url from data_source` -> no rows. `docker exec omnisight-productizer-grafana-1 ls -la /etc/grafana/provisioning/dashboards` -> empty directory. `docker inspect omnisight-productizer-grafana-1` shows exactly one mount: the named volume `omnisight-grafana -> /var/lib/grafana`; docker-compose.prod.yml:1004-1005 mounts nothing else, so deploy/observability/grafana/project_state_health.json is never delivered to the container. Meanwhile backend/tests/test_project_state_alert_rules.py:216-232 asserts the dashboard file exists and that its panel thresholds match the alert thresholds.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: a Grafana dashboard rendering axis health and per-half markers, thresholds aligned with the alerts. Actual: the JSON is a repo file that a unit test reads and that Grafana has never seen; the running Grafana has not even a Prometheus datasource configured.
- **impact:** The human-facing surface of the anti-hollow work does not exist at runtime. With Alertmanager also absent, there is currently no rendered and no pushed channel by which axis health reaches a person — only Prometheus' own /api/v1/alerts, which nothing polls.

### [MEDIUM] A total exporter loss would be invisible: no `absent()` guard exists for the three families that actually exist, and a missing prometheus_client still returns HTTP 200

- **category:** design  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** deploy/observability/prometheus/project_state_health.yml contains exactly one `absent()` rule (line 38) and it points at the never-emitted `omnisight_project_state_calls_total`. There is no absence rule for `omnisight_project_state_axis_total`, `_structural_half_total` or `_axis_latency_seconds`. backend/metrics.py:45-51: on `ImportError` the module sets `_AVAILABLE = False` and defines `generate_latest` to return `b"# prometheus_client not installed\n"`; backend/metrics.py:1420-1421 returns that body with a 200-class response, and backend/metrics.py:1375-1377 replaces the three project-state metrics with `_NoOp()`. Prometheus currently reports both targets `up` with `lastError` empty.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected, per the team's own OP-2561 lesson: 'For Prometheus-backed SLOs, add a companion absent() or equivalent no-data rule for each required metric family, and make the no-data path page or fail the gate at the same severity as the bad-data path.' Actual: exactly one absent() rule exists and it guards a phantom.
- **impact:** If the exporter regressed, /api/v1/metrics would still answer 200 (target stays `up`), all three ratio/quantile rules would go NaN and deactivate, and the only absent() rule is already permanently firing — so the transition would produce no observable change anywhere. The failure mode of the anti-hollow instrumentation is itself silent.

### [MEDIUM] The Phase S verification harness proves that files contain strings, not that anything works

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** backend/tests/test_project_state_alert_rules.py header: 'Pure-Python checks only: promtool is intentionally not required in this environment. The tests pin the loadable Prometheus surface, prod mount, specific rule_files entry, and Grafana threshold alignment.' It asserts exact expr strings (:140-152), that the compose file contains the mount string (:173), that rule_files equals a literal list (:182), and that the dashboard JSON mentions each metric (:205-213 — including the nonexistent `omnisight_project_state_calls_total`). backend/tests/test_alertmanager_wiring.py header: 'Offline / no-docker by design … Code-AC green is pure config-correctness — parse the committed configs and pin their shape. amtool / promtool validation runs only when the binary happens to be on PATH (skip, never fail, when absent).' `test_both_receivers_exist_as_name_only_blackholes` codifies non-delivery as the passing state.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: tests that would have caught 'the exporter emits nothing', 'the rule references a phantom metric', 'no Alertmanager is running', 'Grafana has no dashboards'. Actual: every assertion is satisfied by text in the repository. All of them pass right now while every runtime claim in this report is false.
- **impact:** This is the verification harness that produced confident wrong answers before. A green Phase S test run is compatible with: zero alert delivery, a dead SLO monitor, an uninstalled canary, an unprovisioned dashboard, and a phantom metric enshrined as a requirement. The tests cannot degrade — so the gap can only be found by exactly this kind of live inspection.

### [MEDIUM] The SLO monitor has produced no output in 10 days of running: no success-path log, no heartbeat metric, no OnFailure hook

- **category:** design  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** `journalctl --user -u omnisight-slo-monitor.service -n 25` -> `-- No entries --`, while `systemctl --user status` shows `Active: active (running) since Wed 2026-07-22 22:49:46 CST; 1 week 2 days ago`, `CPU: 2min 9.092s`. `grep -c 'logger\.' /home/user/sora-bridge/backend/orchestrator/slo_monitor.py` -> `6`, all of them error paths (`logger.warning` at :547 for metric-source-unavailable, `logger.exception` at :626, :710, :714, :828, :896). The unit file has no `OnFailure=` (contrast omnisight-disk-floor.service:11, omnisight-log-retention.service:6 etc., which all carry `OnFailure=omnisight-alert@%n.service` after OP-2728/OP-2731). It emits no Prometheus metric of its own.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: a monitor whose liveness and verdicts are observable. Actual: it is a black box that logs only on error and, because its metric queries silently return 0.0, never errors. A dead slo_monitor and a silently-green slo_monitor produce identical evidence: nothing.
- **impact:** There is no way to distinguish 'the SLO monitor is watching and everything is fine' from 'the SLO monitor is watching nothing' from 'the SLO monitor is wedged' — which is how it stayed hollow through a restart on 2026-07-22, two weeks after its fix was merged.

### [MEDIUM] Metric-emission failures in the axis exporter are swallowed to log.debug, and runner-side fetch failures leave no metric trace at all

- **category:** design  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** backend/api/project_state.py:135-136: `except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 — metrics must never break serving` / `log.debug("project_state.metrics_emit_failed", exc_info=True)`. backend/agents/project_state_aggregator.py:950-951: `except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 — metrics must never break aggregation` / `log.debug("project_state.axis_latency_observe_failed", exc_info=True)`. On the consumer side, auto-runner-jira.py:1109-1165 (`_fetch_project_state`) collapses unreachable / timeout / non-200 / malformed-JSON into a single `return None` with a stderr line and no counter; the axis counters at backend/api/project_state.py:112-134 only move on a served 200, so a runner that can never reach the endpoint leaves the metric surface perfectly flat. Corroborating: the counters have moved by ~1 in nine days while `OMNISIGHT_PROJECT_STATE_INJECT=true` is set in /home/user/.config/omnisight/runner.env and all four runner units report `active`.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: the instrumentation itself is fail-loud, or at least fail-visible. Actual: the emit path degrades to a DEBUG log (invisible at the default INFO level), and the client-side failure path produces neither a metric nor a WARN — so 'the runner cannot reach the aggregator' and 'the runner never asked' are the same observation.
- **impact:** The two most likely ways the axis goes quiet again — the exporter breaking, or the consumer losing the endpoint — are both invisible in the metric surface that the entire Phase S alerting chain is built on.

### [LOW] Five further alert rules are mounted into Prometheus but never loaded, because rule_files is a single-entry list

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** configs/prometheus.yml:6-7 -> `rule_files: [/etc/prometheus/obs-rules/project_state_health.yml]`. `docker inspect omnisight-productizer-prometheus-1` shows the whole directory bind-mounted: `/home/user/omnisight-prod/deploy/observability/prometheus -> /etc/prometheus/obs-rules`, and that directory contains alerts.yml, frontend-freshness-alerts.yml and project_state_health.yml. `curl http://localhost:9090/api/v1/rules` returns exactly ONE group (`omnisight_project_state_health`, 4 rules). Parsing the unloaded files: alerts.yml -> ['OmniSightReplicaLagHigh','OmniSightRollingDeploy5xxRateHigh','OmniSightBackendInstanceDown','OmniSightMigrationMismatch']; frontend-freshness-alerts.yml -> ['OmniSightFrontendBuildLagHigh'].
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: rules placed in the mounted rules directory are evaluated. Actual: they are inert. This was already written up in docs/retrospectives/2026-07-25-failed-unit-sweep.md and docs/sop/lessons/L-OP-2728-…md, and remains true one week later.
- **impact:** The alerting surface is thinner than the file tree suggests — including `OmniSightBackendInstanceDown`, which would be the natural backstop for 'the exporter's process is gone'. Anyone auditing by `ls` rather than by `/api/v1/rules` will over-count coverage by 5 alerts.

## leg-3 — Claude cross-session memory (file store, MEMORY.md index, claude_memory_* substrate, ingest/regen/publish)

**Auditor summary:** The leg-3 substrate itself is genuinely sound where it can be checked in the database — the append-only version ledger is trigger-enforced and live, no path loses a prior body version, and the publish endpoint is really human-only — but the governance it implements is decoupled from the thing that is actually injected: MEMORY.md on disk is still the hand-maintained file, so 43 of the 84 index entries currently in every session's system context are `quarantined` and one has no DB row at all. Around that core I found a compound of silent failures: two memories permanently un-ingestable by the slug regex (still indexed, invisible to every regen counter), an ingest CLI that exits 0 on server rejections, a `claude_memory_drift` gauge whose help text names a writer that does not exist anywhere in the repo, the `hook` field (the only free text in the injected index) sitting entirely outside the publish binding and rewritable by three live `["*"]`-scoped API keys, and a documented "server pin hard-refuse" defence that is not implemented on either side.

**Checks performed:**
- Read the full leg-3 source at develop tip: scripts/claude_memory_ingest.py, scripts/claude_memory_regen.py, backend/routers/claude_memories.py (512 lines), backend/claude_memory_bridge.py, backend/alembic/versions/0279_claude_memories.py, 0280_claude_memory_exports.py, backend/metrics.py:1230-1260, backend/main.py:928-948 (_api_key_scope_gate), backend/api_keys.py:68-98 (scope_allows), backend/learned_item_approval.py:47-74 (assert_human_principal), backend/claude_memory_lint.py
- Confirmed the tree I read is current: `git diff --stat gerrit-sora/develop HEAD` over all six leg-3 files is empty (working tree == gerrit-sora/develop at 1a3a1570, 2026-07-29); the local `develop` ref in that clone is stale (2026-06-28) and does not contain the files
- Compared every checkout that could host the host-side CLI: /home/user/omnisight-prod (6881 B regen, 0 hits for _MIN_RETAIN_RATIO), /home/user/work/sora/OmniSight-betaF (11675 B, 3 hits), /home/user/sora-bridge and /home/user/work/sora/OmniSight-Productizer (absent); plus the running prod image via `docker exec omnisight-productizer-backend-a-1 ls -la /app/scripts/claude_memory_*.py`
- Enumerated the live file store: 181 files (180 topic + MEMORY.md), MEMORY.md 51476 B / 50071 chars / 174 index lines / 171 unique slugs (3 duplicate lines) / 150 lines over 200 bytes / max line 1159 B; `.memory.pin` absent
- Queried prod postgres (omnisight-pg-primary, db omnisight) read-only: state counts (137 quarantined / 40 published), 179 versions over 177 slugs with max revision 2, 219 transition events, 9 export-ledger rows, api_keys scopes, \d on claude_memory_versions and api_keys, pg_indexes and pg_trigger for all claude_memory_* tables, hook length distribution, publish-event min/max timestamps
- Recomputed body sha256 for every DB row using the SHIPPED code path (imported _parse_frontmatter from scripts/claude_memory_ingest.py and applied the same `body if body.strip() else raw` rule as _local_body_sha) — positive control: 34/40 published rows and 170/177 head rows reproduced the server sha exactly, which validates the harness before I relied on its negatives
- Established the slug-regex root cause with a positive+negative control: of 180 topic files exactly 2 stems fail ^[a-z0-9_]{1,120}$, and exactly those 2 are the only pre-existing files absent from the 177 DB rows
- Simulated _render_line's 200-byte budget against live (slug, title, hook) lengths to count hooks that would be clipped: 150/168, 39 of them published
- Measured the harness truncation empirically: cumulative characters through index line 84 = 24907 vs line 85 = 25113 against the observed 24.4 KiB limit, cross-checked against my own injected context, which ends at line 84
- Searched for any scheduler or consumer: `systemctl --user list-timers --all`, `systemctl --user list-unit-files`, `crontab -l`, grep over ~/.config/systemd, grep for claude_memory_state/_versions across all Python outside the router/tests/alembic, grep for 'claude-memories' across app/ components/ lib/
- Searched for alerting: grep for claude_memory across all .yml/.yaml/.json including deploy/prometheus/orchestration_alerts.rules.yml, deploy/observability/prometheus/alerts.yml, configs/alertmanager.yml
- Checked recoverability: /home/user/omnisight-prod/data/backups listing (daily .dump.gpg through 2026-08-01, 3.1 MB), tail of /home/user/work/sora/logs/backup/prod-backup.log, systemctl --user show for omnisight-prod-backup.service and omnisight-backup-freshness.service, /home/user/.claude/backups contents, scripts/backup_dlp_scan.py DIGEST_REVIEWED_COLUMNS
- Read the project's own record for stated-vs-actual comparison: docs/operations/claude-memory-index-rendering.md, docs/design/2026-07-22-leg3-claude-memory-unification-design.md, and the memory topic file project_leg3_claude_memory_unification.md
- Disproved one hypothesis by hash: the 2026-07-23 pin 195f1ab8… is not the bare-header 'empty render' output (6435a895…), so a non-empty 29-line index really was written somewhere

**Could not check:**
- Whether the harness surfaces its truncation WARNING anywhere outside the model's own context. The CLI is a Windows binary reached over WSL interop (/home/user/.nvm/.../@anthropic-ai/claude-code/bin/claude.exe); a filesystem search for the warning string timed out at 2 minutes and I did not disassemble the binary. I can only state there is no log file, no metric and no ~/.claude MEMORY.md snapshot.
- Whether omnisight_claude_memory_drift is scraped/displayed at all — curl http://127.0.0.1:8000/metrics inside omnisight-productizer-backend-a-1 returned 404, so I could not confirm the exposition path. The 'no writer, no alert rule' part is solid; 'a dashboard shows a reassuring 0' is inference I did not verify.
- Live behaviour of regen. GET /claude-memories/export INSERTs a ledger row, so calling it (even --dry-run, which still exports) would have been a write; all POSTs were out of bounds. Every regen claim is derived from reading the shipped code plus a locally reproduced drift computation, not from an executed run.
- Which store the 2026-07-23 14:50 regeneration wrote to. The ledger has no store-path field and the operator's notes support both readings (scratch store vs real store). If it did hit the real store, MEMORY.md was restored and .memory.pin deleted by some untracked step; I found no artifact either way.
- Whether claude_memory_* rows are actually present inside the encrypted nightly dumps — decrypting requires the backup passphrase, which I did not touch. Inclusion is inferred from the dump being a whole-database pg_dump of `omnisight`.
- The cause of omnisight-prod-backup.service exiting 2 (ActiveState=failed) on 2026-08-01 even though its log shows the dump completed, DLP-passed and encrypted (3.1 MB). Out of this dimension; flagged only because that dump is the sole remaining copy of claude_memory_state.hook and off-site upload is disabled (OMNISIGHT_BACKUP_S3_URI unset).
- Whether any JIRA ticket exists for SP-3b (the hook write-path gap). I did not call the JIRA API; the only in-repo trace is one line in docs/operations/claude-memory-index-rendering.md.

### [HIGH] The publish state machine governs nothing that is actually injected — 43 of 84 live index entries are quarantined and 1 is unknown to the DB

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** Injection surface is the file, not the DB: /home/user/.claude/projects/-home-user-work-sora-OmniSight-Productizer/memory/MEMORY.md, 51476 B, 174 index lines, still hand-curated format (head -c 600 shows entries directly, no '# MEMORY.md — governed index (regenerated…)' header that claude_memory_regen.py:191 writes). `ls .memory.pin` -> No such file, so regen has never written this store. Cross-mapping the first 84 lines (the part the harness actually loads, see separate finding) against `SELECT s.slug, s.state ... FROM claude_memory_state` on omnisight-pg-primary: Counter({'quarantined': 43, 'published': 40, 'ABSENT_FROM_DB': 1}); the absent one is project_failed_units_remediation_2026_07_25 at index line 2. DB totals: published 40, quarantined 137. All 180 topic bodies are also world-readable on disk regardless of state, so the γ-1 publish lint never gates body consumption either.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: 'every landing is QUARANTINED; publication is the γ-1 human gate' (routers/claude_memories.py:4-6) means unpublished memories do not reach Claude. Actual: publication has zero effect on what reaches Claude — the harness loads MEMORY.md verbatim, and 52% of the entries it loads have never been through the human gate.
- **impact:** The entire γ-0/γ-1 governance chain is currently decorative with respect to its stated purpose. An operator reading the JIRA/ticket record ('γ-0..γ-3 MERGED, publish gate live') would conclude Claude's context is human-governed; it is not, and will not be until regen runs against the real store — which it has not, and cannot today (see the shrink-floor finding).

### [HIGH] Two memories are permanently un-ingestable (uppercase slug), still sit in the injected index, and a regeneration would delete them with no counter, no log line and no refusal

- **category:** data-integrity  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** backend/routers/claude_memories.py:44 `_SLUG_RE = re.compile(r"^[a-z0-9_]{1,120}$")`, enforced at :79 -> reasons.append('bad_slug'). Positive+negative control run over the live store: of 180 topic files exactly two stems fail that regex — ['project_case5_epicB_twoway', 'project_s12g_A_v2_runtime_defense'] — and exactly those two are the only pre-existing files missing from the 177 claude_memory_state rows (`ON DISK NOT IN DB` = those two plus project_failed_units_remediation_2026_07_25, which was created 2026-07-29 after the ingest). Both files predate the 2026-07-23 ingest (mtimes 2026-06-27 and 2026-05-27). Both are still present in MEMORY.md (index lines in the 85-174 range). The operator's own note records the cause and the fix that was never made: project_leg3_claude_memory_unification.md:33 'Ingest 176/178 (2 bad_slug = uppercase filenames; follow-up: widen `_SLUG_RE`, don't rename files)' — develop tip (gerrit-sora/develop == working tree for these files, `git diff --stat` empty) still has the unchanged regex.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: every topic file is represented in the substrate, and anything that cannot be is surfaced. Actual: two are structurally excluded forever, and regen's three loggers (`⚠ DRIFTED`, `budget drop`, the kept/dropped line count) all operate on the export — a slug that was never ingested is in none of them, so it is removed from the regenerated index with literally no output.
- **impact:** On the first real regeneration these two memories vanish from Claude's index silently and permanently. The failure is self-concealing: the more files that violate the regex, the more silent deletions, and the only guard that could notice (the shrink ratchet) attributes the shortfall to expected low publish coverage.

### [HIGH] The ingest CLI's anti-hollow drift check counts rejections as accounted, so a run that drops files exits 0

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** scripts/claude_memory_ingest.py main(): `counts = post_batches(...)` where post_batches builds counts from `r['action']` including the literal 'rejected' branch; then `accounted = sum(counts.values())` and `if accounted != len(items): ... return 1`. Because rejected items are counted, accounted == len(items) whenever the server rejects rather than errors, so the function returns 0. Corroborated by production arithmetic: the store held 179 topic files at ingest time (180 today minus project_failed_units_remediation_2026_07_25, created 2026-07-29), the two bad_slug files were rejected, and claude_memory_state holds exactly 177 rows — and the run is recorded as DONE/success in the project memory. Rejections leave no DB trace at all: no claude_memory_versions row, no claude_memory_transition_events row (`SELECT to_state, count(*)` returns only published 40 / quarantined 179).
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: the block comment calls this an 'Anti-hollow drift check: files vs accounted actions' — i.e. a guard that fails the run when files do not become rows. Actual: the guard can only catch a transport/pagination bug; it is blind to exactly the failure mode it is named for, and the only signal is a stderr line in a manually-invoked CLI.
- **impact:** An ingest that silently drops content reports success to its caller and to CI. This is the same shape as the original 'silent-hollow compound' the Phase-R repair was chartered to eliminate, reintroduced in the repair's own verification code.

### [HIGH] omnisight_claude_memory_drift — the gauge whose help string says 'set by the ingest CLI' — has no writer anywhere in the repo

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** backend/metrics.py:1245-1250 declares `Gauge('omnisight_claude_memory_drift', 'γ-0 (leg-3) anti-hollow: |store files| - |claude_memory_state rows| (nonzero after a full ingest = drift; set by the ingest CLI)')`. `grep -rn 'drift_gauge' --include=*.py .` returns exactly two lines: the definition at :1245 and the `_NoOp` fallback at :1407. There is no `.set(` call, and scripts/claude_memory_ingest.py does not import backend.metrics at all (its complete import list is argparse/json/os/re/sys/urllib.request/pathlib). Real drift right now is 3 (180 files vs 177 rows). No alert rule references it: `grep -rn claude_memory --include=*.yml --include=*.yaml .` returns nothing across deploy/prometheus/orchestration_alerts.rules.yml, deploy/observability/prometheus/alerts.yml and configs/alertmanager.yml.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: a gauge that goes non-zero when the file store and the substrate disagree, per its own help text. Actual: a declared-but-never-written metric. A prometheus Gauge with no writer exports the value 0, i.e. it actively asserts 'no drift' while drift is 3.
- **impact:** The one telemetry hook designed to make leg-3 hollowness visible is itself hollow, and it fails in the worst direction — a dashboard or alert built on it reports health. Caveat: I could not confirm the metric is scraped at all (curl http://127.0.0.1:8000/metrics inside omnisight-productizer-backend-a-1 returned 404), so the operational claim I can stand behind is 'no writer, no alert rule', not 'a dashboard shows 0'.

### [HIGH] `hook` — the only free-text field of the injected index — is outside the publish binding and is rewritten on already-published rows by the service ingest path

- **category:** security  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** backend/routers/claude_memories.py:101-108: `now_touch` = "UPDATE claude_memory_state SET last_seen_at = now(), rank = COALESCE($3, rank), hook = COALESCE($4, hook), updated_at = now() ..." is executed on the same-sha path and returns action 'unchanged' — no lint, no new version row, no transition event. The publish endpoint binds only (version_id, body_sha256) (:296-299), and `\d claude_memory_versions` on prod confirms the version ledger has no hook or rank column (id, project_id, slug, revision, title, description, mem_type, body, body_sha256, links, origin_session_id, created_by, ingested_at). The regenerator's file-binding re-hashes bodies only (claude_memory_regen.py `_local_body_sha`). The 0279 publish-gate trigger only checks `NEW.state='published' -> published_version_id NOT NULL AND lint->'blocking' = '[]'`, so a hook-only UPDATE on a published row passes it. Rendered MEMORY.md lines contain exactly title + slug + hook, so hook is ~150 bytes of unreviewed prose per line in every future session's system context. Known and open: docs/operations/claude-memory-index-rendering.md:77-79 'The write path that lets `hook` be rewritten on a published row without review is a separate defect, tracked as … (SP-3b)'; `grep -rn SP-3b` over the whole repo returns that single doc line — no code, no test, no ticket artifact.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected (design doc line 31): 'γ-2 gives a BACKEND COMPROMISE authorship of SELECTION + RANKING + index-line text … guardrail-lesson suppression is the sneak' — i.e. authoring index text is supposed to require compromising the backend. Actual: the ordinary ingest API writes index-line text onto published rows, so any holder of a write-capable bearer authors it without compromising anything.
- **impact:** Once the regen switch is flipped, the publish gate protects the one field that is never injected (body) and leaves unprotected the one that always is (hook). Combined with the wildcard-key finding below, this is an unreviewed write channel into the system prompt of every future Claude session in this project.

### [HIGH] Three enabled, never-expiring `["*"]`-scoped API keys can write the Claude-memory corpus, defeating the router's stated identity model

- **category:** security  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** `SELECT id, name, scopes, enabled, expires_at FROM api_keys` on omnisight-pg-primary: ak-bbaa97c2f7 'claude-memory-leg3' ["/claude-memories"] (correct); ak-e81a057aa7 'runner-project-state-f7' ["*"]; ak-00c9b3dcf0 'gerrit-webhook (OP-708)' ["*"]; ak-legacy-31be6f6c8763 'legacy-bearer' ["*"] — all enabled=1, expires_at NULL. backend/api_keys.py:68-70 `def scope_allows(self, endpoint): if "*" in self.scopes: return True`, and the K6 middleware (backend/main.py:928-948) is the only gate on these paths — `grep -rn 'claude-memories' backend/ | grep -v routers/claude_memories.py | grep -v tests` returns nothing, so there is no additional scope check. `\d api_keys` shows the scopes column DEFAULT is `'["*"]'::text`, so wildcard is what minting produces unless overridden.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: routers/claude_memories.py:9-11 'an omni_ api-key bearer minted with scopes=["claude-memories"] (NEVER ["*"]) — the K6 middleware pre-gates the path'. Actual: the 'NEVER ["*"]' constraint is a docstring convention with no enforcement, the column default is the opposite of it, and three unrelated live keys — including the runner's, on a code path that self-deploys from a git clone every cycle — satisfy the gate.
- **impact:** Ingest with an arbitrary body and an arbitrary `hook` is reachable by three principals that have nothing to do with leg-3. Because `hook` bypasses the publish binding (previous finding), this is a path from a runner-scoped credential to text in every future session's system context.

### [MEDIUM] The first regeneration will overwrite 150 hand-curated hooks with their own truncations — unversioned, unlogged, and irreversible on the live system

- **category:** data-integrity  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** claude_memory_regen.py `_render_line` clips to `_LINE_B = 200` bytes. Simulating that exact budget arithmetic against the live rows (`SELECT s.slug, octet_length(s.hook), octet_length(v.title), s.state ... JOIN head version`): 150 of the 168 rows that have a hook would be clipped, 39 of them published. Worst cases: project_customer_delivery_capability_audit_2026_05_27 hook 1016 B into a 57 B budget (94% loss), project_leg2_worker_loop_audit 508 B -> 68 B, project_u6_kernel_handoff_and_roadmap 476 B -> 53 B. The round trip that makes it permanent is inside regen itself: step 1 (claude_memory_regen.py:157-166) fires `post_batches(collect_items(store))` whenever the local pin mismatches — which is the normal case, since the harness writes MEMORY.md between runs — and `collect_items` harvests hooks straight out of MEMORY.md, which regen has just overwritten with clipped text; `now_touch` then writes the clipped hook back over claude_memory_state.hook. `claude_memory_versions` has no hook column, and MEMORY.md is overwritten in place, so both copies are gone in one cycle. Not yet triggered: `SELECT count(*) FROM claude_memory_state WHERE hook LIKE '%…'` = 0; max hook 1016 B, avg 192 B.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: the budget is described as 'truncate the hook at a word boundary' — a rendering concern affecting the emitted file. Actual: the truncation is fed back into the store by regen's own reconcile step and overwrites the source of truth, because hook lives only in the mutable state table with no history and no transition event.
- **impact:** A year of hand-curated hook text (the operator's own stated reason the harvest exists — 'the hook differs from the description; a year of curation is preserved') is destroyed by the tool built to preserve it, on its first production run. Recovery would depend on the nightly local pg dump (off-site upload is disabled: prod-backup.log '[WARN] off-site immutable backup skipped (OMNISIGHT_BACKUP_S3_URI unset)'), and nothing would tell anyone to go looking.

### [MEDIUM] DB↔file divergence is detected nowhere outside a regen run, is growing, and will wedge regen permanently in roughly a week

- **category:** data-integrity  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** Recomputed each published row's body sha with the shipped code path (imported `_parse_frontmatter` from scripts/claude_memory_ingest.py, same `body if body.strip() else raw` rule as `_local_body_sha`): 34 of 40 published rows reproduce the server sha exactly (positive control — the harness works), 6 do not: project_case5_b5a_onboard_bringup, project_conference_appliance_setup, project_leg2_worker_loop_audit, project_leg3_claude_memory_unification, project_u4_handoff_working_mode_and_queue, project_u6_kernel_handoff_and_roadmap. It was 3 on 2026-07-26 (docs/operations/claude-memory-index-rendering.md: 'rendering the live prod export … 37 lines' against a 40-row export). File mtimes show the cause: 8 topic files edited on 2026-07-25/27/28/29, after the 2026-07-23 ingest. No scheduler re-syncs: `systemctl --user list-timers --all | grep -iE 'memory|claude'` empty, `crontab -l` empty, `grep -rn claude_memory_ingest ~/.config/systemd` empty. Regen's wholesale-drift HARD-REFUSE fires at `len(drifted) > max(3, len(items)//4)` = >10 for a 40-row export. Publish coverage has been frozen since the day it started: `SELECT to_state, min(at), max(at) FROM claude_memory_transition_events` shows all 40 publishes inside 2026-07-23 14:29-15:08 and nothing since.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: 'ingest-before-regenerate (never clobber a file whose sha changed since last ingest)' — a maintained two-way sync. Actual: ingest is fully manual with no timer, drift accrues at ~0.5 published rows/day, and the only consumer that notices is a regen run that has not happened.
- **impact:** Each drifted row is silently dropped from the regenerated index until a human re-publishes it. At the observed rate the drift count passes the hard-refuse threshold (11) in about a week, at which point regen refuses entirely — and the only remedy, re-publishing, is a hand-driven browser task with no UI (`grep -rln 'claude-memories' app/ components/ lib/` returns nothing) capped at 30/h.

### [MEDIUM] --allow-shrink waives the only guard that would surface the drift exclusion, and the ratchet baseline erodes on every run

- **category:** design  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** claude_memory_regen.py:225-233: `previous = _count_index_lines(idx_path)` reads the MEMORY.md currently on disk; refuse if `len(kept) < previous * 0.5 and not allow_shrink`. Today: export 40, bound 34, previous 174 -> 34 < 87 -> refuse (exit 3). docs/operations/claude-memory-index-rendering.md instructs the operator through it: 'A large shrink is not itself a bug … without the flag it refuses with `would shrink the index from 174 to 37 entries` … with `--allow-shrink` it proceeds.' The same flag also waives the only floor that would have flagged the 6 drifted-out published entries, which are otherwise reported on stderr only. Because the baseline is the file the previous run wrote, a successful 34-line write makes the next run's floor 17, then 8 — the ratchet permits a 50% loss per run indefinitely. If MEMORY.md is absent, `_count_index_lines` returns 0 and `if previous and ...` short-circuits, disabling the ratchet entirely.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: a ratchet that 'encodes the standing hold-the-regen-switch decision into the tool instead of relying on someone remembering it'. Actual: it encodes a decision whose documented resolution is to pass the override, and passing the override also disables detection of an unrelated, genuinely anomalous loss.
- **impact:** The expected shrink (low publish coverage) and the unexpected loss (drifted-out published rows, never-ingested slugs) are indistinguishable at the point of decision, and the runbook trains the operator to approve both with one flag.

### [MEDIUM] The 'server pin hard-refuse on ledger self-inconsistency' defence is not implemented on either side

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** claude_memory_regen.py module docstring lines 15-18: 'SERVER PIN (export ledger) defends against an ~/.claude attacker … HARD-REFUSE only when the server ledger is self-inconsistent with what it previously pinned.' `grep -rn previous_export_sha256 --include=*.py .` returns exactly: routers/claude_memories.py:428 (where it is emitted) and three lines in backend/tests/test_gamma2_regen.py (stub fixtures) — the regenerator never reads the field. The server side is also degenerate: routers/claude_memories.py:408-412 selects `export_sha256 ... ORDER BY at DESC LIMIT 1` over a table that also stores un-pinned export rows with export_sha256 NULL; `SELECT id, export_sha256, requested_by, at FROM claude_memory_exports ORDER BY at` shows 9 rows of which only one (2026-07-23 14:50:04, requested_by 'pin:…') carries a sha, and the newest row (2026-07-26 08:55:36) is NULL — so the field would be NULL today even if a client read it.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: three-legged integrity posture (client file-binding, local race-guard pin, server pin ledger), with the server leg able to hard-refuse. Actual: two legs work (file-binding verified against the live corpus, local pin logic present); the third emits a value nobody consumes, computed in a way that makes it NULL in the common case.
- **impact:** The threat the server pin was designed for — an attacker who can write ~/.claude, i.e. exactly the scenario where the local pin is untrustworthy — has no working defence, while the design record lists it as covered.

### [MEDIUM] The release-pinned prod checkout carries the pre-OP-2729 regenerator with none of the three safety floors

- **category:** mismatch-with-intent  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** /home/user/omnisight-prod/scripts/claude_memory_regen.py is 6881 B (mtime 2026-07-22 22:41) and `grep -c _MIN_RETAIN_RATIO` = 0; reading it confirms the old `_render_line` (clips `hook` only — a long title blows the 200 B cap; no `_one_line`, so a newline in hook or title manufactures a second well-formed index entry), no empty-render floor, no shrink ratchet, and `idx_path.write_text(...)` executed before any of them could exist. The same stale copy is baked into the running prod image (`docker exec omnisight-productizer-backend-a-1 ls -la /app/scripts/claude_memory_regen.py` -> 6881 B, 2026-07-22). The fixed 11675 B version exists only in the working tree /home/user/work/sora/OmniSight-betaF (identical to gerrit-sora/develop — `git diff --stat gerrit-sora/develop HEAD` over the leg-3 files is empty). /home/user/sora-bridge and /home/user/work/sora/OmniSight-Productizer have no copy at all.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: regen is a host-side CLI, so 'merged to develop == available'. Actual: it is run from whichever checkout the operator is standing in, and /home/user/omnisight-prod — the checkout this project's own deploy discipline directs operators to for prod operations — holds the version whose defects OP-2729 was filed to fix.
- **impact:** The most likely invocation path is the unsafe one. The old renderer's newline defect is the index-injection bug documented in docs/operations/claude-memory-index-rendering.md as reproduced-then-fixed; it is unfixed on the path an operator would actually use.

### [MEDIUM] Harness truncation drops 52% of the index at exactly 24.4 KiB of characters, with no operator-visible signal

- **category:** design  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** MEMORY.md = 51476 bytes / 50071 characters / 174 index lines. The harness reported '48.9KB (limit: 24.4KB)' — 50071/1024 = 48.9, so it measures CHARACTERS. Cumulative characters through index line 84 = 24907 (< 24.4 KiB = 24985.6); through line 85 = 25113 (>). My own injected system context ends at line 84, `- [Staging gate producer gap](project_staging_gate_gap.md)`, and does not contain line 85 (project_force_promote_override) or anything after — the arithmetic and the observed cut agree exactly. So the mechanism is: top-down, hard cut at the character budget, 90 of 174 entries never load. Dropped entries include project_next_session_tasks, whose hook reads '▶▶ NEXT SESSION: 5 planned tasks (start here)'. The only signal is a WARNING line inside the model's own context; no log file, no metric, and `/home/user/.claude/backups` contains only .claude.json snapshots.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected (design doc line 6 / memory line 16): 'recall is being truncated TODAY' with an estimate of ~120/171 entries surviving. Actual: 84/174 survive — the loss is 90 entries, not ~51. Nobody had measured it; the design's characterisation of the bug it exists to fix understates it by ~75%.
- **impact:** Half the memory corpus is inert. Because the cut is top-down over a hand-ordered file it happens to preserve the starred entries, but that is a property of the current hand curation, not of any mechanism. Separately: the harness budget counts characters while regen's `_FILE_B` counts 24,000 UTF-8 bytes — different quantities, neither derived from the other. For this emoji/CJK-heavy corpus (51476 B / 50071 chars) the byte budget is the tighter one, so the direction is currently safe, but that is luck rather than design.

### [LOW] Ingest reports action 'unchanged' while mutating the row's rank and hook

- **category:** bug  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** backend/routers/claude_memories.py:106-108 — the same-sha branch executes `now_touch` (which sets rank and hook via COALESCE) and then returns `{'action': 'unchanged'}`. main.py:180-181 increments `claude_memory_ingest_total{action='unchanged'}` for it. There is no transition event and no version row for the mutation.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: 'same body_sha256 -> unchanged (touch last_seen_at only)' (router docstring line 18). Actual: it also rewrites rank and hook — the two fields that determine index ordering and index text.
- **impact:** The per-action counters cannot distinguish 'nothing happened' from 'the injected index text was rewritten', so the metric that would be the natural place to notice hook churn is structurally unable to show it. The docstring is also wrong in a way that would mislead the next reader auditing this path.

### [LOW] The 30/h publish rate cap is per-process in-memory, so prod's two replicas give 60/h and any restart resets it

- **category:** bug  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** backend/routers/claude_memories.py:187-199 — `_PUBLISH_CAP_PER_HOUR = 30` with `_publish_window: list[float] = []` at module scope; `_rate_ok()` filters and appends to that in-process list. `docker ps` shows omnisight-productizer-backend-a-1 and omnisight-productizer-backend-b-1 both Up and healthy behind the same Caddy.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: a 30/h cap on index-affecting human publishes (the project memory plans the publish wave around it: '30/h rate cap -> 177 items ≈ 6h of gated review spread over days'). Actual: ~60/h across two replicas, resettable by a restart.
- **impact:** Low in practice — publishing is human-gated by assert_human_principal — but the cap is a stated defence-in-depth control against a compromised admin session, and it delivers roughly half the protection its constant advertises.

### [INFO] GET /claude-memories/export performs an INSERT, and the ledger records no store path — a rehearsal and a production write are indistinguishable after the fact

- **category:** design  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** backend/routers/claude_memories.py:418-425 — the GET handler INSERTs a claude_memory_exports row. ExportPinBody carries only export_id and export_sha256 (:433-437), and `\d claude_memory_exports` has no store-path column. Consequence in the live ledger: the 2026-07-23 14:50:04 export (manifest n=31) plus its pin (export_sha256 195f1ab8b4f7667e518d85b00a4725bae28c617f349c2adffe7df3697b91df77) prove that a pre-floor regenerator wrote and pinned a 29-line MEMORY.md, but not to which store. The operator's own memory records both readings — project_leg3_claude_memory_unification.md:34 'γ-2 regen PROVEN on scratch store (/tmp/claude-mem-scratch)' and :39 'real regen PROVEN vs prod (29 lines/5.7KB + dual pin)'. `find /home/user -name .memory.pin` returns nothing and MEMORY.md is still the 174-line hand file, which is consistent with a scratch-store rehearsal — I could not establish it either way. I also disproved the benign 'empty render' variant: sha256 of the bare-header content is 6435a895… not 195f1ab8…, so a non-empty 29-line index was written somewhere.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: an append-only server pin ledger that establishes what the regenerator wrote. Actual: it establishes a content hash with no binding to the artifact's location, so it cannot answer the one forensic question it exists for.
- **impact:** Forensics on any past or future index-destruction event are impossible from the ledger alone. Also a non-idempotent GET: any wildcard-scoped key can grow the table, and each un-pinned export makes previous_export_sha256 NULL for the next caller.

## Architectural coherence / silo drift of the "3D memory + U6 three-principals, one governed kernel" memory system

**Auditor summary:** The north star ("ONE governed memory, THREE principals, ONE shared kernel") has not been achieved: I counted at least 25 distinct live memory stores across 4 backing technologies, and the three legs neither share a store, an environment, a capability vocabulary, nor an enforcement point — each has its own ad-hoc gate, and the shared kernel has made ZERO decisions in production in the 10 days both replicas have been up. The healthiest subsystem (lessons) was partially unified into the structural axis as the 2026-07-07 audit recommended, but in doing so it forked into 5+ divergent physical corpora, and the Phase-S "anti-hollow" cure written for exactly this failure mode was itself implemented hollow — its one loaded alert queries a metric that no code ever emits and has been firing uselessly for 23 days.

**Checks performed:**
- Read the stated design intent: docs/adr/ADR-0015 (+ 3 amendments), docs/design/2026-07-11-phase-u6-...-v2.md (FROZEN v3), docs/design/tiered-memory-architecture.md, docs/sop/g-a-v0-kernel-spec.md, docs/sop/architecture-anti-patterns.md #15
- Enumerated every memory-ish table in prod (pg-primary/omnisight) and staging (omnisight-staging-postgres-1/omnisight_staging) via information_schema + query_to_xml row counts; proved the harness works by getting non-zero counts and max() timestamps back from the same query for 12 of 25 tables
- Ran a dynamic last-write scan (auto-detected timestamp column per table) on both prod and staging
- Read backend/agents/authorization_kernel.py, action_guard.py (mode matrix + guard_tool_dispatch), tool_registry KNOWN_FAMILIES
- Executed python inside omnisight-productizer-backend-a-1 to read the LIVE _MODE_MATRIX and OMNISIGHT_ACTION_GUARD_MODE
- Scraped the live /api/v1/metrics exposition on both prod replicas and queried prod Prometheus (label __name__ values, /api/v1/query, /api/v1/rules, /api/v1/alerts)
- Read /etc/prometheus/prometheus.yml rule_files and /etc/prometheus/obs-rules/project_state_health.yml inside the prometheus container
- Read /proc/<pid>/environ of the live auto-runner-jira.py processes repeatedly (cycle ~40s) to get per-instance flag values for claude-1/2, codex-1/2, default
- docker inspect env of prod backend a/b and staging backend a; docker inspect Mounts for backend and cognee containers
- Traced guard call sites (grep guard_tool_dispatch), runner capability enforcement (_require_runner_capability / require_capability), and leg-3 router gates (assert_human_principal)
- Traced every runner prompt-injection block in auto-runner-jira.py (_build_project_state_block, _build_lesson_recall_block, _build_antipattern_block, _build_character_enrichment_block, _render_issue_links_block, _fetch_learned_items_block)
- Read backend/agents/memory_writeback.py fan-out (_do_memory_tool / _do_incident / _do_cognee / _default_cognee_emitter) and classify_lesson
- find on /var/omnisight/memory (host) and inside the prod backend container
- Counted docs/sop/lessons/*.md in 6 locations (develop tip, prod image, staging image, runner main tree, sora-bridge, omnisight-prod)
- Inspected ~/.claude/projects/.../memory/ (181 topic files + MEMORY.md), counted index lines, checked for .memory.pin
- Checked cognee data freshness in both cognee data roots and neo4j /data; enumerated systemd user+system timers and crontab for cognee/graphiti/ingest jobs
- Grepped for readers/writers of episodic_memory, chat_session_summaries, auto_distilled_skills, learned_item_loader, rag_prefetch

**Could not check:**
- Could not authenticate to neo4j (NEO4J_AUTH_PW not in the container env under that name) — I could not run a node count, only inspect /data on disk (2.2 MB, databases dir untouched since the 2026-06-20 container start).
- Could not issue an authenticated GET to /api/v1/project-state (would need a credential; task forbids printing/using one), so the axis payload shape is inferred from code + the Prometheus axis counters rather than observed end-to-end.
- Could not read /var/lib/docker/volumes/... on the host (sudo requires a password); volume contents were read from inside containers instead.
- Could not determine WHY runner_incidents stopped on 2026-07-20 (writer break vs genuinely zero failures) — the fleet has been idle ('no candidate passed pre-pickup checks' every cycle), so both explanations fit. I report the fact, not the cause.
- gemini-1 and grok-1 runner instances never surfaced a readable /proc environ during my sampling window, so their flag matrix is unverified (claude-1/2, codex-1/2, default were all read successfully and were identical).
- I did not verify whether the docs.sora-dev.app rendered lessons site is a 6th corpus copy in content terms — only that omnisight-docs-build.timer runs daily and CLAUDE.md points readers there.

### [HIGH] The one loaded memory alert queries a metric that no code ever emits — firing uselessly for 23 days (Pattern-15 cure re-instantiates Pattern-15)

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** Alert expr: `absent(rate(omnisight_project_state_calls_total[30m]))` at deploy/observability/prometheus/project_state_health.yml:38 and in the loaded copy /etc/prometheus/obs-rules/project_state_health.yml. Prometheus /api/v1/alerts: `ProjectStateNoTraffic firing 2026-07-09T19:21:11Z`. Query `omnisight_project_state_calls_total` returns `{"result":[]}`. `grep -rn project_state_calls backend/ --include=*.py | grep -v tests` returns NOTHING — the metric is never defined in backend/metrics.py (which defines only omnisight_project_state_axis_total:975 and omnisight_project_state_structural_half_total:981). The only other references are deploy/observability/grafana/project_state_health.json:245 (a dead panel) and backend/tests/test_project_state_alert_rules.py:148/213, which assert only that the *string* appears in the YAML.
- **expected vs actual:** ADR-0015 OP-2561 amendment: 'required metric-family absence is itself an alert condition... An SLO or dashboard that queries a non-emitted project-state metric is hollow coverage and does not satisfy this ADR.' Actual: the alert built to satisfy that clause queries a non-emitted metric, so it can never clear, can never distinguish 'no traffic' from 'metric renamed/removed', and its test only string-matches the YAML.
- **impact:** The single alert covering the 3-axis memory system has been in a permanent firing state for 23 days and carries zero information. Anti-patterns cookbook #15 literally lists this symptom ('an audit finds that the alert was querying a metric that was never emitted', docs/sop/architecture-anti-patterns.md:492-560) — the cure written for the incident contains the defect it names. Combined with severity:info and the known absence of an alert delivery path on this host, nobody will ever see it.

### [HIGH] The 'one shared kernel' has made ZERO authorization decisions in production and is universally in shadow mode

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** `docker exec omnisight-productizer-backend-a-1 python -c "..."` → `GUARD_MODE_ENV= None`, `MATRIX= {}`. backend/agents/action_guard.py:113 `_load_mode_matrix()` returns {} on empty env; :146 `resolve_mode` falls through to `_MODE_MATRIX.get((adapter,family),'shadow')`; :528 `proceed = mode != "enforce"` — so every `requires_grant` verdict proceeds. Live exposition on both replicas (`curl /api/v1/metrics`) shows the HELP/TYPE lines for omnisight_action_guard_decision_total / _fail_closed_total / _shadow_classification_total but ZERO label series; Prometheus query returns `{"result":[]}` for all three. Both replicas started 2026-07-22 (10 days). Control: the same scrape returns `omnisight_project_state_axis_total{...} 3.0` lines, so the scrape works.
- **expected vs actual:** North star / U6 v2 §2.A: 'memory can never authorize a side effect... Enforced in the action/tool-dispatch layer, NOT in the prompt' and INV-5 'enforcement lives in code'. Actual: the kernel's ALLOW-path invariant holds (mutating never returns allow), but the guard that consumes the verdict is in all-shadow, so a mutating operation still executes; and in 10 days of production the guard was never invoked at all, so even the shadow telemetry is empty.
- **impact:** The single load-bearing safety property of the whole memory architecture has never been exercised in production. There is no evidence — positive or negative — that the kernel works in prod, and no alert on the metric family being series-less. The kernel is a correctly-built component with zero production coverage.

### [HIGH] Each leg has its own ad-hoc gate with a different, unmapped capability vocabulary — there is no shared kernel in practice

- **category:** design  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** Leg-1 (Sora chat/specialist/a2a): backend/agents/action_guard.py ADAPTER_NAMESPACES={chat,specialist,a2a,runner_sdk}, 14 families from tool_registry (`artifact_write, code_write, dangerous_propose, delegation, deploy, external_comms, gerrit_write, memory_write, read_only, shell_exec, skill_exec, task_write, ticket_write, vcs_write`, read live out of the prod container). Leg-2 (the actual production runner): `ps aux` shows the fleet runs auto-runner-jira.py (via scripts/runner-wrapper/run-ephemeral.sh), which contains NO reference to action_guard/guard_tool_dispatch (grep returns nothing); its gate is backend/agents/capability_matrix.py:47 CAPABILITIES = {code_edit, run_tests, run_lint, gerrit_push, jira_update, mcp_search, memory_recall, run_migration, deploy_action, run_outcomes_grader}. `guard_tool_dispatch` callers are only nodes.py:1223/1368/2028 and tool_dispatcher.py:250 (runner_sdk), and runner_sdk's ToolDispatcher is bound only from auto-runner-sdk.py / scripts/run_s1_via_anthropic_sdk.py — neither of which is running. Leg-3 (Claude memory): backend/routers/claude_memories.py uses `assert_human_principal` (lines 225/279/354/482) — a third mechanism, imported from learned_item_approval, not the kernel.
- **expected vs actual:** North star: 'each scoped, all sharing ONE action-capability kernel'. Actual: three independent gates with overlapping-but-unmapped vocabularies (code_edit↔code_write, gerrit_push↔gerrit_write/vcs_write, jira_update↔ticket_write, deploy_action↔deploy, memory_recall↔memory_write). No translation layer exists between them.
- **impact:** The two gates can give contradictory answers for the same conceptual operation: the kernel classifies every memory read as `read_only ⇒ allow` unconditionally, while the runner matrix can withhold `memory_recall` for a (type, area, tier) triple. A future change to one gate silently diverges from the other. 'One governed kernel' is a documentation claim, not a code fact.

### [HIGH] memory_writeback reports the Cognee store as successfully written when its emitter is a log.debug no-op, and the nightly rebuild it delegates to does not exist

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** backend/agents/memory_writeback.py:572-579 `_default_cognee_emitter(ticket_key)` body is a single `log.debug("memory_writeback.cognee_tickle ticket=%s", ticket_key)`. :488-495 `_do_cognee` calls the emitter and `return True` unconditionally, so `WritebackResult.cognee_tickled=True` and STORE_COGNEE never lands in `stores_failed`. Its docstring (:575-577) justifies this: 'The real subscriber lives in the Cognee ECL pipeline (nightly rebuild already re-ingests by ticket key)'. `systemctl --user list-timers --all` (24 timers) and `systemctl list-timers --all` (system) contain NO cognee/graphiti/ingest timer; `crontab -l` has one unrelated omnisight-fate backup entry. Cognee KG data on disk: /app/.cognee_system/databases last modified 2026-05-12 (container omnisight-cognee, up 6 weeks).
- **expected vs actual:** memory_writeback.py:1-19 promises a three-store fan-out ('Cognee KG (C3) — fire-and-forget tickle so the ticket + lesson are re-indexed on next ingest pass'). Actual: one of three stores is a logging statement that always reports success, and the fallback it names has no scheduler.
- **impact:** This is the 2026-07-07 'silent-hollow compound' pattern still live, one store over: the writeback's own success accounting says 3/3 stores OK when only runner_incidents actually persists. Any operator or dashboard reading stores_failed sees a clean fan-out. It is the exact shape of the 'graphiti dispatcher never assigned' defect the Phase R repair was supposed to eliminate.

### [HIGH] leg-3's governed store is not the artifact that is actually read: MEMORY.md carries 174 ungoverned entries against 40 published rows, and the regenerator has never run

- **category:** mismatch-with-intent  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** ~/.claude/projects/-home-user-work-sora-OmniSight-Productizer/memory/MEMORY.md is 51,476 bytes with 174 index entries (`grep -cE '^- \[.*\]\(.*\.md\)'`) and 181 topic .md files on disk. Prod claude_memory_state: 40 published, 137 quarantined. scripts/claude_memory_regen.py:1-30 states its purpose: 'Regenerates the harness-injected index from PUBLISHED rows only. The live bug this fixes: MEMORY.md is ~50KB against the harness's 24.4KB budget — recall is truncated BLINDLY today', with `_FILE_B = 24_000`. The local race-guard `.memory.pin` does NOT exist in the memory dir (`ls` → No such file or directory), and staging's copy of the same corpus has a completely different publish state (3 published / 173 quarantined vs prod's 40/137).
- **expected vs actual:** leg-3's stated payoff is moving Claude's file store onto the governed claude_memory_* substrate so publication is gated. Actual: the gate exists and holds 40 approvals, but the artifact injected into context is still the legacy 51KB file with 174 entries — 134 of which the governed store has NOT published — and it exceeds the loader budget so it is truncated blindly, which is the exact bug the substrate was built to fix.
- **impact:** Leg-3 is a write-mostly shadow: the store with the governance is not the store being read, and the store being read has no governance. The same corpus also exists in two databases with divergent publication state, so 'is this memory published?' has two different answers depending on which environment you ask. (I observed the state, not the reason it is held; the memory index itself records the regen-switch as intentionally HELD, so this is drift-in-waiting rather than an accident — but the artifact-vs-store divergence is real today.)

### [MEDIUM] The runner capability matrix declares 10 capabilities and enforces exactly one at runtime; `memory_recall` is granted 35 times and never checked

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** `grep -n "_require_runner_capability(" auto-runner-jira.py` → definition at :1001 and exactly ONE call site at :4197, whose capability argument is `"gerrit_push"` (:4198). `grep -rn "require_capability(" --include=*.py . | grep -v tests` → only capability_matrix.py:478 (the def) and auto-runner-jira.py:1016 (inside the helper). config/capability_matrix.yaml grants `memory_recall` 35 times, annotated at line 29 as '# backend/agents/project_memory reads'. There is a separate ticket-admission gate (_pre_pickup_capability_ok, :947) that compares required vs resolved caps before pickup.
- **expected vs actual:** capability_matrix.py:1-8 docstring: 'the auto-runner reads config/capability_matrix.yaml at pickup time and explicitly enables only the listed capabilities; the rest are blocked via CapabilityNotPermitted at call sites (gerrit push, jira update, migration runner, etc.)'. Actual: only gerrit_push has a call site. code_edit, run_tests, run_lint, jira_update, mcp_search, memory_recall, run_migration, deploy_action and run_outcomes_grader are computed, granted, label-overridable, and never enforced at runtime.
- **impact:** Leg-2's memory-access control is decorative. A ticket whose matrix entry withholds `memory_recall` still gets every memory injection block; a ticket without `deploy_action` is not stopped from a deploy by this gate. The operator-facing `capability:disable=` escape hatch does nothing for 9 of 10 capabilities.

### [MEDIUM] The Anthropic Memory Tool per-fleet store — one of ADR-0015's five named sources — is completely empty, and the backend has the env var with no volume backing it

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** `find /var/omnisight/memory -type f | wc -l` → **0**. Ten fleet directories exist (claude, claude-1, claude-2, codex, codex-1, codex-2, default, merger, test) with mtimes 2026-05-18 … 2026-07-12; total 40K, all directory overhead. In the prod backend container `ls /var/omnisight/memory` → 'No such file or directory', yet `OMNISIGHT_MEMORY_TOOL_ROOT=/var/omnisight/memory` IS in its env and `docker inspect --format '{{range .Mounts}}'` shows no mount for that path (only /app/data, /app/.artifacts, /app/.sdks, /cognee-data, /etc/omnisight). The runner units DO set the var and the host dir is real, so the runner path is wired; the persistence target is simply always empty.
- **expected vs actual:** ADR-0015: 'Memory Tool remains per-fleet scratchpad storage' as one of five composed sources. OP-1681 (auto-runner-jira.py:2681-2705) exists specifically so 'a classified lesson is *persisted* to disk under /var/omnisight/memory/<fleet_id>/ — not merely id-returned'. Actual: zero files have ever survived there, and the backend's copy of the env var points at a path that does not exist in its filesystem.
- **impact:** A whole principal-facing store in the architecture diagram holds nothing. Because memory_writeback's memory-tool arm fails open by design (a build failure 'degrades to a logged None' per the OP-1681 docstring) and classify_lesson returns None on any non-matching success, the emptiness is indistinguishable from correct operation. The backend env var is vestigial config that would silently write to the container's ephemeral layer if any backend code ever used it.

### [MEDIUM] Two incompatible L1/L2/L3 tier vocabularies are both implemented and live, and the older one's cardinal rule is violated by live code

- **category:** mismatch-with-intent  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** docs/design/tiered-memory-architecture.md (no supersession marker) defines L1=CLAUDE.md/SOP immutable rules, L2=context window + HANDOFF.md, L3=episodic vector store, and states at :40 '**絕對不可**自動注入 prompt' (L3 must NEVER be auto-injected; only via explicit search_past_solutions tool call). docs/design/2026-07-11-phase-u6-...-v2.md §1 defines L1=chat_messages same-session replay, L2=chat_session_summaries, L3=l3_facts per-user semantic facts, and L3 IS auto-injected (§5 read-path placement, OMNISIGHT_SORA_L3_READ=1 in prod env). Both are implemented: backend/agents/project_memory.py is the old-L1 CLAUDE.md loader; backend/intent_memory.py:1-9 says 'persist operator's clarification choices into L3... Storing these in episodic_memory'; backend/memory_decay.py:1 says 'Phase 63-E — episodic memory quality decay'. The old-L3 no-auto-inject rule is violated by backend/rag_prefetch.py:295 `prefetch_for_sandbox_error`, called from backend/agents/nodes.py:1748 on the sandbox-retry path, which injects a `<system_auto_prefetch>` block built from episodic_memory.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: one tier vocabulary for one governed memory. Actual: 'L1', 'L2' and 'L3' each denote two different things depending on which module you read, and the older scheme's single safety rule is broken by a live injection path.
- **impact:** Any cross-leg reasoning, ticket, runbook or alert that says 'L3' is ambiguous. Worse, the U6 v2 design §1 explicitly flagged this exact rag_prefetch read path as in-scope existing surface to remediate ('rag_prefetch.py:186/308 reads that table back into the prompt... This is the exact poisoning channel U4 was built to close, still open on a different table'), but the §11 freeze fold closes only save_solution and names only `search_past_solutions` as the residual read path — rag_prefetch's auto-injection was dropped from the remediation between §1 and §11.

### [MEDIUM] The lessons corpus — the 'healthiest subsystem' the audit said to unify onto — has forked into 5+ divergent physical copies; the structural KG half serves an image-baked snapshot

- **category:** data-integrity  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** Counts of docs/sop/lessons/*.md: develop tip (OmniSight-betaF) **104**; prod backend image /app **101**; staging backend image /app **101**; omnisight-prod pinned checkout **101**; sora-bridge (coordinator's stale tree) **95**; OmniSight-Productizer main tree **94**. Newest lesson in the prod image is L-OP-2571-*; newest on develop is L-OP-2747-*. ADR-0015 OP-2556 amendment pins `kg_source`/`kg_neighbours` to backend/agents/lesson_retrieval.py BM25 over `docs/sop/lessons/*.md`, pre-warmed at FastAPI startup; `OMNISIGHT_LESSONS_DIR` is unset in the prod backend so it uses the repo-root default inside the image.
- **expected vs actual:** The 2026-07-07 audit's recommendation was to 'unify and feed the dead axes from the living systems rather than add a 7th silo'. The unification happened (OP-2556 wired BM25 lessons into the structural axis) but onto a corpus that has no single physical home.
- **impact:** The structural axis's only content-bearing half serves a corpus frozen at backend image-build time and already 3 lessons stale; a lesson merged today cannot influence pickup context until the next backend deploy. Meanwhile the runner (which git-clones fresh per cycle) would see a different corpus, and the coordinator's sora-bridge tree a third. 'Same query, different answer depending on which process asked' is the definition of an incoherent store.

### [MEDIUM] The same structural JIRA facts are fetched and rendered TWICE into one runner prompt, from two independently-cached sources — reversing ADR-0015 decision #3

- **category:** design  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** auto-runner-jira.py:1705-1716 assembles both `links_block = _render_issue_links_block(f)` (the OP-2538 'Blockers / Blocking / Parent (OP-2538 pickup enrichment)' block, :1232, built from the runner's own pickup GET, ungated) AND `ps_block = _build_project_state_block(key, list(labels))` (:1238), whose payload's structural half is built by backend/agents/project_state_aggregator.py `_structural_jira` from `JIRA_PULL_FIELDS = "issuelinks,parent,status,summary"` (:335). `OMNISIGHT_PROJECT_STATE_INJECT=true` on claude-1/2, codex-1/2 and default (read from /proc/<pid>/environ), so both blocks are live simultaneously. The aggregator side is behind a process-local LRU+TTL cache plus a JIRA negative cache (`jira_negative_cache_hits`); the runner side is live-read.
- **expected vs actual:** ADR-0015 Decision #3: 'The integration layer is the product boundary. The runner consumes /api/v1/project-state, not Cognee, Graphiti, Memory Tool, and failure graph directly.' The R2 amendment records the reversal ('R2 deliberately chose runner-side field enrichment over an API-side cache machine') but does not remove the aggregator's duplicate half, so both now ship.
- **impact:** Two representations of the same parent/blocker/status facts land in one prompt from sources with different freshness semantics (live vs TTL-cached, with a negative cache that returns `jira_source: degraded` on a cached 404). When they disagree the agent sees contradictory structural context and has no way to tell which is authoritative. It also doubles JIRA load per pickup.

### [MEDIUM] No environment hosts all three legs; leg-2 exists only on staging (mostly synthetic fixtures), leg-1 only in prod (2 rows total), leg-3 duplicated in both with divergent publish state

- **category:** design  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** False
- **evidence:** PROD (pg-primary/omnisight): l3_facts 1 (2026-07-22), chat_session_summaries 1 (2026-07-22), claude_memory_state 177 (40 published / 137 quarantined), runner_incidents 1973 (last 2026-07-20), and **0** rows in every leg-2 table (learned_item_versions/snapshots/evidence/citations/utility, memory_publications/approvals/eval_runs/eval_cases, curator_merge_candidates). STAGING (omnisight_staging): curator_merge_candidates 5, learned_item_versions 9, learned_item_evidence 12, memory_publications 8, memory_eval_runs 14, memory_eval_cases 190 — but l3_facts 0 and runner_incidents 0. Env: prod backend carries only OMNISIGHT_U6_L2_WRITE / _SORA_L3_READ / _SORA_MEMORY_SCHEDULER / _U6_METRICS_ENABLED; staging carries those PLUS OMNISIGHT_WORKER_CURATOR=1, _WORKER_CURATOR_LLM=1, _MEMORY_PROMOTION_EVAL=1. 4 of the 5 staging curator candidates are synthetic (OP-9001/9002/9003 'fix uvc bind retry storm' etc., and OP-9101 'calibration probe'); only OP-2724 is a real ticket.
- **expected vs actual:** North star: ONE unified governed memory serving three principals. Actual: the legs are separated by ENVIRONMENT, not by scope. No single database contains a working instance of all three, so the 'one memory' has never existed anywhere at once, and cross-leg behaviour (e.g. claude_memory_bridge.py feeding leg-3 lessons into leg-2's learned_item_versions) can only be exercised on staging where leg-1's causal/L3 inputs are empty.
- **impact:** Cross-leg integration is untestable end-to-end in any environment. The staging 'leg-2 green' evidence rests on a 4-of-5 synthetic corpus. Any promotion of leg-2 to prod is a first-run, not a re-run.

### [MEDIUM] Write-only and read-only-empty stores: chat_session_summaries has no reader at all, episodic_memory has no writer, auto_distilled_skills is empty with the distiller enabled, and embedding_chunks holds an orphaned skill chunk

- **category:** design  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** chat_session_summaries: `grep -rln chat_session_summaries backend/ | grep -v tests` → only u6_memory_scheduler.py, u6_l2_writer.py, u6_l2_outcome.py, u6_metrics_refresh.py, metrics.py, db.py, the migration. No router, no app/ or components/ reference (`grep -rn ... backend/routers/ app/ components/` returns nothing) — so the design's 'UI-only surface' (v2 §2.C) was never built. 1 row, last written 2026-07-22. episodic_memory: 0 rows; the model-callable writer save_solution is deliberately unbound (tools.py:2031 `EPISODIC_TOOLS = [search_past_solutions]`, tools.py:2012-2029), leaving live readers (search_past_solutions, rag_prefetch→nodes.py:1748) over an empty table. auto_distilled_skills: 0 rows in BOTH prod and staging although `OMNISIGHT_SELF_IMPROVE_LEVEL=l1` in both and skill_distiller.is_enabled() returns True for 'l1' (skill_distiller.py:99-103); its only caller is backend/workflow.py:432 (Architect Guild trajectories). embedding_chunks (prod): exactly 1 row, `{"kind": "distilled_skill_summary", "agent_id": "architect_guild", "skill_id": "auto-uvc-camera-bringup", "source_skill_draft_id": "ads-lightup-demo"}`, created 2026-07-03 — a distilled-skill RAG chunk whose auto_distilled_skills source row does not exist.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: every store has both a producer and a consumer, and the RAG index is derived from a source of record. Actual: L2 is write-only with no consumer of any kind; episodic_memory is read-only-empty; the distilled-skill vector index contains a chunk with no backing record, so skill_memory.retrieve_distilled_skills can return a hit that cannot be resolved.
- **impact:** Four stores in the inventory carry no working value path. The orphaned embedding chunk is a concrete cross-store inconsistency: the retrieval index and the record table disagree about what exists. Because the L3-read/loader paths all degrade to empty silently, none of this is distinguishable at runtime from 'nothing relevant was found'.

### [MEDIUM] leg-2's entire publication→injection chain is switched off in every environment — 8 staging publications have no consumer anywhere

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** backend/learned_item_loader.py:42-63: reading requires BOTH `OMNISIGHT_LEARNED_ITEM_PROMOTION_ENABLED` and `OMNISIGHT_LEARNED_ITEM_READ`. `docker inspect ... | grep -c LEARNED_ITEM` returns **0** for omnisight-productizer-backend-a-1, -b-1 AND omnisight-staging-backend-a-1 (control: `grep -c SORA` on the same env returns 2, so the grep works). Prometheus: `omnisight_memory_enabled` = 0 on both prod replicas. The runner-side path is also off: `OMNISIGHT_RUNNER_LEARNED_ITEMS` is UNSET on every runner instance I sampled (auto-runner-jira.py:1083 requires it truthy). Staging holds memory_publications=8, learned_item_versions=9.

Actual: the chain terminates at publication. learned_item_citations = 0 rows in both environments, consistent with nothing ever being injected or cited.
- **impact:** The worker experience loop produces governed artifacts that no principal consumes. The final two stages (injection, citations) have never run against real data, so the loop's actual payoff is unvalidated — and because prompt_loader.py:1219 short-circuits to an empty block on kill-switch-off, the absence looks identical to 'no relevant cards'.

### [MEDIUM] OMNISIGHT_COGNEE_RECALL=true is set on the process that never reads it, and unset on the only process that does

- **category:** bug  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** `docker inspect omnisight-productizer-backend-a-1` env contains `OMNISIGHT_COGNEE_RECALL=true`. The flag is defined at backend/agents/agent_feature_flags.py:63-67 (default False) and its ONLY consumer is auto-runner-jira.py:1315 `if not agent_feature_flags.cognee_recall.enabled(): return ""` (plus :1664 which merely reports it) — `grep -rn cognee_recall --include=*.py . | grep -v tests` finds no backend consumer. Reading /proc/<pid>/environ of the live runners: COGNEE_RECALL is `<UNSET>` on claude-1, claude-2, codex-1, codex-2 and default. Same pattern for ANTIPATTERN_INJECT, REFLECTION_RAG_PROMPT and FAILURE_GRAPH_ENABLED (all unset ⇒ default False on every runner).
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: a flag is set on the principal that evaluates it. Actual: the lesson-recall flag is enabled on the backend container (a no-op there) and disabled by omission on the runner fleet, so `_build_lesson_recall_block` returns '' on every pickup.
- **impact:** An operator inspecting prod config sees Cognee lesson recall 'on'. It is off. Corroborated by the runner logs: across ~60,000 cycles since 2026-07-03 in /tmp/runner-{claude,codex}-*.log there is not a single `[runner] lesson_recall.*`, `[runner] antipattern_inject.*` or `[runner] rpg_enrich.*` line (control: `[runner] progress.record_phase` and `[runner] runner.pickup_blocked_*` lines ARE present, so the grep and the log path work). Three of the runner's six memory-injection blocks are dark and the config suggests otherwise.

### [MEDIUM] The causal axis has returned empty for 100% of production requests, and its 7-day source window has been empty since 2026-07-20

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** Prometheus: `omnisight_project_state_axis_total{axis="causal",content="empty"} 3.0` — and there is no `content="non_empty"` series for the causal axis at all, i.e. 3 of 3 requests since the 2026-07-22 replica start returned empty. project_state_aggregator.py:676 uses a `timedelta(days=7)` lookback into runner_incidents; prod runner_incidents max(created_at) = 2026-07-20 05:40 and `SELECT count(*) FROM runner_incidents WHERE created_at > now() - interval '7 days'` = **0** (control: the same table returns 1973 total and a per-month histogram, so the query works). Staging runner_incidents = 0 rows entirely. `OMNISIGHT_FAILURE_GRAPH_ENABLED` is unset on every runner, so the separate failure-graph pickup block is also dark.
- **expected vs actual:** ADR-0015: 'Causal axis: available only from the durable incident/failure-memory paths that are actually populated. Empty causal context means "no usable causal recall for this pickup", not proof that no causal risk exists.' The honesty framing is in the doc; there is no alert or metric threshold that distinguishes 'legitimately quiet fleet' from 'incident writer broken'.
- **impact:** One of the three axes has produced nothing in production. The ADR-mandated 'non-empty-rate content alert' (anti-patterns #15 cure item 2) does not exist for the causal axis — the only alert on non-empty rate is ProjectStateStructuralHollow, which covers the structural axis only. I could not determine whether the 2026-07-20 stop is a broken writer or a genuinely idle fleet (the runners have logged 'no candidate passed pre-pickup checks' every cycle), and neither can an operator, which is the finding.

### [MEDIUM] Prometheus loads only one of three rule files present on disk, so no leg has alerting except project-state

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** `/etc/prometheus/prometheus.yml` → `rule_files:` contains exactly one entry, `/etc/prometheus/obs-rules/project_state_health.yml`. `ls /etc/prometheus/obs-rules/` shows THREE files: alerts.yml (4 alerts: OmniSightReplicaLagHigh, OmniSightRollingDeploy, OmniSightBackendInstanceDown, OmniSightMigrationMismatch) and frontend-freshness-alerts.yml are present but NOT loaded. `/api/v1/rules` returns exactly one group (omnisight_project_state_health, 4 rules). No rule anywhere references omnisight_u6_*, omnisight_memory_*, omnisight_claude_memory_*, omnisight_learned_* or omnisight_action_guard_*.
- **expected vs actual:** U6 v2 §6: 'No surface ships without its metric (Phase-S rule)'. The metrics DO ship — omnisight_u6_l2_last_summary_age_seconds, omnisight_memory_liveness_heartbeat, omnisight_u6_l3_fact_count, omnisight_claude_memory_drift all exist and are scraped. What is missing is any alert on them.
