- **impact:** Observed values that nobody is alerted on: `omnisight_u6_l2_last_summary_age_seconds` = 855,226 s (**9.9 days**) while the U6 scheduler reports 2,871 successful ticks — an alive-but-producing-nothing loop; `omnisight_memory_liveness_heartbeat` = 0 (the §6 distiller liveness gauge, reading zero); `omnisight_memory_enabled` = 0. Every one of these is the designed anti-hollow signal for a leg, and every one is unwatched. Metric existence was mistaken for coverage — the precise error anti-patterns #15 warns about.

### [LOW] Cognee/Graphiti infrastructure runs as three idle stores with no live caller, no scheduler, and two divergent data roots

- **category:** design  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** False
- **evidence:** Running containers: omnisight-cognee (up 6 weeks), omnisight-neo4j (up 5 weeks, systemd unit neo4j.service active). Data root A = volume omnisight-productizer_cognee-data-a mounted at /cognee-data in the BACKEND (73 MB; newest ingested text_*.txt files 2026-07-08). Data root B = volumes cognee_cognee-system + cognee_cognee-data inside the cognee CONTAINER (452K/444K; /app/.cognee_system/databases last modified 2026-05-12). neo4j /data = 2.2 MB, /data/databases untouched since the 2026-06-20 container start. `OMNISIGHT_COGNEE_NEO4J_URI=bolt://neo4j:7687` and `OMNISIGHT_MCP_GRAPHITI_TOKEN` are both configured in the prod backend. No cognee/graphiti timer exists at user or system level. The only live Cognee callers are gated off: retrieve_lessons_via_cognee (cognee_recall, unset) and retrieve_antipatterns_via_cognee (antipattern_inject, unset). Temporal axis is hard-pinned: project_state_aggregator.py:94 `TEMPORAL_UNAVAILABLE_PAYLOAD = {"status": "unavailable"}`.
- **expected vs actual:** ADR-0015 OP-2556 amendment: 'Cognee infra is PARKED, not deleted — independent runner/pipeline callers remain.' Actual: those independent callers are all behind flags that are off in production, so there are no live callers; and the 'one' structural KG is physically three stores (two Cognee roots + neo4j) with different vintages.
- **impact:** Three long-running services and two divergent knowledge-graph data roots consume host resources and operator attention while serving nothing. More importantly for coherence, the API side and the runner side hold contradictory positions on Cognee: OP-2556 removed it from the aggregator's hot path as latency-broken and key-starved, while auto-runner-jira.py:1318-1327 still tries Cognee FIRST on the pickup hot path with BM25 as fallback. Two components of the same system disagree about whether Cognee is usable.

### [INFO] Silo count: at least 25 distinct live memory stores across 4 backing technologies for a system whose north star is 'ONE governed memory'

- **category:** design  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** False
- **evidence:** Postgres (prod, verified by row-count scan): (1) chat_messages 65, (2) chat_sessions 9, (3) chat_session_summaries 1, (4) l3_facts 1 + l3_approvals 1 + l3_eval_runs 1 + l3_erasure_audit 0, (5) episodic_memory 0, (6) runner_incidents 1973, (7) learned_item_{versions,snapshots,evidence,citations,utility} 0, (8) memory_{publications,approvals,eval_runs,eval_cases,transition_events} 0, (9) curator_merge_candidates 0, (10) auto_distilled_skills 0, (11) embedding_chunks 1, (12) claude_memory_{state,versions,transition_events,exports} 177/179/219/9, (13) agent_skill_state 7, (14) conflict_observations 0, (15) trust_scores 0, (16) blocks 0. Filesystem: (17) docs/sop/lessons ×6 locations, (18) docs/sop/architecture-anti-patterns.md (its own retrieval path), (19) /var/omnisight/memory/<fleet> ×10 dirs, (20) ~/.claude/.../memory/*.md 181 files, (21) MEMORY.md index, (22) project_memory.py CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md rule corpus, (23) cognee data root A, (24) cognee data root B, (25) neo4j graph. Plus per-replica in-process caches: BM25 lesson index, project-state LRU+TTL + JIRA negative cache, learned_item_loader._cache. Plus a configured-but-unavailable Graphiti MCP.
- **expected vs actual:** The 2026-07-07 audit warned the upgrade should 'unify and feed the dead axes from the living systems rather than add a 7th silo'. Since then the project has added leg-1 (4 tables), leg-2's curator layer (1 table on top of U4's 10), and leg-3 (4 tables) — net +9 tables — while retiring none of the dead ones (episodic_memory, auto_distilled_skills, conflict_observations, trust_scores, blocks and the Memory Tool fleet dirs all remain, all empty).
- **impact:** The count went the wrong way. Of the 25 stores, 13 are empty, 4 hold 1 row, and 3 hold data whose last write was 9–12 days ago. Two hold the same corpus in two environments with divergent state. The architecture's own integration cost warning ('Five mature memory products require glue, governance, fallback paths...') has compounded rather than resolved.

---

## leg-2 — the worker experience loop (merge → verify → curator → distiller → eval → human approval → publication → injection → citations)

**Auditor summary:** Leg-2 is code-complete and genuinely well-built in its gate logic (fail-closed holdout, single human-only approval writer, real neg-control catches, unconditional retirement of legacy ungated writers), but as a *loop* it is severed in at least three independent places and runs in exactly zero production environments: prod has no leg-2 flags and all eight leg-2 tables are empty, the ground-truth ingest has never fired anywhere (30 `change_merged_event`s observed by the live SSH bridge vs 0 `merge_verify_total` samples and 0 ledger rows), and the publication stage has no production caller at all — `publish_learned_item_version` is invoked only from tests. Worse, the deployed images (prod *and* staging) still carry the OP-2727 arity bug that makes every successful curator submit report as `error`, and the eval — the loop's advertised quality bar — is structurally unable to ever certify usefulness, so a content-free "See merged Gerrit change N" pointer earns the same approvable `insufficient_evidence` verdict a real lesson would.

**Checks performed:**
- Compared leg-2 env flags across prod (/home/user/omnisight-prod/.env) and both staging backend containers via `docker inspect --format '{{range .Config.Env}}'` — prod: zero leg-2 flags; staging: 6 flags, none of them LEARNED_ITEM_READ / LEARNED_ITEM_PROMOTION_ENABLED / RUNNER_LEARNED_ITEMS
- Row-counted all 8 leg-2 tables on prod (omnisight-pg-primary/omnisight) and staging (omnisight-staging-postgres-1/omnisight_staging)
- Dumped every row of learned_item_versions, curator_merge_candidates, memory_eval_runs, memory_eval_cases, memory_approvals, memory_publications on staging
- Scraped /api/v1/metrics from prod backend-a (port 8000/8001) and staging backend-b (port 8011); grepped all leg-2 counters
- Proved the metrics harness by confirming the metric HELP/TYPE lines are registered while the sample lines are absent (counter never incremented)
- Read backend/agents/worker_loop_curator.py, worker_loop_distiller.py, memory_promotion_scheduler.py, memory_promotion_eval.py, memory_eval_stats.py, learned_item_approval.py, learned_item_loader.py, learned_item_refresh.py, learned_item_publication.py, learned_item_interlock.py, routers/memory_promotion.py, routers/learned_items.py, routers/webhooks.py
- Read the PG trigger sources learned_item_publication_gate and memory_approvals_bind_gate from pg_proc on staging
- Ran the router's _PENDING_SQL logic read-only against staging to enumerate what is approvable today
- Grepped the whole repo for callers of publish_learned_item_version, record_memory_approval, insert_curator_merge_candidate, refresh_scope, baseline_rate
- Counted injection breadcrumbs (lesson_recall./rpg_enrich./reflection_rag./learned_items.result) across all six live runner logs in /tmp/runner-*.log (661k-711k [runner] lines each), and proved the grep harness with known-present tokens (class:subscription=57, tier:=560, project_state=2534)
- Checked /home/user/.config/omnisight/runner.env and the runner-claude@.service unit + drop-ins for OMNISIGHT_RPG_PROMPT_ENRICH / OMNISIGHT_RUNNER_LEARNED_ITEMS
- Read /home/user/work/sora/logs/bridge/systemd.log for change_merged_event volume and timestamps (30 events, last 2026-07-29)
- Diffed the running container source (docker exec sed -n on /app/backend/agents/worker_loop_curator.py) against develop commit 29851198 to confirm the arity hotfix is not deployed
- Grepped deploy/prometheus/*.yml and deploy/observability/prometheus/*.yml for any leg-2 alert rule; queried the prod Prometheus /api/v1/targets
- Verified prod alembic head (0280) and prod .env presence (not value) of OMNISIGHT_GERRIT_WEBHOOK_SECRET / absence of gerrit_http_user/password
- Inspected the in-repo suite configs/plan_triage/manifest.yml and the mounted holdout at /etc/omnisight/holdout inside the staging container

**Could not check:**
- Gerrit-side webhook plugin configuration on sora.services (the NAS) — I could not confirm whether a `change-merged` webhook is even registered, only that zero events reach the backend handler
- staging backend-a (omnisight-staging-backend-a-1) is 'unhealthy' and did not answer /api/v1/metrics on host port 8010 within 20s, so its curator/eval counters are unread
- Whether `patchset_count` is reliably derived on a real production verify — no real verify has ever executed, so both real-shaped staging ledger rows carry NULL patchset_count and I cannot attribute that to the extraction code vs. hand-seeding (marked INFERENCE below)
- End-to-end behaviour of the eval on a deliberately crafted answer-key item — that would require DB/API writes, which the read-only constraint forbids
- Whether the OP-2727 fix would change outcomes in practice, since no candidate has been processed on the current containers

### [CRITICAL] The leg-2 ground-truth ingest has never fired in any environment — the loop is wired to a webhook that cannot authenticate, while the live merge consumer was never wired at all

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** `grep -c change_merged_event /home/user/work/sora/logs/bridge/systemd.log` → 30, last five at 2026-07-28T13:45:50Z … 2026-07-29T02:44:18Z (prod backend up since 2026-07-22T15:02:45Z). Prod `/api/v1/metrics` line 1318-1319 registers `omnisight_merge_verify_total` with ZERO sample lines; same on staging (staging_metrics.txt:1146-1147). `SELECT count(*) FROM curator_merge_candidates` = 0 on prod, `SELECT count(*) FROM episodic_memory` = 0 on prod. The only writer is backend/routers/webhooks.py:1806 (`db.insert_curator_merge_candidate`), reached only from `_on_change_merged` (webhooks.py:1473) via `POST /webhooks/gerrit` (webhooks.py:253). That endpoint hard-401s when a secret is set: webhooks.py:316-318 `if secret and not _verify_gerrit_signature(...): return 401` — and the same handler's own docstring (webhooks.py:262-268) states Gerrit's webhooks plugin v3.13.5 "has NO support for any form of auth metadata … zero references to header, auth, bearer, signature, secret". `/home/user/omnisight-prod/.env` has OMNISIGHT_GERRIT_WEBHOOK_SECRET SET (len=43). The live consumer `pgrep -af gerrit_jira_bridge` → /home/user/sora-bridge/scripts/run_gerrit_jira_bridge.py handles change-merged at gerrit_jira_bridge.py:964/1092 but never calls _save_merged_solution_to_l3 or insert_curator_merge_candidate (repo-wide grep shows webhooks.py:1806 as the sole caller).
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: every merged, human-+2 change lands one row in curator_merge_candidates, feeding the curator. Actual: 30 merges observed by the live daemon in the audit window, 0 verify attempts, 0 ledger rows, 0 episodic rows — in both environments, since deployment.
- **impact:** Leg-2 has no input. The entire loop downstream (curator → distiller → eval → approval → publication → injection → citations → utility) is fed by a source that is structurally incapable of producing a single row. This is the identical failure class as the 2026-07-07 'silent-hollow compound': the repair added an in-handler degraded alarm (`merge_verify_total{outcome="degraded"}` + `security.merge_unconfirmed` audit) but the actual break is UPSTREAM of the handler, so the designed alarm can never fire. `SELECT action FROM audit_log WHERE action ILIKE '%merge%'` on prod returns only merger-bot rows — zero l3_verify_dropped, zero security.merge_unconfirmed. Following the runbook's own Step 0 ("Assert gerrit_webhook_secret is set on serving") is what closes the door.

### [CRITICAL] Leg-2 does not run in production at all — every flag unset, every table empty

- **category:** mismatch-with-intent  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** `docker inspect omnisight-productizer-backend-a-1 --format '{{range .Config.Env}}{{println .}}{{end}}' | grep -iE 'curator|learned_item|promotion_eval|utility_rollup|holdout'` → no output (57 OMNISIGHT_* vars, none leg-2). `grep -niE 'curator|learned_item|promotion_eval|utility_rollup|holdout' /home/user/omnisight-prod/.env` → no output. Prod DB: curator_merge_candidates 0, learned_item_versions 0, learned_item_citations 0, learned_item_utility 0, memory_eval_runs 0, memory_approvals 0, memory_publications 0, learned_item_snapshots 0. `omnisight_memory_enabled 0.0` on both prod and staging. `omnisight_memory_delivery_total` has ZERO samples on both — `get_learned_items_block` (learned_item_loader.py:164, which emits a sample on EVERY exit including kill_switch_off) has never been called in either environment.
- **expected vs actual:** Release ledger v0.8.0 is titled 'three memory legs: leg-1 3-tier + leg-2 worker loop + leg-3 Claude unification' and the memory index records leg-2 as 'CODE-COMPLETE + STAGING-GREEN'. Actual production state: leg-2 code ships in the image (metric HELP strings present at prod /metrics:1320-1327) but not one flag is set and not one row exists.
- **impact:** Any claim that leg-2 'ships' in v0.8.0 is a claim about code presence, not behaviour. For comparison, leg-3 in the same release has 179 rows in claude_memory_versions on prod — so the release genuinely delivered leg-3 and delivered nothing for leg-2. The distinction matters for the release ledger's honesty.

### [CRITICAL] The publication stage has zero production callers — publish_learned_item_version is invoked only from tests, so no approved item can ever become published

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** `grep -rn 'publish_learned_item_version' . --include=*.py --include=*.sh --include=*.md` → the definition at backend/learned_item_publisher.py:302, one docstring mention at :469, and 20+ hits, ALL under backend/tests/. No router, no scheduler, no CLI, no script calls it. By contrast `revoke_learned_item_version` IS exposed at backend/routers/memory_promotion.py:264-272. The runbook docs/operations/leg2-activation-runbook.md:51-52 instructs: "a HUMAN approves via POST /memory-promotions/{id}/approve and publishes" — but backend/routers/memory_promotion.py defines only /memory-promotions/pending (:166), /approve (:216) and /revoke (:239). The approve handler's own docstring at learned_item_approval.py:6-8 says "Approval does NOT publish — U4-C's publisher revalidates and publishes later"; nothing schedules that later step.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: human approval → publisher materialises the item into learned_item_snapshots → loader serves it. Actual: approval writes a memory_approvals row and the chain terminates there; there is no reachable code path that transitions a version to `published`.
- **impact:** Even a perfectly-fed, perfectly-evaluated, human-approved learned item cannot reach a prompt in any deployment. The two `published` rows that exist on staging are dated 2026-07-11 and were produced by the U4-J pilot harness, not by the shipped API. The runbook's Step 5 is not executable as written, and an operator following it would approve items and then observe nothing happen, with no error.

### [HIGH] The deployed prod AND staging images still carry the OP-2727 curator arity bug: every successful submit is counted as `error` and every LLM-lane label is discarded

- **category:** bug  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** False
- **evidence:** `docker exec omnisight-staging-backend-b-1 sed -n '236,248p' /app/backend/agents/worker_loop_curator.py` and the same on omnisight-productizer-backend-a-1 both end with `return "submitted" if result.created else "dup"` — the pre-fix bare string. develop commit 29851198 ([OP-2727] hotfix, 2026-07-23) changes that line to `return ("submitted" if result.created else "dup"), llm_label`. The caller at worker_loop_curator.py:297-299 does `submit_label, llm_label = await _distill_candidate(...)`, so a bare 'dup' unpacks into 3 characters and 'submitted' into 9 — ValueError, caught at :305 into `counts["error"] += 1`.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected (per runbook Step 3.1): "Watch worker_curator_candidates_total{result} (submitted vs deferred)" and Step 3.2 "Watch distilled_llm vs gate_skip_* / llm_* labels". Actual on deployed code: `result="submitted"` and every `llm_*`/`gate_skip_*` label can NEVER increment; every real success lands in `result="error"` with a traceback, AFTER the submit and the distilled=TRUE mark have already committed.
- **impact:** The single metric the activation runbook tells the operator to gate stage-2 on is inverted on the code that is actually running. An operator watching it would see 100% errors during correct operation, or (as now) nothing at all. The fix merged to develop nine days ago; because backend code runs from the prod IMAGE, merged != live and it has not shipped. Data stays correct; observability is destroyed — which is precisely the failure mode this project is trying to eliminate.

### [HIGH] The eval can never certify usefulness — `promote` is structurally unreachable under the calibrated battery, and `insufficient_evidence` is an approvable verdict, so a content-free pointer is indistinguishable from a real lesson

- **category:** design  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** Battery calibration (staging memory_eval_cases, per-case aggregation): all 16 positives `runner-plan-triage.yaml::p01..p16` have baseline_pass = 4/4; all 14 neg-controls `::nc01..nc09` have baseline_pass = 0/4. `decide()` (backend/memory_eval_stats.py:138) returns 'promote' only when mcnemar_p<0.05 AND net_flips>0, and improvements are counted only where `not baseline_pass and candidate_pass` (memory_eval_stats.py:106-108) — i.e. only on cases the baseline FAILS, which are exactly the neg-controls, and a neg-control candidate_pass FORCES reject (memory_promotion_eval.py:549-552). Improvement headroom is therefore 0 by construction; memory_promotion_scheduler.py:286-297 alarms `promote` as an ANOMALY. Meanwhile backend/learned_item_approval.py:137-139 accepts `decision in ('promote','insufficient_evidence')` and the DB trigger learned_item_publication_gate accepts the same pair. Live consequence — running the router's _PENDING_SQL against staging returns three approvable cards: ground-truth:OP-9002, ground-truth:OP-2724, ground-truth:OP-9101, each verdict `insufficient_evidence` (n=30, net_flips=-1) with a clean holdout sibling `insufficient_evidence`, renderer u4r2 (current). Their entire content is the deterministic pointer built at worker_loop_curator.py:83-88: scope="Tickets like OP-2724", one step="See merged Gerrit change 2201 for the applied, human-reviewed (+2) solution to OP-2724."
- **expected vs actual:** Expected (per the project's own research doc, docs/research/2026-07-07-self-improving-agents-survey.md:166-168: "Eval-gated lesson promotion = open territory; OmniSight can be ahead"): the eval gates promotion on demonstrated usefulness. Actual: the eval is a pure HARM filter (it can only reject); it emits the identical approvable verdict for a genuinely useful playbook and for a content-free URL pointer, and even for a mildly harmful one (net_flips=-1, p=1.0 → insufficient_evidence, approvable).
- **impact:** The advertised anti-hollow property of the loop — 'eval (2-leg holdout)' — does not discriminate content. If publication were ever wired, the bank would fill with pointer cards that consume prompt budget and teach nothing, each carrying a green-looking eval card. This is the mechanism by which a repaired system re-hollows: the gate is real but measures the wrong thing.

### [HIGH] The utility metric is not `hits × success-delta`: baseline_rate is written and never read, and the success signal joins a table that structurally cannot be populated

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** `grep -rn 'baseline_rate' backend/ --include=*.py --include=*.sql | grep -v /tests/` → only the DDL (alembic/versions/0278_lifecycle_citations_utility.py:58) and the two INSERT/UPDATE mentions in _ROLLUP_SQL (memory_promotion_scheduler.py:555, :562). No decision path reads it. The revocation query (memory_promotion_scheduler.py:580-589) uses an ABSOLUTE rate `u.successes::float8 / u.hits < $2` (floor 0.2), never a delta versus baseline_rate. The success predicate (memory_promotion_scheduler.py:545-550) is `EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM curator_merge_candidates m WHERE m.ticket_key = c.ticket_key AND m.revert_state='none' AND m.merged_at BETWEEN c.cited_at AND c.cited_at+window)` — curator_merge_candidates is the table proven empty/unfeedable in finding 1. learned_item_citations = 0 rows on staging and prod; learned_item_utility = 0 rows on both. OMNISIGHT_MEMORY_UTILITY_ROLLUP=1 is ALREADY set on both staging backends.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected (research doc line 183-185): "utility = retrieval-hits × post-hit success delta". Actual: hits is real, the delta is never computed, and the success term reads a permanently-empty table. The rollup is live on staging and produces nothing because its input is empty — not because it is correct.
- **impact:** Two-fold. (a) The stated utility metric does not exist; what exists is an absolute success-rate threshold. (b) Latent data-integrity trap: the rollup has no guard requiring the ground-truth ledger to be healthy. The moment citations begin flowing (injection enabled) while the ledger stays starved, EVERY published card will compute successes=0 and, at hits>=3, generate a `memory_revoke_proposal` in proposed_actions. The system will confidently recommend revoking its entire bank on the basis of a structurally-empty input, and the proposal preview will read as an evidence-backed verdict.

### [HIGH] Not one Prometheus alert rule exists for any leg-2 metric, and Prometheus does not scrape the environment where leg-2 actually runs

- **category:** design  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** `grep -nE 'curator|learned_item|memory_delivery|memory_promotion|merge_verify|memory_proposal|memory_snapshot_stale|memory_failclosed' deploy/prometheus/*.yml deploy/observability/prometheus/*.yml` → no output. Harness proof: the same files contain 8 + 4 + 1 = 13 `- alert:` rules (OmniSightReplicaLagHigh, OmniSightBackendInstanceDown, OmniSightMigrationMismatch, …). `curl 127.0.0.1:9090/api/v1/targets?state=active` → exactly two targets, http://backend-a:8000/api/v1/metrics and http://backend-b:8001/api/v1/metrics — the PROD backends only. The staging backends (the only place leg-2 flags are on) are not scraped.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: the anti-hollow counters the team deliberately built — merge_verify_total{degraded}, memory_delivery_total{empty_degraded}, memory_promotion_eval_anomaly_total{promote_decision,neg_baseline_contaminated}, memory_snapshot_stale_total, memory_failclosed_total, worker_curator_candidates_total — are 'loud'. Actual: all are emitted into a registry with no rule watching them, and for the environment that matters, no scraper reaching them.
- **impact:** 'Loud, not silent' is only true relative to a human manually curling /metrics. Every failure this dimension found (zero ingest, zero candidates, error-classified successes) is precisely the class these counters were added to catch, and none of them would have paged anyone. The remediation built the sensors and never connected them.

### [HIGH] 28,323 curator ticks and 28,176 eval ticks reported outcome=ok over ~10 days while processing zero candidates and making zero decisions

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** staging backend-b /api/v1/metrics: `omnisight_worker_curator_ticks_total{outcome="ok"} 28323.0`, `omnisight_memory_promotion_eval_ticks_total{outcome="ok"} 28176.0`, while `omnisight_worker_curator_candidates_total` is entirely ABSENT (no labelled sample was ever produced) and `omnisight_memory_proposal_outcome_total` shows only 3 lifetime samples (insufficient_evidence 2, reject 1) whose `_created` timestamps decode to 2026-07-22T17:07:42Z and 2026-07-22T17:35:29Z. Container start 2026-07-22T16:56:22Z; today 2026-08-01. Ledger last row merged_at 2026-07-22 16:51:03. In worker_loop_curator.py:380 `outcome = "error" if result["error"] else "ok"` — an empty scan is `ok`; the empty case only writes an INFO log line at :329 ("no undistilled candidates (empty_expected)").
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: a green tick stream means the loop is working. Actual: a green tick stream is fully consistent with an input pipe that has been severed for 10 days. The code comment at worker_loop_curator.py:325-328 explicitly anticipates this ("cross-read that gauge here when β-1 adds the LLM cost worth gating") but the cross-read was never implemented.
- **impact:** There is no reconciliation between merges that occurred (30, observable in the bridge log) and candidates ingested (0). Nothing anywhere compares expected vs actual throughput, so the loop's liveness signal is a heartbeat, not a work signal — the definitional shape of the silent-hollow compound.

### [MEDIUM] No periodic bank audit exists: a version is evaluated exactly once, ever, and nothing re-validates published items

- **category:** design  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** backend/agents/memory_promotion_scheduler.py:392-395 — `_SCAN_SQL` selects versions `WHERE NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM memory_eval_runs r WHERE r.version_id = v.id AND r.decision IN ('promote','reject','insufficient_evidence'))`. A scored decision is permanently terminal; there is no re-scan predicate keyed on age, on model change, or on publication state. `grep -rn 'memory_publications' backend/ --include=*.py | grep -v /tests/ | grep -v alembic` → the only periodic reader is run_utility_rollup (memory_promotion_scheduler.py:583). learned_item_versions carries BEFORE UPDATE/DELETE block triggers, so items are immutable once written. The approval freshness bound (learned_item_approval.py:183-188, OMNISIGHT_MEMORY_APPROVAL_MAX_RUN_AGE_DAYS default 30) applies only at approval time. Repo-wide grep for 'misevolution|bank audit|poisoning audit' outside docs/research returns nothing.
- **expected vs actual:** The project's own research (docs/research/2026-07-07-self-improving-agents-survey.md:150-152, :189-190, and the closing loop spec line ~196) names it twice: "Misevolution … measurable safety-alignment decay from benign memory accumulation alone → the lesson bank itself is a drift surface requiring periodic audit" and "periodic misevolution/poisoning audit of highest-retrieval items" / "quarterly poisoning audit". Actual: no such job, timer, endpoint, or scan exists in the codebase.
- **impact:** Once publication is wired, the bank becomes append-only-and-never-rechecked. A card approved under a 2026-07 model/battery stays live indefinitely under a drifted model, and neither the neg-controls nor the holdout are ever re-run against the live set. This is a named design gap, not a bug — but it is the exact gap the team's own research flagged as the reason 'nobody ships ungated reflection in production'.

### [MEDIUM] The baseline arm is cached for the process lifetime and never re-measured, so the neg-baseline-contamination alarm is fed by stale data and can never detect real drift

- **category:** design  ·  **INFERENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** backend/memory_promotion_eval.py:231 `_BASELINE_CACHE: dict[tuple[str,str,int], dict[str,bool]] = {}` — module-level, keyed only by (suite_sha256, model_fingerprint, samples_per_case), with no TTL and no eviction (the only clearer is `_reset_for_tests` at :234). On a hit the baseline arm's ask_fn calls are SKIPPED entirely (:458) and the cached bools are written into memory_eval_cases as this run's baseline_pass (:496-511, :587-597). model_fingerprint (:143-146) hashes provider|model|temperature — a provider-side change behind the stable alias `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` does not change it. The contamination alarm (memory_promotion_scheduler.py:302-318) queries `SELECT case_id FROM memory_eval_cases WHERE eval_run_id=$1 AND baseline_pass` — i.e. the cached values. Corroborating data: all four non-holdout staging runs (16:30:30, 16:56:39, 17:26:15 ×2) report identical baseline_pass = 16/30.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected (memory_promotion_scheduler.py:36-38): "a neg-control whose BASELINE arm complied would force-reject every card and poison the in-process baseline cache — alarmed loudly per run". Actual: after the first run in a process, the baseline arm is never executed again, so a neg-control that STARTS complying (model drift) is invisible — the alarm re-reads the cached pre-drift value on every subsequent run and stays quiet.
- **impact:** The calibration guarantee (19/19 negs refuse) is a point-in-time measurement frozen at container start, presented per-run as if freshly measured. A staging container has now been up 9+ days on one baseline sample. Secondary effect: pinning a constant baseline across candidate samples removes pairing variance from McNemar, which makes discordant pairs a function of the candidate arm alone and biases the exact test toward significance.

### [MEDIUM] Lesson injection into runners is completely dark in production — RPG_PROMPT_ENRICH is enabled yet emits no breadcrumb on any pickup, and the corpus it would retrieve is empty

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** `OMNISIGHT_RPG_PROMPT_ENRICH` is TRUTHY in /home/user/.config/omnisight/runner.env (loaded by runner-claude@.service.d/heartbeat-path.conf). Across all six live runner logs (/tmp/runner-{claude-1,claude-2,codex-1,codex-2,grok-1,gemini-1}.log, 128MB–1.4GB, covering 2026-06-15 → now, 661k–711k `[runner]` lines each): `rpg_enrich.` = 0 real breadcrumbs, `lesson_recall.` = 0, `reflection_rag.` = 0, `learned_items.result` = 0, `antipattern_inject.` = 0 (the handful of textual matches are ticket-body file paths, verified by inspection). Harness proof on the same files: class:subscription=57, tier:=560, project_state=2534, skill_xp=5. Root cause: auto-runner-jira.py:1451 `if character is None or not _rpg_prompt_enrich_enabled(): return ""` returns silently with NO log line, and `grep -ohE 'character:[A-Za-z0-9_-]+'` across the runner logs returns ZERO matches — no ticket has ever carried a `character:<slug>` label, so `_character = character_registry.character_from_labels(labels)` (auto-runner-jira.py:1667) is always None. Even if it fired, prod `auto_distilled_skills` = 0 rows and `embedding_chunks` = 1 row.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: the flag being on means distilled L2 skills reach the pickup prompt. Actual: the enrichment function short-circuits before its own fail-open breadcrumbs, so the flag is a fully silent no-op, and the retrieval corpus behind it is empty anyway. Meanwhile the 104-file lessons corpus at docs/sop/lessons/ is never injected because `cognee_recall` (agent_feature_flags.py:63) is env-default-False and unset in runner.env.
- **impact:** Every knowledge-injection channel into the runner — leg-2 learned items, RPG L2 distilled skills, the 104-lesson SOP corpus, anti-patterns, reflection RAG — is dark in production, and four of the five are dark without emitting a single observable signal. An operator reading runner.env would reasonably conclude lesson injection is live.

### [MEDIUM] Retrieval is not measured anywhere: the delivery counter has never emitted a sample and the citations table is the only retrieval signal, with a single caller that is unreachable

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** `omnisight_memory_delivery_total` has ZERO samples on both prod and staging /api/v1/metrics — yet backend/learned_item_loader.py:161-163 `_finish()` increments it on EVERY exit path including `kill_switch_off`, so zero samples proves `get_learned_items_block` has never been invoked in either environment. The sole citation writer is backend/routers/learned_items.py:58, guarded by `if result == "non_empty" and served and ticket` (:47) — reachable only through GET /learned-items/block, whose only client is auto-runner-jira.py:1078 `_fetch_learned_items_block`, gated on OMNISIGHT_RUNNER_LEARNED_ITEMS which is absent from runner.env. learned_item_citations = 0 rows on prod and staging.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected (research doc line 160-162, the RMM finding the design cites): memories are re-ranked by whether the generator actually cited them. Actual: no citation has ever been recorded, no delivery has ever been counted, and there is no retrieval-quality metric other than the (unfed, and mis-specified — see the utility finding) rollup.
- **impact:** There is no way to answer 'is retrieval working / helping' for leg-2 with data, in either environment. The instrumentation is well-designed (empty_expected vs empty_degraded vs kill_switch_off is a genuinely honest three-way split) but has never produced a single observation.

### [LOW] Human-approval is enforced only in Python; the DB gates check binding and eval shape but never the principal

- **category:** security  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** The PG trigger `memory_approvals_bind_gate` (read from pg_proc on staging) checks only: eval run belongs to version, not the holdout leg, is the latest non-infra run, and no later/tied reject. It does NOT inspect `approved_by`. The publication trigger `learned_item_publication_gate` checks approval_id linkage, decision ∈ (promote, insufficient_evidence), empty neg_control_catches, and a clean holdout sibling — also no principal check, and no requirement that suite_sha256 or model_fingerprint be non-NULL. Human-ness lives entirely at backend/learned_item_approval.py:47-73 (`assert_human_principal`) and :108-109 (`_is_bot_shaped(approved_by)`), whose pattern (:39 `(^|[^a-z0-9])(ai-|ci-)` plus '-bot' anywhere) is documented as deliberately narrow.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected per the kernel invariant 'enforced in code, not prompt': the human gate is a hard invariant. Actual: it is a hard invariant at the application layer only — and there is exactly ONE writer (`grep -rn 'INSERT INTO memory_approvals'` → learned_item_approval.py:204 alone), which is the mitigating fact. But a service account named e.g. `automation@omnisight` passes the bot-shape regex, and a NULL-fingerprint eval run is bindable.
- **impact:** Low today because there is a single sanctioned writer and no auto-approve path anywhere (verified: `publish_learned_item_version` and `record_memory_approval` have no non-test, non-router callers). Worth naming because the DB is the only layer that survives a future writer being added, and the freeze document calls the approvals row 'the freeze's REAL gate (G3)'.

### [LOW] Staging publication ledger carries duplicate event_seq values per version and NULL published_at on every `published` row, making the documented ordering fold ambiguous

- **category:** data-integrity  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** staging memory_publications, 8 rows over 2 versions: version 36341f08 has {published seq=1, approved seq=1, publishing seq=2, revoked seq=3}; version e9c3154f has {published seq=3, approved seq=4, publishing seq=5, revoked seq=6}. `published_at` is NULL on all four `published`/`revoked` rows. backend/learned_item_publication.py:78-86 `reduce_publication_state` folds an ORDERED iterable and explicitly delegates ordering to the caller ('this module does NOT sort — ordering is the caller's contract'); the column is `generated BY DEFAULT as identity`, so explicit values are accepted. Under `ORDER BY event_seq` a tie between `approved` and `published` at seq=1 is resolved arbitrarily.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: the event sequence is a total order per version, and a `published` event stamps published_at. Actual: pilot-era rows violate both, and nothing in the schema prevents it (no UNIQUE on event_seq, no NOT NULL / trigger on published_at for state='published').
- **impact:** These specific rows are 2026-07-11 U4-J pilot data written by a harness rather than the shipped publisher, so the practical blast radius today is nil. It matters because the publisher itself has never run in production (see finding 3), so this ledger's invariants have never been exercised by real code — and the freeze's V3.4 'ledger membership == snapshot membership' invariant is checked by a pure comparator (check_publication_invariant) that likewise has no production caller.

### [INFO] POSITIVE — the parts of the gate that were exercised are genuinely real, not decorative

- **category:** design  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** False
- **evidence:** (a) Neg-control machinery actually fires: `omnisight_memory_neg_control_catch_total 1.0` on staging, and eval run b01cc472 records `neg_control_catches: ["runner-plan-triage.yaml::nc04c-bots-may-vote"]` forcing decision=reject on a version whose raw stats were not reject-shaped. (b) The battery is honestly calibrated: 16/16 positives baseline-PASS, 14/14 in-repo and 5/5 holdout neg-controls baseline-REFUSE (memory_eval_cases aggregation). (c) The paired A/B design is genuinely anti-hollow in shape — the candidate arm substitutes the exact A2 bytes and the baseline arm an empty item slot (memory_promotion_eval.py:93-103, 165-173), so a trivial item yields net_flips≈0; observed: n=30, improvements=0, regressions=1 → insufficient_evidence, never promote. (d) `holdout_missing` is fail-closed at BOTH layers (learned_item_approval.py:152-164 and the PG publication trigger). (e) Legacy ungated FS writers are unconditionally 410 regardless of flag or principal (learned_item_interlock.py:40-63). (f) Rendered payloads are properly nonce-datamark-fenced (staging rendered_payload begins `----- BEGIN UNTRUSTED LEARNED-ITEM DATA [lid-fence-…]`, renderer u4r2). (g) `infra_invalid` is a real terminal — the two 2026-07-22 16:25 runs with reason `ask_exception` wrote no case rows and no scored arm.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected and actual agree here. Recording it so the negative findings are read as 'the loop is severed and mis-aimed', not 'the gates are fake'.
- **impact:** The gate logic is the strongest part of leg-2 and should not be reworked. The remediation work is upstream (ingest wiring, publication caller, image deployment, alerting) and in what the eval is asked to prove (usefulness, not just harmlessness).

---

## intent-mismatch — does the U6 "three principals, one governed kernel" memory system deliver its six stated expectations?

**Auditor summary:** Phase R and the U6 legs produced real, well-engineered mechanisms — a fail-closed kernel, a closed-schema L2/L3, a budgeted regenerator, a delete UI — but almost none of them are on a live path in production, and the gap is concealed by success-shaped telemetry. The headline mismatch: leg-3's publication gate is fully implemented in the database (137 quarantined / 40 published) yet 129 ungoverned entries are injected into every Claude session because the delivery artifact is never regenerated, while `omnisight_claude_memory_drift` reports 0 from a gauge that has no writer anywhere in the repository — the same "silent-hollow compound" the 2026-07-07 audit named, reproduced in the repair itself.

**Checks performed:**
- Read backend/agents/authorization_kernel.py (454 lines) in full — confirmed the §1 invariant is implemented as a return-value guarantee (never 'allow' for mutating) and that the module self-describes as DORMANT pending T11
- Read backend/agents/action_guard.py resolve_mode (:136-147), GuardOutcome, and the stage-1 decision path (:500-650) — located `proceed = mode != "enforce"` at :542 and :603
- Grepped OMNISIGHT_ACTION_GUARD_MODE across the whole develop tree and the prod checkout — hits only in tests and action_guard.py itself
- docker inspect omnisight-productizer-backend-a-1 — enumerated all 76 env var names; no GUARD key present
- Read /proc/<pid>/environ of the live auto-runner-jira.py process — confirmed OMNISIGHT_ACTION_GUARD_MODE absent, OMNISIGHT_RUNNER_SANDBOX_ENFORCE=1 present
- Grepped auto-runner-jira.py for ToolDispatcher/tool_dispatcher (no hits) and located the CLI spawn at subprocess.Popen line 2150
- Enumerated every non-test caller of the guard: only backend/agents/tool_dispatcher.py and backend/agents/nodes.py
- Proved the Prometheus query harness works (`up` returns 2 series; 111 omnisight_* metric names) before relying on any empty result
- Queried omnisight_action_guard_{decision,fail_closed,shadow_classification}_total — all empty; then fetched the raw exposition from http://localhost:8000/api/v1/metrics and confirmed HELP/TYPE present with zero children
- Grepped claude_memory_drift_gauge repo-wide, in /home/user/omnisight-prod, and inside the running container image — definition only, no setter
- Queried prod Postgres (omnisight-pg-primary, read-only SELECTs) for row counts of l3_*, l2/chat_session_summaries, claude_memory_*, learned_item_* tables
- Compared the live MEMORY.md slug set against claude_memory_state published/all sets using sorted comm — quantified 128 quarantined-but-injected and 1 never-ingested
- Tested for the regen header in the live MEMORY.md (absent) and read scripts/claude_memory_regen.py budget constants (_LINE_B=200, _FILE_B=24_000)
- Searched for any scheduler/timer/service invoking claude_memory_regen.py — none outside tests and its own docstring
- Read backend/routers/privacy.py DELETE statement list (:277-320) and cross-checked against l3_facts / chat_session_summaries
- Read backend/agents/u6_l3_store.py erase_user (:193-219) including the NOTE(ops) caveat, and app/settings/memory/page.tsx for the user-facing shred claim
- Read backend/agents/u6_l2_writer.py and queried jsonb_object_keys(summary_outcome) on the live L2 row — confirmed closed schema (topics/resolved/turn_count/outcome_kind/tasks_created/schema_version), no free text
- Read backend/agents/memory_tool_handler.py:770-800 for the L1 cap and oldest-first eviction
- Read backend/learned_item_loader.py:43-106 for the leg-2 kill switch and memory_enabled gauge semantics
- Read ADR-0015 including the 2026-07-08 Phase R reality-lock amendment and the OP-2556 BM25 amendment
- Read docs/audit/codex-reviews/memory-audit-remediation-codex-2026-07-26.txt (329 lines, verdict NOT-SOUND) in full
- Converted epoch timestamps to establish backend start (2026-07-22 15:02Z), newest chat session (2026-07-22 15:40Z) and last metrics refresh (2026-08-01 15:26Z)

**Could not check:**
- Whether the L2 flatline is purely no-traffic or also masks a writer defect: omnisight_u6_l2_summaries_written_total has zero children and the newest chat_session is 2026-07-22 15:40Z, so the scheduler most likely had nothing inactive to summarize since restart. I could not distinguish 'correct and idle' from 'would also fail under load' without generating traffic, which is a write.
- Runner-side guard behaviour at runtime: auto-runner-jira.py runs outside the Prometheus exporter, so no action_guard counters exist for it. My conclusion there rests on the absent env var plus the absent ToolDispatcher import, not on observed runner telemetry.
- Staging environment state (omnisight-staging-*, image sha-61fb3621). All live claims here are prod-only; the leg-2 'STAGING-GREEN' claim was not re-verified.
- Whether pre-shred dek_ref values are actually recoverable from backups/WAL/PITR — no backup or replica access was attempted, so finding #8 rests on the code's own comment rather than a recovery test.
- JIRA state of OP-2535..OP-2551 (Phase R) and the newer OP-2745..2758 DSAR epic — no JIRA queries were made, so I cannot say which of these gaps are already tracked as open tickets versus believed closed.
- The stale /home/user/sora-bridge tree (240 commits behind) and what pipeline-coordinator executes from it — not inspected; it may run an older copy of any of the code cited above.
- Whether the harness's 24.4KB index limit drops entries top-down, bottom-up, or by byte truncation — I observed that loss occurs but not which 129 entries survive.

### [CRITICAL] leg-3 publication gate has zero effect on what is actually delivered: 129 of 169 injected index entries are ungoverned

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** Live injected index /home/user/.claude/projects/-home-user-work-sora-OmniSight-Productizer/memory/MEMORY.md = 169 slugs, 51,476 bytes. DB: `SELECT state,count(*) FROM claude_memory_state GROUP BY state` -> published 40, quarantined 137. Set comparison (comm on sorted slug lists): 128 slugs are `quarantined` in the store yet present in the live index; 1 slug (`project_failed_units_remediation_2026_07_25`) has NO row in claude_memory_state at all; 0 published slugs are missing from the index. Proof the index is the injected artifact: its contents appear verbatim in this session's own system prompt. Proof it was never regen-produced: `grep -n '^# MEMORY.md — governed index' MEMORY.md` -> exit 1 (header written at scripts/claude_memory_regen.py:191-193 is absent).
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: leg-3 is ONE governed memory where quarantine->human-publish decides what reaches a session. Actual: the quarantine/publish state machine is real in Postgres and decorative in practice — the file that is actually injected still carries the entire pre-migration corpus, including 128 items a human explicitly has not published and 1 item that never entered governance at all.
- **impact:** Every governance decision made in leg-3 since 2026-07-23 (the publish wave) has had no effect on model behaviour. Content a human declined to publish is injected into every session unconditionally. The 'ONE governed memory' claim is not true for the leg that has the most data.

### [CRITICAL] `claude_memory_drift` gauge is defined but has no writer anywhere — permanently reports 0 (healthy) while real drift is 129 entries

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** `grep -rn 'claude_memory_drift_gauge' . --include=*.py` returns exactly two hits, both in backend/metrics.py (:1245 definition, :1407 the _NoOp fallback). No `.set()` call exists. Confirmed identical in three trees: develop (/home/user/work/sora/OmniSight-betaF), the release-pinned prod checkout (/home/user/omnisight-prod/backend/metrics.py:1245), and inside the running image (`docker exec omnisight-productizer-backend-a-1 grep -rn 'claude_memory_drift' /app/backend/*.py`). Live value: `curl localhost:9090/api/v1/query?query=omnisight_claude_memory_drift` -> 0 on both backend-a and backend-b. Query harness proven working: the same endpoint returns 2 series for `up` and 111 `omnisight_*` metric names.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected (governance non-negotiable): 'metrics or don't ship' — drift between the governed store and the delivered index must be observable. Actual: an unlabelled Prometheus Gauge that no code ever sets, so it defaults to 0.0 and is scraped as 0 forever. Zero is also the value that means 'no drift', so the metric reads as healthy precisely when it is measuring nothing.
- **impact:** This is a textbook reproduction of the 2026-07-07 anti-pattern ('a causal axis whose only writer never existed'). It is the exact instrument that would have caught the 129-entry governance bypass above, and it will never fire. Any dashboard or alert built on it is structurally incapable of alarming.

### [CRITICAL] The action-capability kernel is not enforcing anywhere in production, and is not on the production runner's execution path at all

- **category:** design  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** (a) `OMNISIGHT_ACTION_GUARD_MODE` is absent from the prod backend container (`docker inspect omnisight-productizer-backend-a-1` — 76 env vars, no GUARD key) and absent from the live runner process (`tr '\0' '\n' < /proc/$(pgrep -f auto-runner-jira.py)/environ | grep -c OMNISIGHT_ACTION_GUARD_MODE` -> 0). Repo-wide the var appears only in tests and in action_guard.py itself. (b) Empty matrix => resolve_mode returns 'shadow' for every known (adapter,family) (action_guard.py:147), and action_guard.py:542 is `proceed = mode != "enforce"` => True. (c) The production runner has zero references to the guarded chokepoint: `grep -n 'ToolDispatcher\|tool_dispatcher' auto-runner-jira.py` -> no hits; it spawns the agent CLI via `subprocess.Popen(wrapped_cmd, ...)` at auto-runner-jira.py:2150. (d) `omnisight_action_guard_decision_total` HELP/TYPE lines are present in the prod exposition (module imported) with ZERO child samples after 10 days uptime.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: 'persistent memory can NEVER authorize a side effect; enforced in code, not prompt.' Actual: the kernel correctly classifies every mutating operation as `requires_grant` and never `allow` — the invariant holds as a *return value* — but the guard then proceeds regardless because no key is set to enforce. Separately, the process that actually writes code, commits, and pushes never calls the guard at all.
- **impact:** The invariant is true in the type system and false in operation. A memory-sourced instruction that induces a mutating tool call reaches `requires_grant` and then executes. The 'one shared kernel' that is supposed to unify all three principals recorded zero decisions in 10 days of prod uptime. Partial mitigation to credit: `OMNISIGHT_RUNNER_SANDBOX_ENFORCE=1` is set on the live runner, so a real filesystem/network jail does constrain the CLI — but that is a different mechanism and does not implement the memory-cannot-authorize invariant.

### [HIGH] The leg-3 index budget is implemented but never applied — the live index is 2.1x over budget and is truncated at every session load

- **category:** mismatch-with-intent  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** scripts/claude_memory_regen.py:49-50 defines `_LINE_B = 200` and `_FILE_B = 24_000` with whole-line bottom-up dropping. Live file is 51,476 bytes (`wc -c`). `grep -rn 'claude_memory_regen' . ` outside tests returns only the script's own usage docstring — no systemd unit, timer, compose service, or Python caller invokes it; `ls deploy/systemd/ | grep -iE 'memory|regen|claude'` shows only memory-tool-monitor and runner units. This session's own system prompt carries the harness warning: 'WARNING: MEMORY.md is 48.9KB (limit: 24.4KB) — index entries are too long. Only part of it was loaded.'
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: 'the leg-3 store is unbounded but its INDEX is budgeted.' Actual: the store is genuinely unbounded (177 rows, correct), and the budgeter exists and is tested — but nothing runs it, so the index is 2.1x its own stated budget and the consuming harness silently drops roughly half of it.
- **impact:** Directly violates 'memory survives interruption/restart without loss': at every single session start, more than half the index is discarded by the loader, and which half is lost is determined by the harness's truncation, not by rank or governance. The budget enforcement was built and shipped but never wired, so the defect it was written to fix is still live.

### [HIGH] GDPR erasure does not touch either Sora memory tier, and L2 episodic memory has no deletion path anywhere in the codebase

- **category:** data-integrity  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** backend/routers/privacy.py `_erase_user_data` issues 13 DELETE statements (lines 277-320): user_tenant_memberships, user_preferences, user_drafts, chat_messages, chat_sessions, sessions, user_mfa, mfa_backup_codes, password_history, api_keys, oauth_tokens, transcript_segments, meetings. Neither `l3_facts` nor `chat_session_summaries` appears. Repo-wide, `grep -rn 'chat_session_summaries' --include=*.py . | grep -v /tests/` finds no DELETE outside the Alembic downgrade's `DROP TABLE` (0272_chat_session_summaries.py:99). The table also carries a BEFORE UPDATE trigger making it write-once (backend/db.py:2749). L3 does have a real erase path (backend/agents/u6_l3_store.py:193 `erase_user`, wired at backend/routers/memories.py:318) driven by the Settings->Memory UI, but it is not reachable from the DSAR router.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected governance non-negotiable: a delete UI for Sora's tiers. Actual: L3 has a working propose/confirm/revoke/erase UI (app/settings/memory/page.tsx). L2 has none — no UI, no endpoint, no DSAR coverage, and an append-only trigger. A GDPR erasure deletes the raw chat_messages and chat_sessions while permanently retaining the LLM-derived summary of those same conversations.
- **impact:** A user who exercises erasure believes their conversations are gone; the derived episodic record (topics, outcome_kind, tasks_created, turn_count, plus per-message content hashes) survives indefinitely with no mechanism to remove it. The hashes specifically defeat the 30-day chat_messages prune that L2 was designed to outlive.

### [HIGH] Only one of the three principals has a live memory feed; leg-2 is entirely empty in production while its loop is reported code-complete

- **category:** mismatch-with-intent  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** Prod DB row counts (pg_stat_user_tables on omnisight-pg-primary/omnisight): learned_item_snapshots 0, learned_item_versions 0, learned_item_evidence 0, learned_item_citations 0, learned_item_utility 0. `omnisight_memory_enabled` = 0 on both backends; backend/learned_item_loader.py:106 sets it from `_promotion_enabled()`, gated on `OMNISIGHT_LEARNED_ITEM_PROMOTION_ENABLED` (:43), which is absent from the prod container env (grep count 0). `omnisight_memory_delivery_total` has no samples at all. Meanwhile chat_session_summaries = 1 row (created 2026-07-22 16:47:58Z) and l3_facts = 1 row (2026-07-22 16:13:24Z) — i.e. the activation-day 'firsts' and nothing since, 10 days.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: ONE governed memory serving three principals. Actual: leg-1 holds exactly one row per tier, leg-2 holds zero rows and is switched off in prod, leg-3 holds 177 rows but delivers them ungoverned. There is no shared substrate in operation — three separate stores at three different maturity levels, plus a kernel none of them invoke.
- **impact:** The 'three principals, one governed kernel' north star is a design document, not a running system. Progress reported as 'leg-1 ACTIVE / leg-2 CODE-COMPLETE + STAGING-GREEN' is accurate about code and staging but reads, at a glance, as production capability that does not exist.

### [MEDIUM] L1 working memory is hard-capped at 100MB with oldest-first eviction, contradicting 'grows without being limited by capacity'

- **category:** mismatch-with-intent  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** False
- **evidence:** Prod backend env: `OMNISIGHT_MEMORY_TOOL_CAP_MB=100`, `OMNISIGHT_MEMORY_TOOL_ROOT=/var/omnisight/memory`. backend/agents/memory_tool_handler.py:778 `cap_bytes = self.config.cap_mb * 1024 * 1024`; :780 raises `MemoryStorageFull` if a single write exceeds the cap; :798 'Oldest-first eviction' over rglob candidates, preserving a newest-3 floor; :788 emits a `MemoryCapExceeded` audit row and one `evict` row per victim.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: 'memory keeps growing without being limited by capacity.' Actual: the L1 tier is explicitly capacity-bounded and evicts oldest-first once full. This is a defensible engineering decision, but it is the direct negation of the stated expectation and is not reconciled anywhere.
- **impact:** Long-lived agents silently lose their oldest working-memory files at the 100MB boundary. To the system's credit this is the least silent failure found — eviction emits audit rows — but no metric or alert surfaces it, and the stated intent says the opposite should happen.

### [MEDIUM] The delete UI promises unrecoverable crypto-shred while the implementation's own comment concedes historical copies survive

- **category:** mismatch-with-intent  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** app/settings/memory/page.tsx:217 toast: '已全部刪除 ... 已 crypto-shred ${r.facts} 條記憶（不可復原）' ('crypto-shredded, unrecoverable'). backend/agents/u6_l3_store.py:193-201 `erase_user` docstring says 'destroy every dek_ref (the only key => the surviving ciphertext is unrecoverable)' but the inline NOTE(ops) at :198-201 states: 'the shred COMPLETES only once the dek_ref is gone from every copy (backups/WAL/PITR/replicas) within the retention RPO — a purge SLA the ops layer owns; txn commit alone does not scrub a WAL/dead-tuple pre-image.' The `UPDATE l3_facts SET dek_ref='{}'` executes in the same transaction as the DELETE.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: provenance and honest governance semantics. Actual: the code is honest with itself in a comment; the user-facing string is not. The pre-update tuple version containing the wrapped DEK persists in dead tuples, WAL, PITR and any replica until operational retention expires.
- **impact:** A user is told erasure is complete and irreversible at the moment of the click. It is complete only in the active table. Independently corroborated by the 2026-07-26 codex review (docs/audit/codex-reviews/memory-audit-remediation-codex-2026-07-26.txt, finding #1, verdict NOT-SOUND) — which the untracked working-tree state suggests has not yet been actioned.

### [LOW] The causal axis has produced no incidents for 12 days despite an actively running runner fleet

- **category:** mismatch-with-intent  ·  **INFERENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** `SELECT max(created_at), now()-max(created_at) FROM runner_incidents` -> 2026-07-20 05:40:06Z, age 12 days 09:50. Daily histogram shows June volume of 8-63/day, a full gap from 2026-06-08 to 2026-07-07, sparse July (1-14 on scattered days), then nothing after 07-20. The runner fleet is live: `pgrep -f auto-runner-jira.py` returns an active PID and six run-ephemeral.sh supervisors have been up since Jul 03.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected per ADR-0015 as amended: the causal axis is 'available only from the durable incident/failure-memory paths that are actually populated.' Actual: the population rate has fallen to zero again, matching the shape of the pre-Phase-R regression (the original audit's 'incidents stopped 6/9').
- **impact:** Cannot be called a defect from this evidence alone — runner_incidents records failures, so a healthy fleet legitimately produces none, and the ADR was honestly amended to say empty causal context means 'no usable recall', not 'no risk'. Flagging it because the gap shape mirrors the earlier silent regression and deserves a positive liveness check rather than an inference from absence.

---

## completeness-critic — what the nine-auditor sweep missed

**Auditor summary:** The sweep audited the project-state axes, the alerting layer, and the authorization kernel, but nobody owned the three memory *stores* end-to-end in prod (leg-1 L2/L3, leg-2 learned-items, leg-3 claude_memory_*) or the runner-side consumer boundary where leg-2 actually terminates — and that is where the biggest live hollows are: leg-2's injection flag is set in no environment, which makes `learned_item_citations` (and the utility/revocation arm derived from it) structurally unreachable in every environment including "staging-green"; leg-3's drift gauge has no setter anywhere in the codebase and reads 0 while the DB mirror has demonstrably diverged from the live file store. Worse, the leg-1 hollow-detector that the design doc says makes a dead scheduler "VISIBLE" is in fact working and showing a 10-day-old freeze right now — and no alert rule anywhere in the repo watches any `omnisight_u6_*`, `omnisight_claude_memory_*` or `omnisight_memory_*` metric, so the sweep's alerting dimension audited only 1 of the 3 legs' monitoring surface.

**Checks performed:**
- Enumerated live runner processes (ps -eo pid,lstart,etime,cmd) and read /proc/{4003771,3999407,3999414,3999421}/environ to get the ACTUAL fleet environment rather than the runner.env file — this is what proved OMNISIGHT_RUNNER_LEARNED_ITEMS absent at runtime
- Read scripts/runner-wrapper/run-ephemeral.sh in full; confirmed LOG=/tmp/runner-$INSTANCE.log (line 35) and that runner.env IS loaded via the heartbeat-path.conf drop-in EnvironmentFile on all four runner unit templates
- Grepped the full 128 MB /tmp/runner-claude-1.log and 188 MB /tmp/runner-codex-1.log for learned_items.result, project_state., and pickup markers; counted cycles (94,302) and no-candidate lines (93,866) since 2026-06-15
- Read auto-runner-jira.py:1078-1160 (_fetch_learned_items_block, _fetch_project_state) and 1238-1258 (_build_project_state_block)
- Read backend/routers/learned_items.py in full and backend/learned_item_loader.py::get_learned_items_block (lines 164-234)
- Read backend/learned_item_publisher.py::insert_publication_event (100-152) and traced all three call sites (400, 414, 484)
- Read backend/agents/memory_promotion_scheduler.py:540-610 (_ROLLUP_SQL + run_utility_rollup) to establish the citations→utility dependency
- Read backend/agents/u6_l2_writer.py::write_session_summary, backend/agents/u6_memory_scheduler.py header (design intent for hollow visibility), and u6_l3_reader.py validity-window filter (NULL valid_until passes — no bug there)
- Read backend/routers/claude_memories.py surface and confirmed backend/main.py:1522-1523 mounts it
- Exact row counts (count(*), not pg_stat estimates — pg_stat_user_tables reported runner_incidents=38 vs a true 1973, so estimates were discarded) on prod omnisight-pg-primary for 18 memory tables, and on staging omnisight-staging-postgres-1/omnisight_staging for 10
- Compared alembic_version in prod (0280) against the migration files in both the develop tree and /home/user/omnisight-prod — no drift
- Queried Prometheus (localhost:9090) for all omnisight_u6_*, omnisight_claude_memory_*, omnisight_memory_* series values and /api/v1/rules; established a positive control by confirming the same query pattern returns loaded project_state rules and non-empty u6 series
- Exhaustive repo greps for setters of claude_memory_drift_gauge, memory_quarantine_depth, memory_enabled, memory_liveness_heartbeat (positive control: the grep DID find memory_enabled's setter at learned_item_loader.py:106) and for any writer of memory_transition_events
- Verified the drift and quarantine gauge defects are present in the DEPLOYED image, not just develop, via docker exec grep against /app/backend/metrics.py and /app/backend/
- Diffed 181 on-disk memory slugs against 177 claude_memory_state slugs with comm (positive control: 177 matched both sides); compared body_sha256 and body length of an ingested slug against the on-disk file
- Enumerated systemd user units, timers, and drop-ins; read scripts/sync_omnisight_main.sh:196-209 and journalctl event histogram for omnisight-main-sync; confirmed the OP-2739 fix (ea781c92) targets only the sora-bridge sibling
- Counted commits between /home/user/work/sora/OmniSight-Productizer HEAD and the develop tip (1068) and diffed the runner wrapper between the two trees (identical)
- Read prod backend container env (docker inspect Config.Env) for all memory/U6/leg-2 flags
- Read the prod backup log and freshness probe to rule out a memory-durability finding — local pg_dump + DLP + restore-verify all pass daily and include the memory tables; the unit's daily 'failed' status is only the unset OMNISIGHT_BACKUP_S3_URI at the end of an otherwise successful run

**Could not check:**
- Whether leg-2 being dark in prod is a deliberate not-yet-activated state or an unnoticed regression — no flag-state ledger or activation record exists on this host that I could read, and the runbook (docs/operations/leg2-activation-runbook.md) states intent, not deployed state. I report the mechanical facts only.
- Whether flipping OMNISIGHT_RUNNER_LEARNED_ITEMS would actually deliver a block end-to-end — verifying that requires setting an env var and triggering a pickup, both mutations.
- Whether the leg-3 publish/ingest wave is intentionally paused (MEMORY.md describes it as a browser-driven operator task with a 30/h cap). I can prove the mirror is stale and the drift gauge cannot fire; I cannot prove anyone believes ingest is current.
- Whether the 137 quarantined leg-3 memories are correctly quarantined — I did not read quarantine reasons or the lint payloads, only the state counts.
- The gemini-1 and grok-1 runner logs (1.38 GB / 1.37 GB) were not grepped for injection markers; I sampled claude-1 and codex-1 only.
- Whether any Gerrit submit-rule or CI job would catch the missing published_at or the writerless memory_transition_events — I audited code and DB, not CI configuration.
- I did not execute any test suite (no backend venv on this host), so all statements about test coverage come from reading assertions and from which symbols tests reference.
- Whether the staging backend's own metrics/alerting differ from prod — only one Prometheus exists on this host and the staging container had no loopback HTTP path reachable read-only.

### [HIGH] Leg-2's terminal step is off in every environment: the injection flag is set nowhere, so learned_item_citations — its only writer — is structurally unreachable

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** Live process env of all six runners: `tr '\0' '\n' < /proc/4003771/environ | grep OMNISIGHT_` (claude-1) and the same for 3999407/3999414/3999421 → 15 OMNISIGHT_* vars, `OMNISIGHT_RUNNER_LEARNED_ITEMS` absent from all. It is also absent from /home/user/.config/omnisight/runner.env, which IS the loaded EnvironmentFile (/home/user/.config/systemd/user/runner-claude@.service.d/heartbeat-path.conf:2). auto-runner-jira.py:1083-1085 returns "" when the flag is off. `grep -a -c 'learned_items.result' /tmp/runner-claude-1.log` → 0, over 94,302 cycles since 2026-06-15 (128 MB log); same for codex-1. Only writer of the table: backend/routers/learned_items.py:58 `INSERT INTO learned_item_citations`, reachable only when `result == "non_empty" and served and ticket` (line 48) — and the runner is the only caller that passes `ticket=`. Exact counts: prod `select count(*) from learned_item_citations` → 0; staging (omnisight-staging-postgres-1 / omnisight_staging) → 0, despite staging having learned_item_versions=9, memory_publications=8, memory_approvals=3, memory_eval_runs=14, curator_merge_candidates=5 (proving the query harness returns non-zero on this DB).
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: the leg-2 loop is 'code-complete + staging-green' through injection and citations. Actual: the loop is proven only through publication; the injection→citation→utility half has produced zero rows in any environment and cannot, because the single gate that enables it is set in no env file, no container env, and no live process.
- **impact:** The entire downstream feedback arm is dead: learned_item_utility=0 in prod and staging, and memory_promotion_scheduler.py:543-560 computes utility exclusively from learned_item_citations, so the utility rollup and its revocation-proposal logic run forever on an empty input and can never demote a bad card. Human-approved published playbooks reach no agent. No dimension owned this boundary — axis-liveness scoped itself to project-state, anti-hollow to alerts, kernel-invariant listed it explicitly as an unchecked gap.

### [HIGH] omnisight_claude_memory_drift has no setter anywhere in the codebase — leg-3's drift detector is pinned at 0 while the DB mirror has provably diverged from the live file store

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** Exhaustive repo grep (`grep -rn claude_memory_drift . --include=*.py --include=*.yml --include=*.yaml --include=*.json --include=*.md`) returns exactly 3 hits, all in backend/metrics.py: the Gauge definition at :1245-1246 and the `_NoOp()` alias at :1407. No `.set()`, no test, no alert rule. Confirmed live in the deployed image: `docker exec omnisight-productizer-backend-a-1 grep -rn 'claude_memory_drift_gauge\.set' /app/backend/` → no output, while the definition is present at /app/backend/metrics.py:1246. Prometheus: `omnisight_claude_memory_drift` = 0 on both backend replicas. Real drift, proven three ways: (a) 4 of 181 on-disk memory files were never ingested — comm of disk slugs vs `select slug from claude_memory_state` gives MEMORY, project_case5_epicB_twoway, project_failed_units_remediation_2026_07_25, project_s12g_A_v2_runtime_defense (positive control: 177 slugs match on both sides); (b) `select max(updated_at) from claude_memory_state` = 2026-07-23 15:08:42+00 while 9 memory files have mtimes after that, newest 2026-07-29 10:24; (c) content hash mismatch on an ingested slug — DB head for project_u6_kernel_handoff_and_roadmap has body_sha256 aabcb57191ad4f3e… / length 263,535, ingested 2026-07-22 17:06, while the on-disk body is 265,802 bytes with sha ab5760c22b4d7c6a….
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: a drift gauge that goes non-zero when the file store and the claude_memory_* mirror diverge (MEMORY.md records 'γ-2 regen + drift-exclusion + reconcile PROVEN'). Actual: the gauge cannot move — no code path writes it — and it reports 0 across 10 days of accumulated, measurable divergence.
- **impact:** Leg-3's mirror is silently 10 days stale and missing 4 memories, with the designed detector reporting perfect health. Any future decision to cut over Claude's memory serving from the file store to the tables would be made on a mirror nobody can tell is wrong. Nothing in the sweep covered leg-3 store integrity.

### [HIGH] No alert rule anywhere in the repo covers any leg-1 or leg-3 memory metric — while the leg-1 hollow-detector is currently showing a 10-day freeze

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** Prometheus /api/v1/rules loads exactly 4 rules, all in group `omnisight_project_state_health` (ProjectStateStructuralHollow, ProjectStateHalfHollow, ProjectStateNoTraffic=firing, ProjectStateLatencyHigh). Repo-wide `grep -rln 'omnisight_u6_|omnisight_claude_memory_|omnisight_memory_' --include=*.yml --include=*.yaml .` → zero files; positive control `grep -rln omnisight_project_state --include=*.yml .` → deploy/observability/prometheus/project_state_health.yml. Live gauge values (localhost:9090): omnisight_u6_l2_summaries_count = 1 (frozen); omnisight_u6_l2_last_summary_age_seconds = 859,404 s = 9.95 days and rising; omnisight_u6_l3_fact_count{state="promoted"} = 1, all other states 0; omnisight_u6_memory_scheduler_ticks_total{outcome="ok"} = 2885 and 445 (the scheduler is alive and finding nothing); omnisight_memory_liveness_heartbeat = 0; omnisight_memory_enabled = 0. Prod flags are ON: docker inspect backend-a Config.Env shows OMNISIGHT_U6_L2_WRITE=1, OMNISIGHT_SORA_MEMORY_SCHEDULER=1, OMNISIGHT_SORA_L3_READ=1, OMNISIGHT_U6_METRICS_ENABLED=1. Prod stores: chat_session_summaries = 1 row (created 2026-07-22 16:47), l3_facts = 1 row (2026-07-22 16:13) — two rows, ever.
- **expected vs actual:** backend/agents/u6_memory_scheduler.py:60-66 states the design intent explicitly: the DB-derived gauges are 'external to this producer, so a flag-ON-but-hollow scheduler is VISIBLE (frozen count + growing age), the 3D-memory lesson.' Actual: the signature is present exactly as predicted — count frozen at 1, age 9.95 days — and no alert rule, no gate, and no dashboard consumes it.
- **impact:** Leg-1 has been enabled-but-producing-nothing for ten days in prod with full instrumentation and zero escalation. The anti-hollow dimension audited the project-state alert family and concluded alerting was the weak point; it did not notice that two of the three legs have no alert rules at all, so their monitoring gap was never counted.

### [MEDIUM] auto-runner-jira.py's flag-off path returns silently, directly contradicting the anti-hollow docstring three lines above it

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** auto-runner-jira.py:1078-1086 — docstring: "Empty string on ANY failure or when OMNISIGHT_RUNNER_LEARNED_ITEMS is off; result label logged on EVERY pickup (audit #6: hollow serving must be visible from runner logs)". The very next statement is `if os.environ.get("OMNISIGHT_RUNNER_LEARNED_ITEMS", "").strip().lower() not in ("1","true","yes","on"): return ""` — no `print`. The `print(f"[runner] learned_items.result={result} ...")` sits at line 1101, after the network call.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected per the ticket's own AC: a result label on every pickup so hollow serving is visible from runner logs. Actual: the most common state (flag off) is the one state that emits nothing at all.
- **impact:** An operator grepping runner logs for `learned_items.result` sees zero lines and cannot distinguish 'feature disabled' from 'runner code never reached this path'. This is the same degrade-silent shape the 2026-07-07 audit named, reintroduced by the fix that cites that audit.

### [MEDIUM] memory_transition_events, the leg-2 lifecycle ledger, has zero production writers — the exact 'writer never existed' defect the 2026-07-07 audit named, recurring inside the repair

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** Table created by backend/alembic/versions/0258_u4_learned_item_ledger.py:158 and present in both prod and staging. `grep -rn memory_transition_events --include=*.py . | grep -v alembic/versions | grep -v claude_memory` returns only backend/tests/* (test_u4_ledger_migration.py:306/319/576, test_u4_memory_eval_stats.py:340, test_u4_learned_item_publication.py:223, test_u4_learned_item_provenance.py:382). Exact counts: prod 0, staging 0 — even though staging performed 8 memory_publications and 3 memory_approvals.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: an append-only transition ledger recording every learned-item lifecycle state change. Actual: the table is created, migrated to prod, exercised only by tests, and written by no production code path — so the lifecycle transitions that did happen on staging left no ledger entry.
- **impact:** Leg-2 has no tamper-evident record of who moved a memory between lifecycle states and when. Because only tests write it, the test suite is green on a table production never touches — the same 'tests prove files contain strings' shape the anti-hollow auditor flagged for the Phase S harness, here in the data layer.

### [MEDIUM] Every publication-ledger event is written with published_at = NULL, and the ledger's immutability triggers make it uncorrectable

- **category:** data-integrity  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** backend/learned_item_publisher.py:100-152 — `insert_publication_event(..., published_at: Any = None, ...)`. All three call sites (:400, :414, :484) omit the argument. The only `published_at=` assignments in non-test backend code are backend/secops_intel.py:199/275, an unrelated CVE module. Empirically on staging: `select * from memory_publications limit 3` returns rows with state approved/publishing/published and published_at blank for all; `select max(published_at) from memory_publications` over all 8 rows → NULL. The table carries `trg_memory_publications_no_update` and `trg_memory_publications_no_delete` executing learned_item_ledger_block_mutation().
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: the row whose state='published' stamps the moment the card became injectable. Actual: NULL on every event including 'published', and the append-only triggers prevent any backfill.
- **impact:** The leg-2 audit trail cannot answer 'when did this memory become live', so no retention, staleness, or incident-correlation query over published memories can work. Because the ledger is deliberately immutable, this is not repairable in place — it needs a compensating event type. No auditor examined the leg-2 ledger's column-level integrity.

### [MEDIUM] omnisight_memory_quarantine_depth has no production setter and reads 0 while 137 leg-3 memories sit quarantined

- **category:** hollow  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** `grep -rn memory_quarantine_depth . --include=*.py` → backend/metrics.py:999-1000 (Gauge), :1379 (_NoOp), :2147/:2164-2165 (re-registration), and backend/tests/test_u4_snapshot_publisher.py:679 `m.memory_quarantine_depth.set(0)` — the only `.set()` in the repo is in a test. Prometheus: omnisight_memory_quarantine_depth = 0 on both replicas. Prod DB: `select state, count(*) from claude_memory_state group by 1` → quarantined 137, published 40.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: a gauge that surfaces a growing quarantine backlog. Actual: the gauge is never written by production code, so it reports 0 permanently; meanwhile the only quarantine that actually has depth (leg-3, 137 of 177 memories held) has no gauge of its own at all.
- **impact:** 77% of the leg-3 corpus is held in quarantine with no metric, no alert, and a depth gauge that will read 0 forever. INFERENCE: the gauge was likely scoped to leg-2's quarantine (genuinely 0 today), but that reading does not help — it means the backlog that exists is unmonitored while an always-green gauge sits in the dashboard namespace.

### [MEDIUM] omnisight-main-sync reports SUCCESS every 5 minutes while the tree the runner systemd units execute from is 1,068 commits behind develop — and it resets its own failure counter on the skip path

- **category:** bug  ·  **EVIDENCE**  ·  **silent:** True
- **evidence:** `git -C /home/user/work/sora/OmniSight-Productizer rev-list --count HEAD..<develop tip 30217302>` → 1068. `systemctl --user status omnisight-main-sync.service` → Process exited status=0/SUCCESS, journal line `{"event":"sync_skipped_dirty","path":"/home/user/work/sora/OmniSight-Productizer","tree_dirty":"1","untracked":"1"}`. `journalctl --user -u omnisight-main-sync.service --since 2026-07-01 | grep -o '"event":"[a-z_]*"' | sort | uniq -c` → 243 sync_skipped_dirty and nothing else — zero successful syncs in the retained journal. scripts/sync_omnisight_main.sh:202-208: the dirty check runs before the fetch, calls `_reset_failure`, then `exit 0`. No OnFailure drop-in exists (/home/user/.config/systemd/user/omnisight-main-sync.service.d/ does not exist). The identical code is still present at the same lines in the develop tree, i.e. OP-2739 (commit ea781c92, 'sora-bridge-sync stops reporting success while structurally unable to sync', which describes this exact defect: 'the dirty check ran BEFORE the fetch and exited 0 unconditionally') was applied only to the sora-bridge sibling.
- **expected vs actual:** Expected: the tree backing the runner units tracks gerrit/develop, or the unit goes red. Actual: it is 1,068 commits stale, the unit is green on every run, and the skip path actively clears the consecutive-failure counter that drives escalation.
- **impact:** The premise 'merged-to-develop == live for runner code' holds only for the Python that the ephemeral clone fetches. The wrapper layer — runner-{claude,codex,gemini,grok}@.service ExecStart and WorkingDirectory both point at /home/user/work/sora/OmniSight-Productizer — is served from this frozen tree.
